# [7] Finding: The meaning unit — what a communication conveys

**Source:** `/Users/nsstorm/Desktop/projects/AIOpsMan/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md`

---
status: active
model: claude-opus-5-5
effort: unknown
refines: devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-59__three-fundamental-meaning-gaps-of-communication-craft/finding.md
---
# Finding: The meaning unit — what a communication conveys

## Changes from Prior

**Prior path:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-59__three-fundamental-meaning-gaps-of-communication-craft/finding.md

**Revision trigger:** The user asked for a deep dive on Gap 2 (the meaning), "if it is still relevant".

**What's preserved:**
- The meaning stays one of the three fundamental gaps.
- A unit still carries companions, a status and a basis.
- Relations between units still matter.
- The question-and-answer list of units remains a hypothesis, not a definition.

**What's changed:**
- **Relevance:** the gap is smaller. The circularity is solved by the need work.
- **The unit:** it is now *content with force*, not just "an answer, act or stance".
- **Reasons and limits are split:** limits sit inside the unit; reasons are linked units.
- **Status and origin** sit together under one heading, "standing". Every status value needs a quotable entry condition.
- **"Held"** now means "quotable at check time", not "always written".

**What's new:**
- the removal test;
- disclaimed non-claims;
- separate extraction with author confirmation;
- the hypothesis that explicitness gaps are mostly missing limits or referents.

**Migration:** none.

## Question

Is the meaning gap still relevant after the need findings? If so, what is one unit of meaning, and what must travel with it so a communication's meaning can be quoted and checked?

The user named four things that might travel with a unit:
- its reasons and limits;
- its status (settled by the author, taken from a source, left open, proposed by an AI);
- where it came from;
- how it connects to other units.

The answer had to stay generic and not over-define.

## Finding Summary

### Is it still relevant?

**Yes, but smaller.**
- **The circularity is solved.** The need finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_16-18__the-need-what-a-communication-must-achieve-and-for-whom/finding.md) answers "important according to what?": a piece of meaning matters when it serves a requirement of some holder.
- **What remains:** what that piece *is*, what travels with it, how pieces connect, and whether it must be written.
- **Why units are still needed:** checking text against requirements catches missing answers. It cannot catch:
  - an answer that is false to its source;
  - an AI decision nobody confirmed;
  - a limit dropped in a summary;
  - two split messages that contradict each other.

  All four need a unit of meaning to compare.

### What one unit is

**One unit of meaning is a point, plus what is being done with it, plus the limits that keep it true.**

| Example | What is done with the point | Limit |
|---|---|---|
| "The migration is done for the EU accounts." | asserted | for the EU accounts |
| "Use B if budget allows." | recommended | if budget allows |
| "Thank you for the release weekend." | thanked | — |
| "Who owns X after March?" | left open | — |
| "We have not tested Y." | disclaimed | — |

- **Force matters.** "What is being done with it" lets thanks, recommendations and open questions count as meaning, not only claims. This matches the need's questions and conditions.
- **Granularity.** If a reader could accept one part and reject another, they are two units.

### Limits and reasons are different things

This is the most useful distinction found.
- **Drop a limit and the point becomes false**, or becomes a different commitment ("done" vs "done for the EU accounts"). That is always a fidelity error.
- **Drop a reason and the point is still true, but unsupported.** How much that matters depends on two things:
  - whether a holder needs the "why";
  - whether you are transforming a source. Then a material dropped reason is a preservation loss, as the materialization-vs-content finding already says.

### Connections between units

A few connection types carry most of the structure. Losing them changes meaning:
- support (because / therefore);
- condition (if / unless);
- contrast (but / although);
- sequence (then).

The list is open, not fixed.

**One rule for "if / unless":** it belongs inside the unit when dropping it would make the unit false or a different commitment.

### Standing: status and origin

Every unit has a standing with two parts:
- **who or what settles it** (status);
- **where it can be quoted from** (origin).

**Each status needs a quotable entry condition:**

| Status | Entry condition |
|---|---|
| "settled" | an author quote or an author answer |
| "gathered" | a source quote |
| "proposed" or "found in draft" | everything else |

**An AI can never mark its own units as settled.**

### Held

Meaning does not have to be written in advance. It must be **quotable, with its standing, when a check needs it**. Two routes:
- **write it first** (a short brief is fine), which is better for high stakes or when the author will be unavailable later;
- **have a separate agent extract the units from the draft**, then have the author confirm them.

### One concept for every kind of "meaning"

Intended, stated, received and source meaning are all units. They differ only in standing.

### The removal test

**The removal test says what any loss does:**

| Remove… | Effect |
|---|---|
| a unit | a requirement goes unanswered |
| a limit | the meaning changes |
| a disclaimer | the claim is inflated |
| a reason | support is lost |
| a connection | the reader may infer the wrong link |
| the standing | the unit can't be verified |
| the place in the text | the unit is not conveyed |

Which check handles the loss then depends on the need and on whether this is a transformation.

### A bridge to explicitness (hypothesis)

The explicitness bank's demanded fillers ("which one", "of what", "when", "by what criterion") look like questions about a unit's limits and referents. If so, an explicitness gap is usually a unit whose limit or referent the text doesn't carry. This is untested.

**Confidence limit:** no real text has been through unit extraction yet. That is the first next step.

## Finding

### 1. Is this still worth doing?

The three-gaps finding named the meaning as Gap 2 because "essence" was defined as "the important meaning for the present use". That definition was circular, and it was allowed to stay unwritten. Meanwhile the content check needs meaning it can quote.

The need finding fixed half of this. "Important" now has a criterion: it serves a requirement (a question, a condition or a constraint) of some holder's state.

The other half remains. Requirements say what must be answered, not what the answer *is* or *where it stands*.

Four losses are invisible without a unit of meaning:
- **an answer false to its source:** you need the unit's origin to compare;
- **an AI decision nobody confirmed:** you need its status;
- **a qualification dropped in a summary:** you need the unit's internal limit;
- **contradiction between split messages:** the several-parties finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_16-28__several-parties-holders-layering-and-constraints/finding.md) requires their facts to match, and comparing facts needs units.

So the gap is still relevant, and smaller.

### 2. The unit: content with force

A unit of meaning has three parts:
- **content:** the point (what it is about and what it says);
- **force:** what is being done with the point;
- **internal limits:** the scope, condition or qualifier that keeps it true.

**Why force is needed.**
- A claims-only unit cannot hold a thank-you, an open question or a recommendation. "Use B" is neither true nor false.
- The need finding showed that expressive needs have *conditions*, such as "a thank-you must name what was done". Something has to meet those conditions, and that is a unit whose force is thanking.
- Wording can change force: "might" vs "will", quoting vs endorsing. Scientific hedges ("may", "suggests") are force too. Summaries routinely drop them, which inflates claims.

**The forces seen so far:**
- asserted;
- recommended;
- asked;
- promised;
- thanked;
- left open;
- disclaimed ("we have not tested Y").

These are examples, not a fixed list.

**Granularity.** The test is whether a reader could accept one part and reject another. If so, they are separate units.

**Units are not sentences.**
- One sentence can carry several units.
- One unit can span several sentences.
- A unit can be meant without being stated. Whether that is acceptable belongs to the conveyance gap.

### 3. Limits inside, reasons linked

The user grouped "reasons and limits" together. They behave differently when removed.

**Limits.** "The migration is done for the EU accounts."
- Remove "for the EU accounts" and the statement becomes false.
- "Friday works, provided Maya approves."
- Remove the condition and it becomes a different commitment.
- So limits are *part of the unit*. Losing one is always a fidelity error.

**Reasons.** "The migration is done, because the load test passed."
- Remove the reason and the claim is still true, just unsupported.
- Reasons are often important claims themselves, with their own standing.
- So reasons are *separate units linked by a relation*.

**How serious a dropped reason is depends on the situation.**
- **In fresh writing**, an omitted reason is a fit question: does any holder need the "why"?
- **When transforming a source** (summarising, replying), dropping a material reason is a preservation loss. The materialization-vs-content finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_13-08__materialization-check-vs-content-check-in-communication-craft/finding.md) already defines faithfulness as *"nothing material from the sources dropped (a reason, a qualification)"*.
- "Material" is judged by whether any holder's requirement asks "why".

"Companions" (from the essence-formation finding) remains a fine umbrella word for both, as long as the difference is kept.

### 4. Connections

The substrate finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_22-09__communication-substrate-foundation-and-dimensions/finding.md) says *"the relationships between these facets carry much of the meaning."*

A small core of connection types carries what changes meaning when dropped:
- **support:** because / therefore;
- **condition:** if / unless, when it links two units;
- **contrast or concession:** but / although;
- **sequence:** then.

Rhetorical Structure Theory, a standard catalogue of how parts of a text relate, lists over 20 relations. Most others, such as elaboration and example, rarely change meaning when dropped. Add them when a real case needs them.

**Connections are lopsided.** One unit is the point; the other supports it. The *main point* of a communication is the unit that answers the primary holder's main requirement. It can be worked out from the need and does not have to be declared.

**Losing a connection while keeping both units is its own problem.** "A. B." instead of "A because B" invites the reader to guess the link, or to guess the wrong one.

### 5. Standing: who settles it, and where it can be quoted from

Every unit has two pieces of standing:
- **Status: who or what settles it.**
  - settled by the author;
  - gathered from a source;
  - produced, i.e. worked out in this work;
  - proposed by an AI;
  - found in a draft;
  - left open;
  - to be asked;
  - attributed (a reader's interpretation);
  - superseded.
- **Origin: where it can be quoted from.** A source document, the author's own text, or an answer the author gave.

**No status may be assigned without a matching quote.**
- **"Settled"** requires an author quote or an author answer.
- **"Gathered"** requires a source quote.
- **Everything else** stays "proposed" or "found in draft".

This keeps the rule that a model never certifies itself. An AI can never mark its own units as settled.

**Status matches practice outside this project.**
- Architecture decision records use proposed, accepted and superseded.
- Nanopublications attach provenance to every assertion.
- The materialization-vs-content quote test is, in effect, a check on standing.

**One concept serves every kind of "meaning" in the project.** Intended, stated, received and source meaning are all units. They differ only in standing: settled or proposed, found in draft, attributed, gathered.

### 6. Held: quotable when needed

The substrate finding allows meaning to stay unwritten. The materialization-vs-content check needs it quotable. Both hold, at different times: **meaning must be quotable, with its standing, when a check needs it.**

**Route 1: write first.** A short brief or a list of units, before drafting.
- Prefer this when stakes are high, or when the author won't be available later.
- A one-paragraph brief is fine. Units are extracted from it when a check needs them.

**Route 2: extract, then confirm.**
- A *separate* agent (not the drafter) extracts units from the draft.
- Each unit is marked "found in draft" or "proposed", unless a source quote makes it "gathered".
- The author confirms the units, which makes them "settled".
- Confirming units is easier and more honest than confirming prose.
- This also produces the list of "what the AI decided" without asking the AI to report on itself.

**For transformations** (summaries, replies, translations), only the units at risk need holding: those serving requirements, plus their limits.

### 7. The removal test

Each part of the meaning is defined by what its loss does:

| Remove… | What happens |
|---|---|
| a unit serving a requirement | the requirement goes unanswered |
| an internal limit | the unit becomes false or a different commitment |
| a disclaimer | the reader assumes more than was claimed |
| a reason | the point is unsupported |
| a connection (both units kept) | the reader may infer the wrong link |
| the standing | the unit can't be verified; the content check can't route |
| the unit's place in the text | it is not conveyed |

This tells you **what changed**, which is the first diagnostic step. The second step, which check handles it, depends on the need and on whether this is a transformation. Section 3 shows the dropped-reason case. That is why this is a test, not a fixed mapping.

### 8. A bridge to explicitness (hypothesis)

The explicitness bank's demanded fillers ask things like:
- which one;
- of what;
- who;
- when;
- by what criterion.

These look like questions about a unit's **referent** (what the content is about) or its **internal limits** (scope, time, criterion). If so, an explicitness gap is usually a unit whose referent or limit the text doesn't carry. That would connect Gap 2 (meaning) and Gap 3 (conveyance) concretely.

**This is untested.** The test is to classify the real gaps in benchmarks 2 and 3 as missing limit, missing referent, or other.

## Inherited Commitments Re-test

**The essence is "the important meaning for the present use", and it need not be a separate file.**
- **Source:** 22-09, section 1.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, frame revised.
- **Evidence:**
  - "Important" is now defined via requirements (16-18).
  - "Need not be separate" holds before drafting.
  - At check time, the meaning must be quotable with standing.

**Relations carry meaning; per-assertion qualifiers (whose view, rests on, support, agreement, openness).**
- **Source:** 22-09, sections 3 and 6.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
- **Evidence:** the qualifiers map onto standing and internal limits; the relation core keeps the structure.

**Faithful means "nothing material from the sources dropped (a reason, a qualification)"; the quote test.**
- **Source:** 13-08, summary.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, frame revised.
- **Evidence:**
  - The critique found that a dropped reason in a transformation is fidelity, which conflicted with the first sensemaking version.
  - Resolution: limits always change meaning; reasons are fidelity in transformations when material.
  - The quote test becomes the standing entry conditions.

**Moves (settled, gathered, selected, produced, open, asked), companions, backward formation, and that a list of silent decisions is derived, never self-reported.**
- **Source:** 15-47, summary.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, frame revised.
- **Evidence:**
  - The moves become status values.
  - Companions split into limits and reasons.
  - Backward formation becomes separate extraction plus author confirmation.

**Meaning units answer questions, meet conditions, and respect constraints.**
- **Source:** 16-18, Next Actions (COULD).
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
- **Evidence:** force is what lets a unit meet a condition (thanking, owning).

**Gap 2's hard core (a unit with attachments and relations); the list hypothesis refined to units plus relations.**
- **Source:** the three-gaps finding, sections 5 and 8.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
- **Evidence:** all parts are developed here. The list remains a hypothesis, not a schema.

## Next Actions

### MUST

**Extract units from 3 real drafts, one of them AI-written.**
- **How:** use a separate agent. Record each unit's content, force and limits. Mark its status by the quote rule. Then have the author confirm.
- **Who:** a test inquiry. The user picks the drafts.
- **Gate:** before any meaning reference or skill is written.
- **Why:** both ways of holding meaning, and the "what the AI decided" list, are untested.

### COULD

**Classify the real gaps from explicitness benchmarks 2 and 3 as missing limit, missing referent, or other.**
- **Who:** a test inquiry.
- **Gate:** any time.
- **Why:** it would confirm the bridge between meaning and conveyance, and could guide the conveyance gap.
- **Depends-on:** MUST "unit extraction". OVERRIDE: this is adoption-ready even though the MUST is open. Reason: it uses existing gap lists and the unit definition only, not the extraction results.

**Compare real summaries with their sources, and mark each loss as a limit or a reason.**
- **Who:** a test inquiry.
- **Gate:** after the MUST.
- **Why:** checks that dropped limits and dropped reasons really route differently.
- **Depends-on:** MUST "unit extraction". This COULD is GATED.

### DEFERRED

**Humans sign off on lists of units instead of prose.**
- **Gate:** when a sign-off step in AI drafting is designed.
- **Why (if revived):** approval becomes more honest and faster.

## Reasoning

**Killed: claims-only units.** They cannot hold thanks, recommendations or open items, and those are needed to meet the need's conditions.

**Killed: a brief instead of units.** A one-paragraph brief can't show which limit a summary dropped, or which part an AI decided. It survives as a way to write meaning down first.

**Killed: an AI assigning "settled" itself.** That is vouching. Every status needs a quote.

**Refined: "dropping a reason is a fit question, not fidelity."** The materialization-vs-content finding's own definition of faithful lists a dropped reason. Now: limits always change meaning; reasons are fidelity losses in transformations when material, and fit questions in fresh writing.

**Refined: the removal test.** It was first a one-to-one mapping to problem families. It is now "what the loss does", followed by context-dependent routing.

**Refined: explicitness gaps as missing limits or referents.** "Mostly" was unmeasured, so it is now a hypothesis with a test.

**Survived:**
- content with force;
- granularity by independent acceptability;
- the open relation core, plus the rule for when a condition sits inside a unit;
- standing with quotable entry conditions;
- quotable-at-check-time with two routes;
- disclaimed non-claims as units;
- receiver-attributed units;
- holding only the preservation set for transformations.

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

In the MUST test, record any piece of meaning that isn't content, force, limit, relation or standing. Two of the same kind would reopen the unit.

### Research Frontiers

- **Holistic residue** (overall tone, impression). Probably a conveyance or register question.
- **Implicature.** How does a unit meant but not stated get checked?
- **Long documents.** How does extraction scale? Probably by section, as with the detector.

### Refinement Triggers

- **Reopen "limits inside, reasons linked"** if, in real summaries, two or more losses cannot be classified as one or the other.
- **Reopen "the AI never self-settles"** only if the author has delegated settling authority to the AI in writing for that domain.

- Core to define first: what one unit of meaning is, and what must travel with it:
  - its reasons and limits;
  - its status: settled by the author, taken from a source, left open, or proposed by an AI but not confirmed;
  - where it came from;
  - how it connects to other units ("therefore", "unless").

# Finding: Conveyance — what a text may leave unsaid

## Changes from Prior

**Prior path:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-59__three-fundamental-meaning-gaps-of-communication-craft/finding.md

**Revision trigger:** The user asked for a deep dive into Gap 3 (conveyance). Its hard core is a principle for what a text may legitimately leave unsaid.

**What's preserved:**
- Conveyance is judged from the text and its declared setting, never from what a particular reader happens to know.
- What counts as "supplied" is a closed list.

**What's changed:** The vague hard core ("a principle") is now three named grounds and one test that can be demonstrated.

**What's new:**
- a mapping of all 23 of the explicitness bank's omission rules onto the three grounds;
- an explanation of why the gates spread;
- the difference test;
- stakes and repairability as the grain;
- an author-question form for the fix skill;
- the same principle applied to economy.

**Migration:** none yet. A bank v6.8 built on the principle is the first next action.

## Question

What may a text legitimately leave unsaid? The aim was a principle, not another rule.

The explicitness bank (the project's question bank for finding gaps in a text) keeps adding exemption rules. Its rules for "a particular thing isn't needed here" keep spreading, because no principle sits behind them. The user asked for a deep dive into conveyance generally, centred on this principle. The answer must be generic, must judge the text rather than reader knowledge, and must not be over-defined.

## Finding Summary

- **A text may leave something unsaid on only three grounds.**
  1. **Supplied:** the text, its declared setting (for example "an internal research finding for project members"), or the standard sense of its words fixes exactly one filler. The filler must arrive at, before, or right after the point that needs it.
  2. **Any reasonable choice gives the same result** for what the text asks the reader to do, know or judge, or for the reader's declared need. I call this "indifferent".
  3. **Declared open, or handed to someone:** for example "to be decided", or "each owner chooses".

  Otherwise it is a gap. The gap type says which ground failed:
  - **not stated at all:** no ground holds;
  - **stated too late:** Ground 1 failed on timing;
  - **several fillers fit:** Ground 1 failed, and the fillers give different results.
- **All 23 of the bank's omission rules turn out to be instances of these three grounds.**
  - The substitution test, the task values, "one end state", "the point is about the whole", "a second reading must change the point" and "no relation cue" are all Ground 2.
  - The supply rules and the next-sentence window are Ground 1.
  - "To be decided", declared level and declared discretion are Ground 3.
- **Why the gates spread.**
  - The Ground 2 rules were written as surface stand-ins: "one end state", "the point is about the whole", "only gives a steer". Each contains a word of extent.
  - A detector can claim the stand-in applies without ever checking that the result really is the same.
  - All four recorded failures are of this kind, and fixing them by tightening the wording just adds more stand-ins.
- **The fix is one test you can demonstrate.**
  - Name the reasonable fillers, grouped by the result each gives.
  - One group means it is indifferent: fine to leave unsaid.
  - Two groups mean a gap.
  - Example: "complete the Airtable records". Filling empty fields and checking every field against the source are different work with different finished states. So it is a gap, though v6.7 exempted it as "one end state".
- **The evidence already sits in the bank.** One of the bank's rules (V6.7-A5) is written as a demonstration of difference: "two readings … that lead to different conclusions, counts, owners or actions". It was not among the gates that wrongly exempted real gaps. The stand-in gates were.
- **The test has to be bounded and concrete, or it fails the same way.**
  - Fillers come only from what the text puts in play, the word's standard senses, and the named parties. One representative per group is enough.
  - Each group's result must be stated as an observable difference: who does what, what exists, what someone knows. "Both leave the records complete" names no difference and doesn't count.
- **How fine "the same result" must be depends on stakes and on whether the reader can ask back.**
  - A contract or a prompt to an AI agent needs fine grain; live chat tolerates coarse grain.
  - Both are read from the declared setting.
- **The principle reaches beyond detection.**
  - **Writing:** omit only if any reasonable choice would do.
  - **Fixing:** ask the author "A (→ X) or B (→ Y)?", not "which records?".
  - **Economy:** stated content that is indifferent *may* be cut.
  - **Meaning units:** every part of a unit (referent, limit, force, relation) gets the same test.
- **What it doesn't cover:** a text conveying something unintended. That is a separate conveyance question.
- **Confidence limit.** The principle explains every rule and every recorded failure, but it has not been run. Whether it really breaks the precision/recall seesaw needs a v6.8 experiment.

## Finding

### 1. Why this was the hard part

The explicitness bank has two tests:
- **demand:** is something particular needed here?
- **sufficiency:** does the text single it out?

Sufficiency is now well defined. Demand is where the bank keeps changing. Every version adds rules for when nothing particular is needed, and the results seesaw:

| Version | Precision | Recall |
|---|---|---|
| v6.6 | 56–62% real | highest |
| v6.7 | 81–83% real | fell, because its new gates exempted about 48 real gaps against the 6–8 predicted |

The planned v6.8 fix was to tighten the wording of each gate. The user's diagnosis: the rules keep spreading because no principle sits behind them.

### 2. The three grounds

**Ground 1: supplied.**
- The text fixes exactly one filler, early enough: at the point, before it, or in the very next sentence.
- It may do so through its own words, its declared setting, or the standard sense of a word.
- Expert knowledge counts only when the setting names the field. For example, a term used in the standard sense of a field the setting names counts as ordinary vocabulary.
- Standard inference counts as supply when it uses only the text, the setting and standard senses. Inference that needs private knowledge does not count.

**Ground 2: any reasonable choice gives the same result.**
- Every reasonable filler leads to the same result for what the text sets the reader to do, know or judge.
- When a need is declared, the result is measured against that need (see devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_16-18__the-need-what-a-communication-must-achieve-and-for-whom/finding.md).
- This is the old idea that information matters only if it could change the decision (the value of information). It is also Grice's maxim "as informative as required", made into a test.

**Ground 3: declared open, or handed to someone.**
- The text says it is open ("to be decided", "found by judging the first 5"), or hands the choice to someone ("each owner chooses").
- Silent discretion doesn't count. Readers often don't know they were given the choice.

**Anything else is a gap.**

| Gap type | Ground that failed |
|---|---|
| CANNOT (not there) | no ground holds |
| LATER | Ground 1 failed on timing |
| MULTIPLE | Ground 1 failed, and the fillers' results differ |
| CONFLICT and UNSPECIFIC | Ground 1 failed |

### 3. The bank's rules all fit

The explicitness bank v6.7.2 is at `artifacts/skills/non-explicitness-detection/references/explicitness-bank.md`.

**Ground 1, supplied:**
- R1 and V6.5 (supplied at, before, or in the next sentence);
- V6.6-A (the quote fills its own slot; the supplier is itself supplied; competitors are listed);
- A2 (sources for "of what");
- A6 (a competitor must fit);
- A7 (announced below);
- A9 (a frame word gives the kind; field terms);
- R3 (the addressee is the only fitting doer);
- A8 (a doer with no fitting candidates).

**Ground 2, indifferent:**
- the substitution test ("any approvals" means the same);
- task values and the act list (slots the task doesn't use);
- judgment words outside a judge task;
- group actors;
- R4 (general terms in lists);
- A1 ("one end state");
- A3 ("the point is about the whole");
- A4 (a criterion only when someone applies it);
- A5 (a second reading must change the point);
- "no relation cue" (separate notices need no link).

**Ground 3, declared open or handed over:**
- R2 (declared openness);
- declared level ("at a high level");
- A4 (declared discretion);
- A8 (authorising the addressee's own plan).

**Overrides and scope rules:**
- **"Demanded when the main point rests on it."** Ground 2 run in reverse: the omission is not indifferent.
- **V6.6-C ("an exemption covers only its own slot").** Indifference must hold slot by slot.

### 4. Why the gates spread

The Ground 2 rules were written as stand-ins for "every reasonable choice gives the same result", not as that test itself. Each stand-in contains a word of extent: "one end state", "the point is about the whole", "only gives a steer", "the point rests on it". A detector can assert the stand-in without checking.

All four recorded failures (from `compare/benchmark_data/explicitness-bank/results-v67.md`) are stand-ins that a check would have refuted:

| Case | Stand-in claimed | Reasonable fillers → results | Under the test |
|---|---|---|---|
| "complete the Airtable records" | one end state | fill empty fields → partly done; verify every field → checked; set status to done → only the status changes | gap |
| "switch off the writing workflows" | point is the set's status | all of them / the three named earlier → different systems stop | gap |
| "your handoff" | not rested on | the 2 Oct note / the handoff section → different content to read | gap |
| "who runs the experiment" | "only gives a steer" | you / Bob → a different person does the work | gap |

**The proposed v6.8 fixes would have tightened each stand-in's wording**, for example "A3 needs the predicate to name the property". That adds new surface conditions with new words of extent. That is the spreading the user named.

**The evidence that the principle is right.** Only one gate was already written as a demonstration of difference. V6.7-A5 reads: *"A gap needs two readings, each cued by something other than the separator or the number itself, that lead to different conclusions, counts, owners or actions."* It was not among the gates that leaked. A1, A3 and A8 were.

### 5. The difference test

**For each slot the wording demands, check in order:**

1. **Supplied?** Quote what fixes exactly one filler, in time. If so, it is fine to leave unsaid.
2. **Indifferent?** List the reasonable fillers and group them by their result. One group means fine; record a representative filler and the shared result.
3. **Declared?** Quote the words that leave it open or hand it over. If so, it is fine.
4. **Otherwise, it is a gap.** Record two fillers from different groups and their two results.

**What makes it hold, where the stand-ins did not:**
- **Bounded fillers.** Fillers come only from:
  - what the text puts in play;
  - the standard senses of the word;
  - the parties the text names.

  Imagined fillers don't count. One representative per group is enough; this is the same idea as testing one input per equivalence class in software testing.
- **Concrete results.** Each group's result must be an observable difference: who does what, what exists, what someone knows. "Both leave the records complete" names no difference, so it cannot support "the same".
- **Cues, not exemptions.** The old gate wordings ("about the whole", "one end state") stay only as cues for *where to run step 2*. They never exempt on their own.
- **An undecided list.** Cases that can't be decided go to a separate list, not into the gap count. If that list grows beyond about 10% of decisions, the result-phrasing rule needs tightening.

### 6. How fine "the same result" must be

The grain depends on:
- **stakes:** the cost of the wrong filler to whoever relies on the text;
- **repairability:** whether the reader can ask back.

A one-day difference is "the same" for a team newsletter and not for a contract deadline. An agent prompt is low in repairability: AI readers resolve ambiguity silently and confidently, so an undetected gap becomes a silent wrong action.

A detector reads both from the declared setting ("a contract", "a prompt to an agent", "a live chat"). With no declaration, it uses a default grain.

### 7. The principle beyond detection

| Use | How the principle applies |
|---|---|
| **Writing** | Before omitting, ask whether any reasonable choice would give the same result. If not, say it. |
| **Fixing** | The author question becomes concrete: "Did you mean A, which leads to X, or B, which leads to Y?" That is more answerable than "which records?". |
| **Economy** | Content whose every reasonable version gives the same result *may* be cut. The same principle decides both what must be said and what need not be. It is "may", because deliberate redundancy (confirming a time zone in a high-stakes setting) is not indifferent at fine grain. |
| **Meaning units** | Every part of a unit gets the test: its referent, its limits, its force (is it a request?), its relations. This fits the meaning finding's hypothesis that explicitness gaps are mostly missing limits or referents (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md). |

### 8. What conveyance covers, and what is left

The principle covers every form of *unsaid*:
- absent;
- late;
- ambiguous;
- implied by standard inference;
- a missing connection;
- a missing force;
- a missing limit.

It does not cover **over-conveyance**: the text saying something unintended, such as an implication the author didn't mean. That is a separate conveyance question, still open.

## Inherited Commitments Re-test

### Bank V6.6-A, what counts as supplied, and V6.6-C, an exemption covers only its own slot

- **Source:** bank v6.7.2, lines 232–296.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, with the frame revised.
- **Evidence:** V6.6-A is Ground 1 in full. V6.6-C is why indifference is judged slot by slot.

### Bank A1–A9 gates

- **Source:** bank v6.7.2, lines 23–133.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Found INVALID as exemptions, valid as cues.
- **Evidence:** four recorded failures, each refuted by the test. A5, written as a demonstration, did not leak.

### Persistent misses: "words of extent get the widest reading"; the planned v6.8 tightening

- **Source:** the bank-persistent-misses record; `results-v67.md` lines 117–143.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. The observation is confirmed. The planned remedy is found INVALID.
- **Evidence:** tightening adds new stand-ins. The difference test replaces them.

### Detect the text's explicitness, not reader knowledge

- **Source:** user correction (memory record `detection-target-explicitness`).
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
- **Evidence:** all three grounds are judged from the text, its declared setting and standard senses. Field knowledge enters only when the setting declares the field.

### "Before" means established context only; requirements as the reference

- **Source:** the need finding (16-18), section 2.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
- **Evidence:** this is the reference for Ground 2 when a need is declared.

### Explicitness gaps are mostly missing limits or referents (hypothesis)

- **Source:** the meaning finding (16-16), section 8.
- **Status:** INHERITED-WITHOUT-RE-TEST.
- **Reason:** it is consistent with the principle, but classifying real gaps is a separate test.

### Gap 3's hard core; what counts as supplied is a closed list

- **Source:** the three-gaps finding, section 6.
- **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
- **Evidence:** the hard core is now the three grounds plus the test.

## Next Actions

### MUST

- **What:** Build bank v6.8 on the three grounds.
  - The A-gates become cues.
  - Add the difference test with bounded fillers and concrete results.
  - Add output fields for filler groups and results.
  - Run it twice on benchmarks 2 and 3, judged by blind editors, against v6.7.2.
- **Who:** the next bank inquiry.
- **Gate:** before the principle is used in the fix skill or in writing guidance.
- **Why:** this is the empirical test of whether the principle breaks the precision/recall seesaw.

### COULD

- **What:** First, re-decide by hand the roughly 48 real gaps v6.7 exempted, plus about 30 known false gaps, using the difference test.
  - **Who:** one agent or one session.
  - **Gate:** before the full v6.8 run.
  - **Why:** a cheap early check that the test recovers real gaps without bringing back false ones.
  - **Depends-on:** MUST "bank v6.8". OVERRIDE: adoption-ready despite the open MUST. Reason: this is a cheaper pre-test of the same idea; it informs the MUST and does not depend on it.
- **What:** Change the fix skill's author questions to the form "A (→ X) or B (→ Y)?".
  - **Who:** the fix skill's maintainer.
  - **Gate:** after the MUST.
  - **Why:** more answerable questions.
  - **Depends-on:** MUST "bank v6.8". This COULD is GATED: the questions need the filler-group fields.

### DEFERRED

- **What:** Over-conveyance (unintended meaning).
  - **Gate:** when a real case of harmful unintended implication is recorded.
  - **Why (if revived):** it is the part of conveyance this principle doesn't cover.
- **What:** Economy checks using the "may cut" rule.
  - **Gate:** when an economy check is built.

## Reasoning

**Killed: tightening the surface gates.**
- It is cheap and was the planned v6.8.
- But each tightening adds a new stand-in with new words of extent. The v6.6 → v6.7 history shows the seesaw it produces.
- Its wordings survive as cues.

**Ruled out: reader-relative omission.** "The reader can work it out" brings back reader-knowledge guessing, which the user rejected. Expert knowledge enters only through the declared setting.

**Refined: "same result".**
- The critique found that "same result" can be vouched just like "one end state".
- Results must now be concrete, observable differences, with one representative per group, from a bounded filler set.

**Refined: stakes.** A detector cannot read stakes from prose. Stakes and repairability come from the declared setting, with a default otherwise.

**Survived:**
- the three grounds;
- the full mapping of the bank's rules;
- the explanation of why the gates spread;
- the difference test;
- gates as cues;
- the undecided list as a quality signal;
- the "A → X or B → Y" author question;
- economy as "may cut";
- the test applied to every unit part.

**External support:**
- **Decision theory:** information is worth giving only if it could change the decision.
- **Software testing:** equivalence classes, one input per class.
- **Interface specifications:** unspecified means the implementer chooses, so discretion must be acceptable for every choice.
- **The bank's own A5.**

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

In the v6.8 run, track three numbers:
- the size of the undecided list;
- how often results are written vaguely ("both complete");
- whether recall recovers without precision dropping.

### Research Frontiers

- **Over-conveyance:** how is unintended meaning detected and judged?
- **Default grain:** what should it be when the setting declares no stakes?

### Refinement Triggers

- **Re-open the three grounds** if the v6.8 run produces legitimate omissions that fit none of them. Two of the same kind is enough.
- **Re-open the difference test** if precision falls below v6.7.2 by more than the noise floor (about 8–10 real gaps).

## Later evidence (added 2026-10-09)

The MUST was run: bank v6.8 was tested against v6.7.2 on benchmarks 2 and 3. Results are in `AIOpsMan/compare/benchmark_data/explicitness-bank/results-v68.md`.

**Precision rose.** There were about 35 fewer false gaps per run in a blind editor pool, and fewer certain false alarms on benchmark 3.

**Recall did not rise.**
- Editor-judged real gaps were flat.
- Authors' issues fully found fell from 55 to 46.
- Benchmark 3 was mixed.

**Why the test failed in operation:** the difference test leaked in the way this finding's critique warned it could.
- All 229 "indifferent" exemptions gave a single shared result, which the spec allowed. Many of those results only restated the sentence.
- Results were also judged against a different act from the one the slot belongs to.

**What this means for the principle.** The three grounds are not refuted; they still explain the rules. The demonstration must be stricter:
- write a result for each filler, never one shared result;
- judge results on the slot's own act;
- remove UNDECIDED, which was misused for real gaps.

This is v6.8.1, not yet tested.

**Follow-up, v6.8.1 (same day).** The stricter demonstration was built and tested: per-filler results, the slot's own act, no UNDECIDED. Results are in `AIOpsMan/compare/benchmark_data/explicitness-bank/results-v681.md`.

- **The leak is fixed.** Exemptions are now well-formed, and the lost cases (for example "complete the Airtable records") are recovered.
- **Performance now equals v6.7.2.** Editor-judged real and false gaps, authors' issues and benchmark 3 are all within noise, and run-to-run consistency is slightly better.
- **The principle holds, but it is not a recall lever.** The three exemption regimes changed which gaps were reported, not how many. Recall is limited by what one detection pass can report.

## Source Input

<details>
<summary>Raw user input for this finding</summary>

```text
3. Conveyance: what a text actually makes available to its reader.
- Only one part is defined: the explicitness test.
- Core to define first: a principle for what a text may legitimately leave unsaid.
- This is exactly where the explicitness bank keeps failing. Its exemption rules keep spreading because there's no principle behind them.


lets dive deep into thsi
```

</details>

# [9] Finding: Essence formation — the moves, revisited

**Source:** `/Users/nsstorm/Desktop/projects/AIOpsMan/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_17-15__essence-formation-moves-revisited/finding.md`

---
status: active
model: claude-opus-5-5
effort: unknown
refines: devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-47__essence-formation-method/finding.md
---
# Finding: Essence formation — the moves, revisited

## Changes from Prior

**Prior path:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-47__essence-formation-method/finding.md

**Revision trigger:** The user asked for a deep dive into how an essence is formed. Since the prior finding, the need, the meaning unit and conveyance have each been defined, and the prior finding had been judged too tied to the operational case.

**What's preserved:**
- Formation is answering, not filling in facets.
- The facets serve as a final sweep.
- The user's seven moves.
- Companions.
- Coverage vs sufficiency.
- Backward formation.
- The AI/author dialogue.

**What's changed:**
- The seven moves are re-sorted into two kinds:
  - where an answer comes from;
  - two operations every answer goes through.
- "The act's questions" are now the need's requirements: questions, conditions and constraints.
- "Produced" splits into three kinds.

**What's new:**
- Two moves at the ends: stating the need first, and closing checks last.
- Failures become open conditions you can see.
- Ask the author only when the possible answers lead to different results.
- One settle sheet for the author.
- When the author is absent, an answer's unconfirmed status is shown in the text.
- "Done" means formed, not correct.

**Migration:** none.

## Question

The user summarised what we understood about forming an essence:
- answer the questions the recipient's next act raises, one at a time, with the 14 facets as a final sweep;
- each answer comes from one of seven moves: settled by the author, gathered from sources, selected for this exchange, produced, grounded, left open, or asked of the author.

They asked for a deep dive, generic and not over-defined, now that the need, the meaning unit and conveyance have been defined.

## Finding Summary

- **The seven moves are two different kinds of thing.**
  - **Five say where an answer comes from:**
    - settled by the author;
    - gathered from a source;
    - produced;
    - asked of the author;
    - left open.

    "Asked" and "left open" are really what happens to a requirement that can't be answered yet: asked is temporary, and its reply comes back as settled; left open is final for this exchange.
  - **Two are done to every answer:** selected (traced to a requirement, at the right level) and grounded (its status and origin attached).

    Listing them beside the sources made them look optional. That is how the dropped statuses and limits in the past failures happened.
- **Formation answers the need's requirements, not only questions.**
  - Requirements come in three kinds: questions, conditions (for example, "a condolence must acknowledge the loss") and constraints.
  - A condition is met by an answer of the right kind (an expressed sorrow). A constraint is met when no answer breaks it.
- **"Produced" covers three kinds of work.**

  | Kind | What it means | What it needs | Past failure |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Concluding | Drawing a conclusion from gathered material | the gathered material | example 7: five documents described, no conclusion drawn |
  | Choosing | Recommending or deciding between options | options and criteria | — |
  | Designing | Creating something new | its constraints | example 1: the continuing operation was never designed |

  Producing creates meaning that didn't exist, which in Workcraft is Semantic Formulation's work. Formation may produce, but every produced answer stays marked "proposed, untested" until the author settles it and it has been tested.
- **Two moves were missing at the ends.**
  - **First, state the need:** who must reach which state, with its source. Without this, the questions are guesses. This was the September failure.
  - **Last, close with three checks:**
    - a facet sweep, whose output is missed requirements that go back to the need;
    - a sufficiency probe: a fresh reader quotes where each requirement is carried;
    - a test of whatever was produced, whose output is objections the author settles, never "passed".
- **Formation is closing the open conditions.** Every old failure is now a visible condition, not something anyone has to confess.

  | Open condition | Old failure it catches |
  |---|---|
  | requirement with no source | the need was never stated |
  | requirement with no answer | a conclusion or design was skipped |
  | answer with no standing | statuses and limits were dropped |
  | answer not traced to a requirement | the AI chose the level silently |
  | produced answer unsettled or untested | the plan was never tested |

  Formation is done when no condition is open, or each remaining one is declared open.
- **Who does what: the AI proposes, the author settles.**
  - **The AI:** gathers with quotes, grounds, traces, proposes selections and produced answers, drafts questions, runs the closing checks.
  - **The author:** settles produced answers, answers questions, decides what stays open, confirms the level.
- **Ask the author only when the possible answers lead to different results.**
  - The question takes the form "A, which leads to X, or B, which leads to Y?". This is the conveyance finding's difference test.
  - Where the answers don't differ, the AI picks one, marks it proposed, and logs it in a skimmable section of the author's sheet.
- **The author gets one settle sheet.** It lists the questions, the produced answers and the AI's picks, with production questions first, because they are the long pole.
- **When the author is absent,** an unconfirmed answer that the message relies on is marked in the text, for example "recommendation (not yet confirmed)".
- **There are two routes.**
  - **Forward:** form, then draft. Better when the stakes are high.
  - **Backward:** draft, extract the answers, then confirm. The default when stakes are low.

  Both produce the same list of open conditions, and formation interleaves with drafting.
- **"Formed" is not "correct".** Closing every condition does not prove the sources are true or a decision is sound. Every report says what was not checked.
- **Confidence limit.** The model was tested on a constructed condolence note and on the earlier recorded failures. It has not been run on real drafts. That is the next step.

## Finding

### 1. Why revisit this

The earlier formation finding listed seven moves and found that real failures were moves skipped silently. The user later judged that finding too tied to one manager's briefings.

Since then, three foundations have been defined:
- **the need:** a state for each party, which produces requirements;
- **the meaning unit:** a point, plus what is done with it, plus its limits, with a standing that needs quotes;
- **conveyance:** what a text may leave unsaid.

This inquiry rebuilds formation on those foundations.

### 2. The seven moves, re-sorted

**Where an answer comes from:**

| Source | Starting status | What it needs |
|---|---|---|
| Settled by the author | settled | a quote from the author, or an answer the author gave |
| Gathered from a source | gathered | a quote from the source |
| Produced (conclude, choose or design) | proposed, untested | the author settles it; it is tested |

**What happens to a requirement that can't be answered yet:**
- **Asked:** turned into a concrete question for the author. The reply returns as settled.
- **Left open:** the author decides it stays open for this exchange. That is legitimate, and the conveyance finding counts "declared open" as one of its three grounds.

**What is done to every answer:**
- **Select:** trace it to a requirement, and decide its level and depth.
- **Ground:** attach its standing (who or what settled it, and where it can be quoted from), using the quote rule.
- **Companions travel with it:** limits stay inside the answer; the reasons a holder needs are linked to it.

**Why this matters.** In a flat list of seven, "grounded" sits beside "gathered" as though you might do one or the other. Failures like example 2 and the Christine summary happened because grounding was skipped. Making select and ground mandatory for every answer is the point of the re-sort.

These source labels match the meaning finding's status values (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md). Backward formation adds one more: "found in draft".

### 3. Producing: concluding, choosing, designing

Three kinds of work create meaning that didn't exist before:
- **Concluding:** drawing what follows from gathered material. Example 7 described five documents and drew no conclusion.
- **Choosing:** recommending or deciding between options. It needs the options and the criteria stated.
- **Designing:** creating something new, such as an operation, a plan or a structure. Example 1's continuing operation was never designed.

Producing is, in Workcraft terms, Semantic Formulation's work, not Communication's. Formation may include it, but it must be **visible**:
- every produced answer starts as "proposed, untested";
- the author settles it;
- a test challenges it.

The old failures were never "the AI produced something". They were producing silently, or not producing at all when a requirement needed it.

### 4. The two moves at the ends

**First: state the need.**
- Following the need finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_16-18__the-need-what-a-communication-must-achieve-and-for-whom/finding.md), name who must reach which state, with the source for it.
- Derive the requirements from that.
- The request is evidence of the state, not the state itself.
- With no source, state a visible assumption.

**Last: close.**
- **Facet sweep.** Do the 14 facets suggest a requirement nobody stated? Examples:
  - a relationship to maintain;
  - history to acknowledge;
  - a later reader.

  New requirements go back to the need. The sweep runs once per pass.
- **Sufficiency probe.** A fresh reader quotes where the text carries each requirement.
- **Test produced answers.** A fresh agent challenges the plan or recommendation and returns objections, each tied to the assumptions it quotes. The author settles. The test never declares "passed".

### 5. Formation as closing open conditions

Every earlier failure leaves a visible trace:

| Open condition | Example failure |
|---|---|
| a requirement with no source | September: the need was never stated |
| a requirement with no answer | examples 1 and 7: nothing was produced where producing was needed |
| an answer with no standing, or with a lost limit | example 2 and the Christine summary |
| an answer not traced to a requirement | the AI chose the level silently |
| a produced answer unsettled or untested | examples 5 and 6: the plan was never tested |

**Formation is done** when none of these is open, or each remaining one has been declared open by the author.

That turns formation from a procedure into a short, checkable list. It scales down: a one-line reply has one requirement, one settled answer, and trivial checks.

**Worked check on a condolence note:**
- **Frame:** the colleague knows the loss is recognised and that support is offered. The source is the occasion.
- **Fill:** the expressed sorrow is the author's own, so it is settled. The offer of support is produced, so it is proposed until the author confirms it.
- **Close:** the facet sweep raises the relationship (how close are they?). That adds a requirement, "warmth that matches the relationship".

### 6. Who does what

| Move | The AI alone | The author |
|---|---|---|
| State the need | proposes it from sources, or states a visible assumption | confirms it when it was inferred |
| Gather | yes, with quotes | — |
| Select | proposes, traced to a requirement | confirms the level and the main point |
| Ground | yes, by the quote rule | — |
| Produce | proposes (proposed, untested) | settles |
| Ask | drafts the question as "A → X or B → Y" | answers |
| Leave open | proposes | decides |
| Sweep, probe, test | yes, using fresh agents | acts on the results |

**Ask only when it matters.**
- If the candidate answers lead to the same result, there is nothing to ask. The AI picks one, marks it proposed, and records the candidates and the shared result.
- Those picks go in a short, skimmable section of the author's sheet, and the author can overturn any of them.

**One settle sheet.**
- It holds the questions, the produced answers and the AI's picks.
- Production questions come first, because they need the most author time.
- The author is interrupted once.

**When the author is absent,** an unconfirmed answer the message relies on is marked as such in the text: "recommendation (not yet confirmed)". Its standing is conveyed honestly instead of hidden.

### 7. Routes and interleaving

Writing research shows that drafting reveals what you think, so formation and drafting interleave. There are two routes in:
- **Forward:** form first, then draft. Better for high stakes, because a missing conclusion or design is caught before anyone writes around it.
- **Backward:** draft first, extract the answers (each starting as "found in draft"), then confirm. Cheaper for low stakes.

Both end in the same list of open conditions.

### 8. What "done" does not mean

Closing every condition means the essence is **formed**:
- every requirement has a traced, grounded answer, or has been declared open.

It does not mean the essence is **correct**:
- a gathered answer can quote a wrong source;
- a settled answer can be a poor decision.

Checking the truth of sources belongs to the content check (Workcraft's Context Accumulation). Judging whether a decision is sound belongs to the produced-answer test and the author.

Following the materialization-vs-content finding's declaration rule, every formation report says what it did not check.

## Inherited Commitments Re-test

- **Commitment:** formation is answering questions; the facets serve as a sweep.
  - **Source:** 15-47, Finding Summary.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** questions widen to requirements. The sweep's output becomes new requirements fed back to the need.
- **Commitment:** the moves are settled, gathered, selected, produced, grounded, left open, asked.
  - **Source:** 15-47, Finding Summary.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** they are re-sorted into sources and mandatory operations. Its own sentence, "An AI can mostly gather, select and ground… Producing a judgment and deciding what stays open usually need the author", already grouped select and ground with gather.
- **Commitment:** enough = coverage + sufficiency; companions; backward formation; a preservation map.
  - **Source:** 15-47.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:**
    - coverage = no unanswered requirement;
    - sufficiency = the probe;
    - companions split into limits and reasons;
    - backward = the low-stakes route.
- **Commitment:** requirements; the request as evidence; visible assumption.
  - **Source:** the need finding (16-18).
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** this is the first move at the ends, "state the need".
- **Commitment:** standing with quotable entry conditions; the AI never self-settles; extract and confirm.
  - **Source:** the meaning finding (16-16).
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** the sources set the starting status. All AI work stays proposed until settled.
- **Commitment:** the three grounds; the "A→X or B→Y" question.
  - **Source:** the conveyance finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-52__conveyance-what-a-text-may-leave-unsaid/finding.md).
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** "ask only when the answers differ" and "declared open" both come from it.
- **Commitment:** the 14 facets as a guide to attention.
  - **Source:** the substrate finding (22-09).
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** the facets act as the closing sweep.

## Next Actions

### MUST

- **What:** Run backward formation on 3 real drafts, one of them AI-written. For each:
  - state the need;
  - extract the answers;
  - list the open conditions.

  Then check whether the conditions match the problems the author recognises.
- **Who:** a test inquiry; the user picks the drafts.
- **Gate:** before any formation skill is written.
- **Why:** the model has not met real texts.

### COULD

- **What:** Run forward formation on one real high-stakes message, with the author answering one settle sheet. Record:
  - the author's time;
  - the questions asked;
  - the picks the author overturned.
- **Who:** a test inquiry.
- **Gate:** after the MUST.
- **Why:** it tests the dialogue and how much author time it costs.
- **Depends-on:** MUST "backward formation on 3 real drafts". This COULD is GATED.

### DEFERRED

- **What:** Use the settle sheet as the standard human interface for AI drafting.
- **Gate:** when an AI-drafting skill is designed.
- **Why (if revived):** the author's job would shrink to the decisions that change something.

## Reasoning

**Killed: backward formation as the only route.**
- Extraction does reveal a missing conclusion, as an unanswered requirement.
- But it reveals it late: after a full draft and the author's reading.
- For high stakes, forming first is cheaper.

**Superseded: the flat list of seven.** It hid that select and ground are mandatory.

**Refined:**
- **"No open conditions" now means formed, not correct.** A gathered answer can still be wrong, and a settled one can be a poor decision.
- **"The AI picks when answers don't differ" now requires logged picks** on a skimmable sheet, because the AI's "it makes no difference" is itself a judgment.
- **"Test produced answers" now returns objections, not verdicts,** because "the plan holds" from a fresh agent would be vouching.

**Survived:**
- sources and operations;
- produce splitting into three;
- the moves at the ends;
- formation as closing conditions;
- the division of labour;
- the settle sheet, with production questions first;
- unconfirmed standing shown in the text when the author is absent;
- the two routes.

The condolence check shows it works outside briefings.

**Established practice that supports it:**
- journalism separates gathering, verification and news judgment;
- issue-based design rationale records options, criteria and the decision;
- agile's definition of done mirrors the closing checks.

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

In the MUST test, record any failure the author recognises that none of the open conditions catches. Two of the same kind re-opens the list.

### Research Frontiers

- Which facets most often reveal missed requirements?
- Where exactly does formation hand over to Semantic Formulation?

### Refinement Triggers

- **Re-open "ask only when the answers differ"** if, in the forward test, the author overturns more than about 20% of the AI's picks.
- **Re-open "the backward route by default for low stakes"** if real low-stakes drafts show missing productions caught too late to fix cheaply.

## Source Input

<details>
<summary>Raw user input for this finding</summary>

```text
What we now understand about how an essence gets formed:
- The work isn't filling in the 14 facets. It's answering the questions the recipient's next act raises, one at a time. The facets work better as a final sweep for anything missed.
- Each answer comes from a different kind of move:
  - already settled by the author;
  - gathered from sources;
  - selected for this exchange;
  - produced (a judgment, recommendation or design that doesn't exist yet);
  - grounded (with its source and status);
  - left open deliberately;
  - asked of the author.  lets dive deep into this now
```

</details>
