# [10] Finding: the substrate concept that arranges the message

**Source:** `/Users/nsstorm/Desktop/projects/AIOpsMan/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-31__the-substrate-concept-that-arranges-the-message/finding.md`

---
status: active
model: claude-opus-5-5
effort: unknown
refines: devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_18-38__why-a-full-substrate-still-produced-a-weird-opening/finding.md
---
# Finding: the substrate concept that arranges the message

## Changes from Prior

- **Prior path:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_18-38__why-a-full-substrate-still-produced-a-weird-opening/finding.md
- **Revision trigger:** stronger framing. You pointed out that if the substrate's order caused the failure, then the substrate itself is missing something.
- **What's preserved:**
  - the diagnosis (the draft copied the substrate's own order);
  - the governing point;
  - fact roles;
  - the fresh-reader check on the text.
- **What's changed:**
  - **Before:** 18-38 split arrangement across three places:
    - a governing point in the substrate;
    - a separate "arrangement plan" as a bridge;
    - the text.
  - **Now:** there are two places.
    - **The substrate holds a point structure.** This is meaning, and the message's order is part of it.
    - **Materialization holds the wording.**
  - The bridge plan is simply the top of the structure.
- **What's new:**
  - ranked requirements;
  - a declared main point that carries its standing;
  - key points traced to requirements;
  - facts placed under points;
  - no orphan facts;
  - three checks on the substrate itself, before any text is written.
- **MUST/COULD drift:**
  - **Prior MUST:** "Change the substrate template: the essence opens with the governing point; each fact carries a role; the paragraph plan becomes lead sentences in the order of the reader's questions; add an entry-state line."
  - **New MUST:** "Write the substrate as a point structure: ranked need; main point with standing; key points naming their requirement; facts under points with relation and standing; order note only for exceptions."
  - **Rationale:** the separate paragraph plan is absorbed into the structure, and requirement ranking is added.

## Question

You asked: if the draft copied the substrate's order, the substrate is missing something. "Maybe substrate should have a concept to, to arrange this thing. Let's dive deep into this."

**Goal:** a conceptual account of the missing concept:
- whether it is really missing;
- what it is;
- how it relates to the existing parts of Communication Craft (the need, meaning units, relations, several holders);
- how it is formed and checked;
- where it lives.

## Finding Summary

- **Yes, the substrate is missing something. It isn't a new idea; it is the substrate's shape.**
  - The substrate had the right *contents*, in separate sections: need, essence, facts, choices, ask, plan.
  - It never said how they **hang together**: which requirement matters most, which point answers it, and what each fact does for which point.
  - So the draft followed the order of the sections.
- **The pieces already existed in earlier findings, scattered:**
  - "which questions are answered, **and in what order**… the decision and the recommendation come first" (12-53, the view-combination finding). This was lost when 16-18 widened questions into requirements.
  - "Reading order follows from rank. It is not a source of rank." (16-28, the several-parties finding.)
  - "One unit is the point; the other supports it." (16-16, the meaning-unit finding.)
  - None of these had a place in the substrate.
- **The missing concept is the point structure.** The substrate becomes an outline, main point at the top:
  - **Ranked need.** The requirements are marked main, next, or depth, with the rank sourced the way the need itself is.
  - **Main point.** One statement of the act, the answer, and the ask. It answers the top requirement and shows its standing. A proposed main point is the first thing the author settles.
  - **Key points.** Each answers a requirement the main point raises: why? what does it take? what does it cost? what do you need from me?
  - **Facts.** Each sits under a point, with its relation (support, limit, condition, contrast, background, sequence) and its standing. That relation is the fact's "role".
  - **No orphans.** A fact that cannot name its point is either background or a point the author has not yet stated.
- **Order is read off the structure, not decided separately.** Tell it top-down: a point before its support, and siblings in rank or sequence order. Depart from this only for a sourced reason, recorded in a short order note:
  - the genre;
  - the reader's entry state;
  - a relational condition such as softening bad news.
- **The structure is meaning, not presentation.** Ranking and relations change what the reader takes away: "1,633 reports exist" as the point and as background are different messages. Only the wording is left to materialization.
- **The top of the structure is already a short message.** The main point plus the key points is the "four lines" you would review. Copying the substrate's order is now correct rather than harmful.
- **Three checks on the substrate, run before any text is written:**
  - quote the main point and the top requirement: does one answer the other?
  - can every fact name its point?
  - does every key point trace to a requirement?
- **Tested literally on the Signals substrate.**
  - Its own content, placed in the structure, gives the good opening and the four good leads.
  - The 1,633 figure lands as background under "what does it cost?" and cannot lead.
  - No draft has been written from it yet.

## Finding

### 1. Why this question

The previous inquiry (18-38) found that a draft built from a complete substrate opened weirdly because it copied the substrate's own order:
- the essence was written as a story;
- the facts were a flat list;
- the paragraph plan was ordered by kind of content.

You drew the obvious conclusion: if the substrate's form causes the order, the substrate is missing a concept. This inquiry asks what that concept is.

The **substrate** here means the structured account of what a message must convey, built before the text. Its parts so far are the need (who must reach what state), the essence, the facts with their sources and standing, the choices, and the ask.

### 2. Is something really missing?

The strongest counter-view is that nothing is missing. The concepts exist in earlier findings, and the author simply did not apply them. That view fails for two reasons.

**The concepts had no place to live.** The substrate's sections have:
- no field for rank;
- no field for a fact's relation;
- no declared main point.

The form decides what gets filled in, and the Signals author filled in exactly the form.

**One concept was actually lost.** The 12-53 view-combination finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_12-53__what-determines-the-view-combination-an-exchange-needs/finding.md) said the need fixes "which questions are answered, and in what order. The primary act's questions lead."

The later need finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_16-18__the-need-what-a-communication-must-achieve-and-for-whom/finding.md) widened questions into requirements: questions, conditions and constraints. It kept the questions, but nothing carried their rank. The Signals substrate's "reader's need" listed three things with no rank among them.

So the answer is: **missing from the substrate, but not new to the framework.** What is missing is the *shape* that brings the existing pieces together.

### 3. What the concept is: the point structure

The substrate is written as an outline, main point at the top.

**1. Ranked need.** Each requirement is marked main, next, or depth.
- Rank comes from the same sources as the need, in order:
  - the request;
  - a standing arrangement;
  - the workflow;
  - the genre's convention;
  - asking the author.
- Where nothing settles the rank, the order makes no difference to the result. In the conveyance finding's terms it is indifferent, and any reasonable order is fine.

**2. Main point.** One statement: the message's act ("I'm proposing…", "I need a decision on…", "For information…"), the answer, and the ask or next step.
- It must answer the top-ranked requirement.
- It carries its standing:
  - **settled:** quoted from the author;
  - **gathered:** quoted from a source;
  - **proposed:** produced by the AI or found in a draft.
- The main point is usually the most consequential answer and often the least settled. So a proposed main point is the **first item on the author's settle sheet** (the single list of questions and proposed answers the author confirms, from the formation finding).

**3. Key points.** Each one names the requirement it answers. Usually these are the next-ranked requirements: the questions the main point raises in the reader.
- For a request they are typically: why? what does it take? what does it cost? what do you need from me?
- If the main point raises a question the need did not list, that question is added to the need.

**4. Facts.** Each fact sits under a point, with two things:
- **its relation** to that point, which is its role:
  - support;
  - limit;
  - condition;
  - contrast;
  - background;
  - sequence.
- **its standing.**

This uses the relation core from the meaning-unit finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md), plus "background".

**5. No orphans.** A fact that cannot name its point is one of two things:
- **background** that belongs deeper;
- **a point the author hasn't stated yet,** which goes back to formation.

Research-diary material shows up here, before it can reach the text.

**6. Order note.** Present only when the order departs from top-down, and always with its source.

**With several holders,** branches carry holder labels. Each holder's essentials sit above their likely stopping point. This is the several-parties finding's placement rule, now with a structure to apply it to.

**With documentation,** the structure has several main points (one per top question). The order follows how readers look things up.

### 4. The Signals substrate, rebuilt

Taking the actual Signals content and placing it, with nothing added:

- **Ranked need:**
  1. "what operational response you recommend" (main);
  2. "what the work has established" (why);
  3. "the size of the initial commitment" (cost).
- **Main point (proposed; settle first):** I'm proposing that AI Operations runs a regular Signals review, which I'd initially coordinate. I'm asking you to approve a bounded start.
  - **Why?** Similar-looking reports need very different responses.
    - *Support:* a trial of three reports gave three conclusions: an instruction correction, a known tool limitation, and a recovery question.
  - **What does it take?** The report, the change and the result need separate evidence, so the review must follow work through to its result.
    - *Support:* trial finding.
  - **What does it cost?** The real effort is not known yet.
    - *Limit:* the trial timings are "not a weekly effort estimate".
    - *Background:* one preserved week holds 1,633 reports.
  - **What do you need from me?** A starting time allowance and a review date.

Read top-down, this gives the opening and the four leads that the previous critique judged good. **The 1,633 figure can only land as background under cost.** It cannot open the message, because it is not the main point and does not answer the top requirement.

### 5. Order follows from the structure

The default is top-down: the main point first, each point before its support, and siblings in rank order or sequence order. This follows from the several-parties rule that readers stop early, so their essentials must come first.

Some messages are not top-down. In each case the *structure* is the same; only the *linearization* (the order in which the structure is told) changes:

| Message | How the structure handles it |
|---|---|
| Incident report | The summary leads, and the timeline is a sequence relation among key points |
| Instructions | The goal leads, and the steps are in sequence |
| Bad news with reasons first | A sourced exception (a relational condition), recorded in the order note |
| Scientific paper | The genre's order, recorded as its source |

So the earlier rule "order follows from rank" (16-28) is refined to: **order follows from rank and relations, with sourced exceptions.**

### 6. Where the line between meaning and expression falls

| Part | Layer | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Rank, main point, relations | **meaning (substrate)** | Changing them changes what the reader takes away (the conveyance finding's difference test) |
| Order | **derived from meaning** | Read off the structure; only exceptions are recorded |
| Wording, transitions, paragraphing | **materialization** | Can change without changing the takeaway |

This resolves an earlier tension:
- The materialization finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_19-33__communication-substrate-materialization-and-review-loop/finding.md) put "selecting and arranging" in materialization.
- The substrate-concepts finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_15-59__communication-substrate-concepts-and-boundaries/finding.md) said ordering that expresses a relation is meaning.

Both are right once "arranging" is split into structure (meaning) and wording (expression).

**The top of the structure is already a short message.** The main point plus the key points can be sent as is. So the substrate is not purely "thinking" separated from "presenting". The ordering is meaning that also reads as a message, which is why copying it is now safe. The substrate's other parts (sources, standing, the need) stay as substrate only.

### 7. How it is formed and checked

**Forming.** The structure is built by steps the formation finding already has:
- **state the need,** now with rank;
- **select:** trace each answer to a requirement, which now also places it under a point;
- **ground:** attach standing.

The new work is:
- one main-point statement;
- one requirement label per key point;
- one relation tag per fact.

**Checking the substrate, before any text exists.** All three checks compare quoted items; none asks "is it good?".
1. **Main point vs top requirement.** Quote both. Does the main point answer it?
2. **No orphans.** Every fact names its point.
3. **Key points traced.** Every key point names a requirement.

In Signals, check 1 would have failed at once. The essence's first sentence, "We have collected a substantial body of feedback…", answers no requirement.

**Checking the text afterwards.** The fresh-reader check from 18-38 stays. The reader states the point after sentence 1 and after the leads, and that is compared with the main point and the key points. The structure is now what the check compares against.

### 8. What this changes in earlier findings

- **16-16 (meaning unit).** "The main point … does not have to be declared" is corrected: the main point is declared, with standing. Signals shows that undeclared means undecided.
- **16-18 (need).** Requirements carry rank. This restores 12-53's "in what order".
- **19-33 (materialization).** "Arranging" splits into structure (meaning, in the substrate) and wording (materialization).
- **18-38 (weird opening).** The separate arrangement plan is absorbed. The leads you review are the key points.
- **16-28 (several parties).** "Order follows from rank" becomes "order follows from rank and relations, with sourced exceptions".

## Inherited Commitments Re-test

- **Commitment:** the arrangement layer: a governing point in the substrate, a separate arrangement plan as a bridge, and a fresh-reader check on the text.
  - **Source:** 18-38 finding, Finding §3–5.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - The governing point and the fresh-reader check stand.
    - The separate plan is absorbed. Its content (leads in reader-question order) is the top of the point structure.
    - The three-layer split becomes two layers, because ordering that expresses relations is meaning (the literal Signals build).
- **Commitment:** the need is a state per holder, which yields requirements (questions, conditions, constraints) from quotable sources.
  - **Source:** 16-18 finding.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Requirements need rank.
    - The 12-53 "in what order" was dropped in the widening.
    - Signals §1's unranked list shows the cost.
- **Commitment:** the main point is a role and "does not have to be declared"; reasons are linked units; the relation core is support, condition, contrast and sequence.
  - **Source:** 16-16 finding.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED — "does not have to be declared" found INVALID; the rest confirmed.
  - **Evidence:**
    - In Signals nobody worked out the main point, and the essence opened with background.
    - The relation core works as the fact roles, with "background" added.
- **Commitment:** placement by stopping point; "reading order follows from rank".
  - **Source:** 16-28 finding.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** incident reports, instructions and bad-news letters need relations and sourced exceptions as well as rank.
- **Commitment:** the two-half message brief (upper half: reader state, essence, plan; lower half: answers with source and status).
  - **Source:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_17-45__signals-proposal-dialogue-as-communication-substrate/finding.md.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - The lower half (answers with source and status) becomes the facts' standing inside the structure.
    - The upper half's essence and plan become the main point and key points.

## Next Actions

### MUST

- **What:** Rebuild the Signals substrate as a point structure (section 4 above, written out). Settle the proposed main point. Have a fresh agent, given only that substrate, draft the message. Run the fresh-reader check.
  - **Who:** this session, with you settling the main point.
  - **Gate:** your go-ahead.
  - **Why:** the structure was tested by placing content, not by drafting from it. This shows whether the substrate's form alone now gives the right order.
- **What:** Write the point-structure shape as a short reference:
  - ranked need;
  - main point (act, answer, ask, standing);
  - key points with their requirement;
  - facts with parent, relation and standing;
  - order note for exceptions;
  - the three substrate checks.
  - **Who:** the Communication Craft substrate reference (the 17-45 two-half brief, rewritten).
  - **Gate:** after the Signals redraft passes the fresh-reader check.
  - **Why:** it gives every next message the form that carries order.

### COULD

- **What:** Build the structure for a second real message of another kind: an explanation, an update or a condolence.
  - **Who:** this session.
  - **Gate:** the next such message you write.
  - **Why:** the general claim rests on constructed cases.
  - **Depends-on:** MUST item "Write the point-structure shape". This COULD is GATED.
- **What:** Choose a name. Options are "point structure", "structure", "spine", or your own word.
  - **Who:** you.
  - **Gate:** before the reference is written.
  - **Why:** it is used in every substrate.

### DEFERRED

- **What:** Different lengths as cuts of one structure at different depths (subject line, chat message, email, appendix).
  - **Gate:** the first time two materializations of one substrate are needed.
  - **Why (if revived):** a common mechanism for several materializations and for several holders' stopping points.

## Reasoning

**Survived: the point structure in the substrate.**
- It was tested by placing the actual Signals content.
- The result reproduced the good opening and leads, and forced the 1,633 figure into background.
- It held against prosecution on both of its premises:
  - "order follows from rank" was refined to include relations;
  - "the main point can be fixed early" gained the settle-first rule.

**Refined: "it's just Minto's pyramid".** Partly true, and that is external support. The framework adds:
- rank sourced from the need, not the author's sense of importance;
- standing on every node;
- the orphan check;
- quotable substrate checks.

**Absorbed: the separate arrangement plan (18-38).** It duplicated the top of the structure and would drift from it.

**Absorbed: "rank the requirements and tag the facts" alone.** Without a written main point and key points, the flat list stays and the drafter still has no order to copy.

**Reframed: "the structure is just the shortest message, keep it out of the substrate".**
- It is true that the top levels read as a message.
- It is wrong that they are only text: ranking and relations change the takeaway.

**Dead: "apply existing principles better".** The form decides what gets filled in, and one principle (ordered questions) had already been lost from the definition.

**Dead: "order is pure expression".** The difference test shows ranking changes the message.

**Open: the name.** It is a labelling choice, left to you.

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

- Does a fresh agent drafting from the Signals point structure produce a point-first opening without any instruction about order?

### Research Frontiers

- **Ranking conditions against questions.** For example, a condolence that also asks something.
- **Ordering several main points** in documentation.
- **How deep the structure should go** before it costs more than it helps.

### Refinement Triggers

- If a draft from a correctly built point structure still opens with background, then the form alone does not carry order, and materialization needs its own constraint.
- If authors routinely cannot rank requirements without asking, the ranking source order needs a default, such as "the act's question first".

# Finding: harmony across the communication substrate

## Changes from Prior

**Prior path:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-31__the-substrate-concept-that-arranges-the-message/finding.md

**Revision trigger:** user correction, plus new evidence.
- The user rejected "the substrate is missing its *shape*" as either the wrong explanation or the wrong word. They proposed **harmony**: of meaning, of structure, of who is sending to whom, and of every paragraph.
- The user also said the earlier traverses were rushed.
- This inquiry re-read the verbatim Signals substrate and draft instead of the earlier summary. It also read the prior findings in full.

**What's preserved:**
- **The point structure.** The 19-31 finding's ranked need → main point → key points → placed facts is kept as one part of the answer.
- **The diagnosis.** The draft reproduces whatever the substrate gives it (18-38).

**What's changed:**
- "Shape" is dropped, both as a word and as the whole explanation.
- The point structure is no longer "*the* missing concept". It is **one line of harmony**: need → meaning → order.
- In the actual Signals material, 11 of the 13 misfits found lie outside it.

**What's new:**
- **Harmony as the missing property.**
- **The frame:** a stated centre that everything else must fit.
- **Derivation:** forming the other elements from the frame.
- **Three grounded checks:** traces, misfits, and the implied-frame diagnostic.
- **Two output channels** for AI drafting.
- **A real-reception test** on the Duncan thread.

**MUST/COULD drift:**
- **Prior MUST:** "Rebuild the Signals substrate as a point structure … have a fresh agent, given only that substrate, draft the message. Run the fresh-reader check."
- **New MUST:** "Write the Signals frame and point structure in the message's own frame, with traces and an author edit list; have a fresh agent draft from it; run traces, misfits and the implied-frame diagnostic."
- **Rationale:** the test now covers all levels of harmony, not only order.

## Question

The previous answer said the substrate (the structured account of what a message must convey, built before drafting) was missing its "shape". The user replied:

> when you say it's missing the shape I don't think that's like correct explanation or maybe it's a bad choice of um, word … it is missing I guess something like a harmony of uh, meaning but … not only for harmony there is a harmony of the structure there is a harmony of um, like other other concepts inside the substrate yeah for example harmony of who's sending to this message this communication to who um, and the all uh, paragraphs they should also have harmony with it too

**Goal:** a conceptual account of what the substrate is missing, framed as harmony, or as a corrected concept if harmony failed testing. It should cover:
- what harmony is, and what it is between;
- what kind of thing it is;
- how the point structure relates to it;
- how it is formed;
- how it is checked without vouching ("vouching" means asking a model to judge "is it good?" rather than comparing quoted things);
- how paragraphs are held in harmony;
- what the Signals case shows.

The user also asked for the traverse to be run at full depth, without shortcuts.

## Finding Summary

### What is missing, and what Signals showed

- **"Harmony" is the right word. "Shape" was wrong both as a word and in substance.**
  - Re-reading the actual Signals substrate and draft turned up **13 places where two parts don't fit each other**.
  - Order (the opening) is only one of them.
- **The other twelve misfits found in Signals:**
  - **Two addressees.** "…still need your decision" is a note to the author, sitting inside a message to the boss.
  - **Unsteady speaker.** "We" and "I" alternate, and "we" is never identified.
  - **Wrong-frame pronoun.** "[your chosen prioritisation approach]" means the author, but reads as the boss.
  - **The sender's own role hidden.** They are the proposed coordinator, but the message never says so.
  - **Research narrated against the need.** Two of four paragraphs walk through research, although the need said "without reconstructing the research".
  - **Need, essence and paragraph plan disagree.** Plan paragraph 3 answers no stated requirement.
  - **Register against relationship.** A researcher's register in an operations manager's voice ("direct, practical and precise").
  - **One thing, five names:** feedback, reports, Signals, Signal records, concerns.
  - **Recommendation said twice,** in two different terms.
  - **Trial evidence split** across paragraphs 1 and 3.
  - **Decisions in the sender's voice** that the substrate never contained.
  - **Essence overstates a fact.** "A substantial body of feedback" runs ahead of the fact's own limit.

### What harmony is

- **Harmony is the property that every part of a communication fits one frame, and fits the parts it depends on.**
- **The frame is the missing centre.** It is one steady, sourced statement of:
  - who speaks, and in what role;
  - to whom, and in what role;
  - the relationship between them;
  - the act (proposing, informing, asking…);
  - the need (what state each reader must reach, as defined in the need finding);
  - the medium and moment;
  - who drafted it, if not the speaker;
  - the names used for key things.
- **Signals had the frame's pieces but never one frame.** They were scattered across sections, and they disagreed. The substrate itself was written inside the AI-to-author conversation ("Your boss needs…"), so that conversation's frame leaked into the message.

### How harmony is formed, checked and bounded

- **Formed by derivation from the frame:**
  - register follows from the relationship;
  - what is selected follows from the need;
  - the main point and the order follow from the act and the need's rank (the 19-31 point structure);
  - voice follows from the speaker;
  - what may be stated as decided follows from who has decided.

  The classical name for this fit is **decorum**. Linguistics shows that the situation (who, what for, how) governs the meanings chosen, their form and their arrangement.
- **Checked by three comparisons, never by "is it harmonious?":**
  - **Traces:** every element names the frame line or point it comes from. This catches orphans and unbacked decisions.
  - **Misfits:** two quoted elements that don't fit. Look first at **seams**, where elements from different sources meet. This catches contradictions.
  - **Implied-frame diagnostic:** a fresh reader writes the speaker, addressee, act and ask they infer from the text alone. That is compared line by line with the stated frame. This catches what the recipient actually receives.
- **Misfits can occur at four levels:**
  - the frame itself;
  - the substrate;
  - the text against the substrate;
  - the text against itself (paragraphs, names, voice).
- **Bounded in two ways:**
  - **A declared tension is not a misfit.** The music term is an "accidental": a note outside the key, marked on purpose.
  - **The frame changes only by the author's decision.** The drafter never changes it silently.
- **For AI drafting, two output channels:**
  - the message;
  - a separate edit list for the author.

  Nothing addressed to the author may appear in the message.

### How it was tested

- **Signals, applied literally:** all 13 misfits are caught. Eleven are fully grounded. Two (how much detail, and register) still involve a judgment of degree.
- **Real reception, the Duncan thread:** every complaint in the manager's reply maps onto the frame. This includes "AI-produced material that you likely have not read" and "This should show your judgement", which together are the drafter-vs-speaker split, in the recipient's own words.

### Where it sits in the framework

- **The point structure (19-31)** is one line of harmony.
- **The 15 text peers** (consistency, register, authority…) each check one pair on the text side.
- **The three gaps** (need, meaning, conveyance) each compare two things and **assume a frame they never form**.
- **The 16-16 open question about "holistic residue (overall tone, impression)"** is closed: the overall impression is the sum of the fits.

## Finding

### 1. Why this question

Communication Craft builds a **substrate**, a structured account of what a message must convey, before the text is written.

**The sequence so far:**
- **Signals proposal.** A full substrate still produced a draft whose opening the user found "weird".
- **18-38** traced the opening to the substrate's own order.
- **19-31** concluded that the substrate lacked a "shape": a ranked point structure from which order could be read off.
- **The user then objected.** "Shape" was the wrong explanation or the wrong word. The real missing thing was **harmony**: of meaning, of structure, of who speaks to whom, and of every paragraph.

The user also said the earlier traverses had been rushed. So this inquiry made two changes:
- it went back to the **verbatim** substrate and draft rather than my earlier summary;
- it read the prior findings in full and grounded outside concepts in sources.

That changed the evidence base, and the result.

### 2. What the verbatim Signals material shows

Read pair by pair, the Signals substrate and draft contain 13 places where two elements do not fit each other. Each is a **misfit**: two quotable things that clash.

| # | Misfit | The two things that clash |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | two addressees | draft line "…still need **your** decision" (to the author) vs the message's recipient, the boss |
| 2 | unsteady speaker | "We have one collected week…", "**My** recommendation…", "**I** propose…", "**our** existing arrangements" |
| 3 | pronoun in the wrong frame | "[**your** chosen prioritisation approach]" inside a message to the boss |
| 4 | sender's role hidden | §5 "Who coordinates — I proposed **you**" vs draft "coordinated by [person or role]" |
| 5 | content against need | §1 "without reconstructing the research" vs paragraphs 1 and 3, which narrate the trial |
| 6 | need, essence, plan disagree | §1 lists three things the boss needs; plan paragraph 3 ("what the operation would make visible") answers none of them |
| 7 | act against opening | §6 act: "approval for a defined starting arrangement"; sentence 1 reports data |
| 8 | register against relationship | §7 "direct, practical and precise" vs "the available work and result evidence", "an unresolved recovery question connected to existing work" |
| 9 | one thing, five names | feedback / reports / Signals / Signal records / concerns |
| 10 | recommendation twice | "a regular part of AI Operations" (end of P1) vs "I propose a weekly review" (start of P2) |
| 11 | one topic in two places | trial evidence in P1 ("Following three reports…") and P3 ("The trial also found…") |
| 12 | decisions not in the substrate | P2 "Straightforward responses … would follow our existing working arrangements … we would involve the relevant owner" (not in §5's choices) |
| 13 | essence overstates a fact | "a substantial body of feedback" vs "establishes the available starting material … does not mean all reports have been fully investigated" |

The 18-38 and 19-31 findings saw only item 7. **The user's sense that something broader was missing is borne out by the text itself.**

### 3. What harmony is

**Harmony is the property that every part of a communication fits one frame, and fits the parts it depends on.**

The misfits fall into two groups:
- **Items 1–5, 7 and 8:** some element does not fit *who is speaking, to whom, doing what, for what*.
- **Items 6 and 9–13:** elements do not fit *each other* inside that frame.

So harmony has a **centre** and **neighbourhoods**.

**The centre is the frame.** It is one steady, sourced statement:

| Line | Signals example | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| Speaker and role | the user, operations manager in AI Operations | §7 "yours as an operations manager" |
| Recipient(s) and role | the boss, who decides support and priority | the dialogue; §1 |
| Relationship | reports to a manager who has asked for distilled judgment | the manager's own words (Duncan thread) |
| Act | propose a recurring review; ask approval for a bounded start | §6 |
| Need | the boss can assess the recommendation without reconstructing the research | §1, using the need finding's definition |
| Medium and moment | written message, under 500 words | §7 |
| Drafter | an AI, for the author's review | the dialogue |
| Names | one name for each key thing ("Signals" = …) | settled by the author |

**This is not new material.** These lines are drawn from four places:
- facets 1, 2, 4, 5, 9 and 12 of the substrate foundation (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_22-09__communication-substrate-foundation-and-dimensions/finding.md);
- the need finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_16-18__the-need-what-a-communication-must-achieve-and-for-whom/finding.md);
- the several-parties finding's opening block (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_16-28__several-parties-holders-layering-and-constraints/finding.md);
- a defined-terms list, as in legal drafting.

What is new is that they are **selected and stated once**, as the reference everything else must fit.

**Signals had the pieces but never one frame:**
- §1 was addressed to the *author* ("Your boss needs…");
- the act sat in §6;
- the voice sat in §7;
- the drafter was nowhere;
- the substrate was written inside the AI-to-author conversation.

That is how the AI-to-author frame leaked into the message (items 1 and 3).

**The established names.** Classical rhetoric calls this fit **decorum**: words and subject "aptly fit to each other, to the circumstances and occasion, the audience, and the speaker". Cicero treated it as "a constant process of attuning and adjusting", not a fixed rule. Bitzer calls a good message "a fitting response" to its situation.

"Harmony" is kept for three reasons:
- it is the user's word;
- it covers all seven kinds of fit found below;
- its musical sense, notes fitting a *key*, matches the structure of a frame and the elements in it.

### 4. What harmony is between: four levels

| Level | What must fit | Signals items | Where the fix goes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The frame itself** | one speaker, one addressee, one act, one need; the drafter kept apart | 1, 2, 3 | substrate (author) |
| **The substrate** | need, main point, key points, facts, register, ask and standing fit the frame and each other | 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 13 | substrate (usually asked of the author) |
| **The text against the substrate** | every paragraph carries a key point, in the frame's voice, with the substrate's standing | 5, 7, 12 | rewrite |
| **The text against itself** | one name per thing, one topic in one place, one voice, no repeated point | 9, 10, 11 | rewrite |

**Kinds of misfit.** All seven senses of "harmony" occur in Signals:
- **decorum:** voice and register fit the relationship;
- **coherence:** meaning hangs together;
- **cohesion and consistency:** one name per thing;
- **alignment:** each part serves the point;
- **proportion:** weight follows importance;
- **frame leak:** two communications mixed;
- **standing:** decided things appear as decided only if they were decided.

That is why one word felt right to the user.

### 5. How harmony is formed: derivation from the frame

Each other element is formed *from* the frame, not separately:

| Element | Derived from | Prior support |
|---|---|---|
| register | relationship line + the need's conditions | 16-18: register is "partly need-driven, partly relationship-driven" |
| what is selected | the need | 16-18 |
| main point and order | the act + the need's ranked requirements | 19-31 point structure; Hasan (below) |
| voice | the speaker line | (new) |
| what may be stated as decided | who has decided (standing) | 16-16 meaning-unit finding |
| placement for several readers | each holder's stopping point | 16-28 |

**This is how linguistics describes it.** Halliday's field, tenor and mode (what is happening, who is involved, through what channel) "determine the range within which meanings are selected and the forms which are used for their expression". Hasan's *contextual configuration* determines "the occurrence and arrangement" of a text's structural elements.

**So order, the subject of 18-38 and 19-31, is downstream of the frame.** That is why the point structure is one line of harmony and not the whole of it.

### 6. How harmony is checked without vouching

Alexander's design theory gives the method. Good fit is hard to define positively, so design proceeds by finding and removing **misfits**. Every felt wrongness traces to a specific misfit.

In both real cases, every part of "weird" and "difficult to engage with" traced to a quotable pair. So harmony is checked by **three comparisons of written things**:

1. **Traces (positive fit).**
   - Every element of the substrate and every paragraph names the frame line or key point it comes from, with its standing.
   - An element with no trace is either an orphan or an unbacked decision.
   - This extends the formation finding's "select" (trace each answer to a requirement) and "ground" (attach its standing) to every element.
   - **Catches:** item 6 (plan paragraph 3 answers no requirement) and item 12 (decisions with no source). Misfit pairs alone would miss them, because an orphan has no partner to clash with.
2. **Misfits (contradiction).**
   - Each misfit is recorded as "expected X from line L, found Y at Q", with its level, kind and fix route.
   - **Pass order:**
     - first the **seams**, where elements from different origins meet: the author's words, gathered sources, AI-produced content, an earlier proposal. In Signals, items 4, 6, 12 and 13 sat on seams;
     - then the pairs that depend on each other (need → main point → key points → facts);
     - then continuity in the text (names, one topic, one voice).
   - A disharmony that shows only in aggregate is recorded as a **pattern misfit**: one frame quote plus two or more text quotes showing the same drift. Item 8, register, is one.
3. **The implied-frame diagnostic (what the reader receives).**
   - A fresh reader, given only the text, writes down the speaker, the addressee, the act, the main point and what is wanted.
   - This is compared line by line with the stated frame.
   - It extends the need finding's "implied state versus sourced state" comparison to the whole frame.
   - On Signals, it would show an ambiguous addressee, a "we/I" speaker and a "report" act within four lines.

"No misfit found" is always reported together with what was compared. It is never reported as "harmonious".

**Fix routing follows the materialization-vs-content finding** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_13-08__materialization-check-vs-content-check-in-communication-craft/finding.md):
- frame and substrate misfits change the meaning, and usually go to the author;
- text misfits against a settled substrate are fixed by rewriting.

**Where judgment remains.** Two kinds of misfit still need a judgment of degree:
- **how much detail** counts as "reconstructing the research" (item 5);
- **how far register may drift** (item 8).

The pair is quotable, but the threshold is not. These are flagged as judgment-bearing in the record.

### 7. How paragraphs are held in harmony

Paragraphs are held in harmony at the last two levels:
- **Against the substrate:** each paragraph traces to one key point, in the speaker's voice, stating as decided only what the substrate has decided.
- **Against each other:**
  - one name per thing;
  - one topic in one place;
  - one voice;
  - the main point stated once.

Most of these reuse existing text checks from the peer taxonomy (compare/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-05_18-49__peer-categories-of-explicitness-in-communication-problems/finding.md):
- consistency;
- register;
- authority;
- economy.

The new part is that each check now compares against a *stated* frame and substrate, rather than against an assumed one.

### 8. AI drafting: two channels and a voice rule

An AI drafter is in two communications at once:
- **AI → author:** the working conversation;
- **author → recipient:** the message.

Two rules keep them apart:
- **Two output channels.** The message, plus a separate edit list for the author (choices to confirm, placeholders, notes). A placeholder in the message marks a location only, such as "[allowance]". It never addresses anyone.
- **A voice rule.** Every sentence must be sayable by the frame's speaker to the frame's recipient. Every commitment in it must exist in the substrate with standing.

Together these make items 1, 2, 3 and 12 impossible by construction.

The substrate itself must be **written in the message's frame**: "I, as operations manager, am writing to my manager to propose…". It must not be written inside the drafting conversation ("Your boss needs…").

**The drafting conversation is never a source for the message's frame.** This rule came from testing the opposite idea, an inferred frame. That idea was killed because an inferred frame is exactly what produced item 1.

### 9. The real-reception test: the Duncan thread

Signals has no recipient reaction, only the author's. The September model-evaluation message has a real one: the manager's verbatim reply (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-05_23-03__highest-value-next-communication-check/development-packet/sources/duncan-thread.md). Every complaint maps onto the frame:

| The manager's words | Harmony reading |
|---|---|
| "Your slack message describes what each document covers which tells the reader very little" | act against need: the message *describes documents*; the need was to say what is understood, concluded and achieved |
| "AI-produced material that you likely have not read through start to finish yourself" | drafter against speaker: the drafted material speaks; the sender does not |
| "This should show your judgement" | the speaker line: the sender must be present as the one who concluded |
| "any other stakeholder who needs briefed on this project, which remember is partly this message's purpose" | a second holder reached *through* the manager: the several-parties "survive being forwarded" condition |
| His five-part structure (where we are → learned → put in place → next → need from you) | order derived from the relationship and the act |
| "difficult for a person to engage with" | the sum of the above |

The drafter-vs-speaker line was the newest part of this account, and **here it appears in a real recipient's own words.** This is the strongest external support the account has.

### 10. Where this sits in the framework

- **The point structure (19-31)** is harmony along one line: need → main point → order. It is kept, and narrowed from "the missing concept" to "one part".
- **The 15 peers** each check *the text against one thing*. Harmony is not a sixteenth peer. It is the relation the peers sample pairwise. The new parts (the frame, the substrate-level misfits, traces, the implied frame) sit *before* the text, where no peer looks.
- **The three gaps** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-59__three-fundamental-meaning-gaps-of-communication-craft/finding.md: need, meaning, conveyance) each compare two things.
  - They assume a frame and never form it: the speaker, the drafter and the relationship are in none of them.
  - Signals items 1–3 and the manager's "AI-produced material" are failures none of the three explains as stated.
  - That meets the three-gaps finding's own reopen condition, partially. The result is not a fourth gap. It is a refinement: "adequacy", which that finding defined as the verdict over the three gaps, **becomes harmony**: no misfit among frame, need, meaning and text.
- **The meaning-unit finding's open question** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md) listed "holistic residue (overall tone, impression)". That residue is the sum of the fits, and it is now checkable through misfits.
- **The substrate foundation** said "the relationships between these facets carry much of the meaning" and gave an example chain ("speaks in a role … invite a decision from someone else"). It never asked those relations to *agree*. Harmony supplies that missing half.

### 11. When the frame is needed

The frame is **not** a mandatory form. The substrate foundation rejected "a mandatory full template".
- **Required:** when the drafter is not the sender, when there are several readers, or when the stakes are high.
- **Otherwise:** optional.
- **In an ongoing thread** where every line is already established, the frame is a reference ("same as the thread"). This is the conveyance finding's "supplied" ground.
- **A change of act or recipient mid-thread** is a new frame, and it must be stated.

## Inherited Commitments Re-test

- **Commitment:** the point structure is the missing substrate concept; order is read off it.
  - **Source:** 19-31 finding, summary.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** the point structure catches item 7 and helps with items 10 and 11. Eleven of 13 verbatim misfits lie outside it. It is now one line of harmony (derivation of selection, main point and order).
- **Commitment:** the draft reproduces the substrate's order (diagnosis).
  - **Source:** 18-38 finding.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** generalized. The draft reproduces the substrate's disharmonies of every kind (item 6 need/essence/plan; item 1 frame leak), not only its order.
- **Commitment:** 14 facets; "the relationships between these facets carry much of the meaning"; no mandatory full template.
  - **Source:** 06-22-09, summary, §4, Reasoning.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** the frame is facets 1, 2, 4, 5, 9 and 12, selected and stated. Relations must now also *fit*. The no-template rule is kept by making the frame required only conditionally.
- **Commitment:** need = state per holder → requirements; setter; register partly need-driven, partly relationship-driven; implied versus sourced state.
  - **Source:** 16-18.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:**
    - the frame imports the need unchanged;
    - register derivation uses 16-18's statement;
    - the implied-frame diagnostic extends 16-18's implied-state diagnostic and keeps its quoted form.
- **Commitment:** placement by stopping point; facts in a shared core must match across split versions; register conflicts split.
  - **Source:** 16-28.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** the Duncan thread's "stakeholder who needs briefed" is 16-28's forward condition. Matching shared-core facts is harmony across versions (text against text).
- **Commitment:** unit = point + force + limits; standing with quotable entry conditions; the main point "does not have to be declared"; holistic residue as a frontier.
  - **Source:** 16-16.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - standing drives the voice rule and traces (item 12);
    - the "need not be declared" part was already corrected in 19-31;
    - the residue frontier is closed by harmony.
- **Commitment:** two kinds of check (materialization: rewrite; content: change the meaning), each declaring its kind.
  - **Source:** 13-08.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** misfit routing follows it exactly. Substrate misfits are content (asked); text misfits are materialization (rewritten).
- **Commitment:** 15 peers, one property of the text against one referent; three families.
  - **Source:** the peer-taxonomy finding.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - harmony is not a sixteenth peer;
    - consistency, register, authority and economy are pairwise slices of it on the text side;
    - register gains a declared reference (the relationship line), which may improve how groundable it is (untested).
- **Commitment:** adequacy is the verdict over the three gaps, not a fourth gap.
  - **Source:** three-gaps finding, summary and reasoning.
  - **Status:** RE-TESTED. Confirmed, but frame revised. The reopen trigger fires partially.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Signals items 1–3 and the manager's "AI-produced material" are frame failures outside need, meaning and conveyance as defined;
    - not a fourth gap;
    - adequacy is refined into harmony: no misfit among frame, need, meaning and text.

## Next Actions

### MUST

- **What:** Redraft Signals in harmony.
  - Write the Signals frame (section 3's table), with the names line and the coordinator choice settled by you.
  - Put the point structure under it, with traces and an author edit list.
  - Write all of it in the message's own frame.
  - Have a fresh agent draft from that substrate only.
  - Run the three checks (traces, misfits seams-first, implied-frame diagnostic) on the result.
  - **Who:** this session, with you settling the frame lines.
  - **Gate:** your go-ahead.
  - **Why:** the account is tested by applying it to existing text, not by drafting. A recurrence of any of the 13 misfits shows which part of harmony the substrate still fails to carry.

### COULD

- **What:** Write a one-page harmony reference: the frame lines with sources, the derivation table, the three checks, the misfit record form, and the two drafting channels.
  - **Who:** Communication Craft reference (the substrate brief).
  - **Gate:** after the MUST redraft.
  - **Why:** gives every AI-drafted message the frame and the checks.
  - **Depends-on:** MUST "Redraft Signals in harmony". This COULD is GATED.
- **What:** Apply the frame and the three checks to a real condolence or apology, and to one message that was split for different readers.
  - **Who:** a test inquiry.
  - **Gate:** the next such message available.
  - **Why:** the robustness claim beyond decision messages rests on constructed cases.
  - **Depends-on:** MUST "Redraft Signals in harmony". OVERRIDE: adoption-ready. Reason: these tests use only the frame and the checks as defined here. They do not depend on the Signals result.
- **What:** Choose the name of the centre: "frame" (plain, but overloaded) or "key" (continues "harmony"; precise).
  - **Who:** you.
  - **Gate:** before the reference is written.

### DEFERRED

- **What:** A sourced standing frame per relationship (speaker, relationship and conventions lines reused for the same manager), every line quoted with source and date.
  - **Gate:** after three messages to the same recipient have used a stated frame.
  - **Why (if revived):** lowers the frame's cost. It is killed if any line is unsourced, because that would be a reader profile.
- **What:** Fix misfits with the existing explicitness fix architecture (decide with a quoted basis or ask; word; apply literally; check independently).
  - **Gate:** after the misfit pass has run on at least three real drafts.
- **What:** Test whether the act line predicts required elements (Hasan's generic structure). For example, a proposal needs an ask and a cost; a condolence needs an acknowledgement.
  - **Gate:** during the six-genre need test already pending from 16-18.

## Reasoning

**Why harmony and not "shape".**
- The verbatim re-read is decisive: 11 of 13 misfits have nothing to do with arrangement.
- Their mechanisms differ:
  - a leaked frame;
  - a need ignored;
  - decisions without standing;
  - unsteady naming.

  None of these is polish on the point structure.

**Why a frame (a centre), and not purely mutual fit.**
- The strongest counter-argument was that decorum is mutual, with no element governing, and that content can force the frame to change.
- **It was partly upheld:** the frame is revisable, but only by the author's decision.
- **It was partly rejected:**
  - Halliday and Hasan show that the situation governs selection, form and arrangement;
  - a centre makes checking tractable (eight frame checks plus dependency pairs, instead of 45 pairs);
  - 7 of 13 Signals misfits are failures to fit the frame specifically.
- The remaining misfits (names, repetition, a split topic) are covered by continuity checks that do not need the frame.

**Why three checks and not one.** Each catches what the others miss:
- **traces** catch orphans, which have no partner to clash with (item 6);
- **misfits** catch traced-but-clashing elements (item 13);
- **the implied frame** catches what the reader receives, before anyone opens the substrate (item 1), and is the only check that explains the manager's "AI-produced material".

**Killed:**
- **"Shape" or point structure as the whole answer:** 11 of 13 misfits lie outside it.
- **An impression-only harmony check:** unquotable, and the kind of judgment models give fluently and wrongly (16-18).
- **The frame inferred rather than stated:** inference from the drafting conversation produced item 1. Seed kept: the drafting conversation is never a source for the message's frame.
- **Harmony superseding the point structure:** order still needs it; the relationship is "refines".
- **Harmony as a sixteenth peer or a fourth gap:** it is not one property against one referent, and it presupposes the gaps rather than standing beside them.
- **An all-pairs checking template:** violates the no-template rule. Replaced by seams first, then dependency pairs, then continuity.

**Refined:**
- **"Every disharmony is a quotable pair"** gained pattern misfits (one frame quote plus two or more text quotes), because register drift exists only in aggregate.
- **"Check the hub and dependency pairs"** became **seams first**: misfits concentrate where independently produced elements meet. This was the inversion result, matched by film continuity, where errors happen between takes shot separately.
- **"Write the substrate as the sender"** (the contrarian idea of having no frame component, and letting one voice prevent leaks) was kept as a rule. The stated frame stays, because the relationship and the act still need sources.
- **The standing frame per relationship** is allowed only if every line is sourced.

**External grounding:**
- the Signals substrate and draft, read verbatim;
- the manager's actual reply (downstream-consumer behaviour);
- sourced descriptions of decorum (Silva Rhetoricae; Aeon), Bitzer's rhetorical situation, Halliday's field/tenor/mode, Hasan's contextual configuration, de Beaugrande and Dressler's standards of textuality, and Alexander's goodness of fit.

**Analogies used only to explain, not as evidence:**
- **music:** key signature and accidentals;
- **film:** continuity supervision;
- **software:** type checking ("expected X, found Y").

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

- In the Signals redraft, which of the 13 misfits recur despite the frame? Any recurrence points to a part of harmony the substrate does not yet carry.
- Across the next AI-drafted messages: is the frame leak (drafter's frame in the message) the most common misfit, as expected?

### Research Frontiers

- **Grounding degree.** How much detail is "reconstructing the research"? How far may register drift? Can the threshold be anchored in a quotable phrase from the need or the relationship, instead of taste?
- **Harmony across versions** (split messages, summaries, forwards), beyond 16-28's "facts must match".
- **Live and spoken exchanges**, where the frame shifts turn by turn.

### Refinement Triggers

- **Reopen "the frame governs":** if, in two real cases, content repeatedly forces the frame to change after drafting, the frame should be formed later, or jointly with the content.
- **Reopen "every disharmony is a quotable pair":** if a real recipient's complaint cannot be traced to any pair or pattern record.
- **Reopen "harmony is not a fourth gap":** if frame failures turn out to need their own formation moves beyond stating the frame, for example negotiating the relationship.

## Correction note (added 2026-10-09)

*Added by the inquiry devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_20-02__naming-and-defining-what-harmony-points-at/finding.md. The text above is unchanged.*

**What was wrong.** This finding says: "Signals, applied literally: all 13 misfits are caught." That check tested the method against this finding's own list of misfits, not against the text, so it was circular.

**The corrected count.** When the verbatim Signals substrate and draft were re-read item by item, the 13 items fell into three groups:
- **Real misfits in the message (5):**
  - items 7, 8 and 12;
  - items 5 and 9, partly.
- **A different problem (2):** items 1 and 3. These are the AI's notes to the author that ended up in the same block as the message ("two outputs mixed"), not misfits inside it.
- **Not misfits once the whole text is read (6):**
  - item 2, "we"/"I" (normal);
  - item 4, an open choice correctly left open;
  - item 6, plan paragraph 3, which does serve the need;
  - item 10, a general recommendation followed by its specific form;
  - item 11, two trial findings for two points;
  - item 13, "substantial", which is accurate.

**What changed in the concept.** The concept is refined, not superseded. "Every part fits one frame and its neighbours" becomes "every part is right as it stands in one communication", in three regards:
- **owned:** presented as whose it really is, how firmly, on what basis;
- **apt:** right for these recipients, this act, need and view;
- **agreeing:** consistent with the other parts.

The frame, traces, misfit pairs and implied-frame diagnostic survive as the way it is checked. See the 20-02 finding for the corrected account and the proposed names.

**Further correction (added 2026-10-09).** The 20-02 account referred to above is itself corrected by devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_21-40__kinds-of-harmony-and-a-better-word/finding.md. Harmony is now "the different parts of a communication belong together as one whole", in four kinds: through-line, skin, who-to-whom, due weight. This finding's claim that "adequacy … becomes harmony" is withdrawn, because adequacy needs fit and fidelity too. Its frame mixed lines that belong to fit, fidelity and harmony.

**Further note (added 2026-10-10).** This finding's four levels ("The frame itself / The substrate / The text against the substrate / The text against itself") return, re-described in devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_07-57__content-and-substrate-harmony/finding.md:
- the frame and the substrate form the account level;
- the text against the substrate is the match between text and substrate;
- the text against itself is the text level.

Its definition of harmony stays corrected.
