# Finding: what "harmony" points at, and a better name for it

## Changes from Prior

**Prior path:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-42__harmony-across-the-communication-substrate/finding.md

**Revision trigger:**
- **User correction.** You said "harmony" is too generic to check against, asked for a more accurate concept and a defining name of two or three words, and asked for the check to never be "is it harmonious?".
- **New evidence.** When the prior finding's own misfits were re-tested against the verbatim texts, most of them did not hold.

**What's preserved:**
- The property is about every part of a communication fitting one communication.
- The method's pieces survive in new roles:
  - **the frame** (the statement of who speaks to whom, for what);
  - **traces** (each part named back to its source);
  - **misfit pairs** (two quoted parts that clash);
  - **the implied-frame diagnostic** (a fresh reader writes down who a message is from, to whom and for what, and this is compared with the intended frame).
- Ownership stays inside the concept.

**What's changed:**
- **The evidence.** Of the prior finding's 13 Signals misfits:
  - 5 are real in the message, 2 of them only partly;
  - 2 are a different problem (two outputs mixed in one block);
  - 6 are not misfits once the whole text is read.
- **The verification.** The prior claim "all 13 misfits are caught" was checked against my own list, not against the text. It was circular.
- **The definition.** Seven kinds of misfit around a frame become **three regards**: owned, apt, agreeing.
- **A rename.** "Fitted" becomes **"apt"**, because "fit" is already a family name in this project.
- **Ownership** now explicitly includes the **basis** of a claim (on what grounds it is held), not only whose it is.

**What's new:**
- the definition sentence;
- three written check questions, each compared with its own reference;
- "stray part" as the term for a finding;
- a held-out test on nine real cases this inquiry did not use;
- three candidate names, with trade-offs.

**Migration:**
- A dated correction note is appended to the prior finding.
- The memory note that repeats the 13-misfit claim is corrected.

**MUST/COULD drift:**
- **Prior MUST, quoted:** "Redraft Signals in harmony. … Write the Signals frame … Have a fresh agent draft from that substrate only. Run the three checks (traces, misfits seams-first, implied-frame diagnostic) on the result."
- **New text:** COULD "Redraft Signals from its substrate written in the message's own frame, then check the redraft with the whole-level diagnostic and the three questions."
- **Rationale:**
  - The checks changed: they are now derived from the three regards.
  - The redraft is now an application, not the validation of this finding.
  - This finding's validation rests on the held-out test already run, and on the human test that is the new MUST.

## Question

**Your question.** You rejected checking by "is it harmonious?":

> "I think it cannot be checked by asking is it harmonious. And that's why we have to understand kind of what is harmony. And then how it applies to different situations. … harmony is actually a generic name. I think we have to find first a more accurate thing of this thing we are dealing with. Um, it is not only for operational view, for other things, other, other views as well. … So it should be a generic name. … a more better defining name and it should maybe two words or three words and give me some variations, two or three variations."

**The goal had three parts:**
1. a precise definition of what "harmony" points at;
2. how it applies across situations and views (conceptual, technical, operational, strategic, evaluative);
3. two or three candidate names, two or three words each, with trade-offs.

A fourth thing was also to be settled: what changes for checking.

**Three readings of "I disagree" were kept open:**
- the three checks I offered are insufficient until the thing is understood;
- "harmony" is too generic to check against;
- my sentence was misheard.

This finding answers all three. See section 1.

## Finding Summary

**The definition**
- **What "harmony" points at:** *every part of a communication is right as it stands in one communication.* "As it stands" means how the part is presented and where it is placed.
- **"Right" has three regards:**
  - **Owned.** The part is presented as whose it really is, how firmly it is really held, and on what basis.
  - **Apt.** The part is right for these recipients, this act, this need and this view. That covers its role, place, weight, register (tone and formality) and who it addresses. The whole message counts as one part too, for its overall length and medium.
  - **Agreeing.** The part is consistent with the other parts, and with what the communication builds on (earlier messages, other versions).
- **"Harmony" stays as the word for what a reader feels** when no part is out of place. The defined name is for the property you can check.

**The check**
- **It never asks "is it harmonious?".** It asks three written questions, each answered in writing and compared with a different reference:
  - **"Is it theirs, as presented?"** Compared with the record of who decided what.
  - **"Is it apt here?"** Compared with the stated situation (the recipients, the act, the need, the view).
  - **"Does it agree?"** Compared with the other parts.
- **A part that fails is a "stray part".**
- **A whole-level written check comes first.** A fresh reader writes who the message is from, to whom, what it is for and what it wants, and quotes any part that didn't belong. This is compared with the intended frame.
- **What earlier failed and what now passes:**
  - The earlier "is it harmonious?" form asked for a verdict.
  - Each new question asks for a written answer that can be compared.
  - Ownership is partly invisible in the text: it needs the record, or the sender. That is part of why the earlier checks felt insufficient.

**The names: three variants**
- **"Three-way congruence"** (recommended umbrella noun).
  - Every part is congruent with its source, its situation, and the rest of the communication.
  - It sits in an established line of thought: Virginia Satir's congruent communication honours self, other and context, and Carl Rogers' congruence means outward communication matching inner experience.
  - It needs a one-line gloss.
- **"Owned, apt, agreeing"** (the definition as a name; it doubles as the check).
  - "Authentic" is an alternative first word if "owned" gets misheard as "has a task owner".
- **"One-voice integrity"** (the ownership-emphasis variant).
  - It suits contexts where AI drafting is the main risk.
  - It hides the most frequent failure (apt), and "voice" can also mean tone.
- **Recommended pairing:** "three-way congruence: owned, apt, agreeing".
- **Rejected:**
  - "communicative congruence": already means one-way authenticity in psychology;
  - "situated coherence": drops ownership and collides with a technical term;
  - "harmony" alone: you asked for better.

**Where it holds**
- **Real texts:**
  - Signals and Duncan's thread (operational);
  - the prior finding's own "all 13 caught" claim (evaluative: it fails "owned", because it claimed a basis it didn't have);
  - the peer-taxonomy finding (conceptual: it passes, because it states that its sources were recalled from memory).
- **Technical and strategic views:** constructed examples only.
- **Acts and uses:** constructed examples cover proposals, report-backs, handoffs, condolences, documentation and instructions to an AI. They cover composing, inspecting, re-expressing someone's work, and asking for another explanation.

**The tests behind it**
- **Literal test.** Applied word for word to the verbatim Signals material and Duncan's thread, the definition catches every real failure. It passes the six items that turned out not to be misfits.
- **Held-out test.** Nine real cases from an earlier inquiry, written before this concept existed:
  - 4 landed inside the concept (2 apt, 2 owned);
  - 5 correctly fell outside it, to existing concepts (missing reasons, vague wording, unsound plans);
  - the kind of check matched that inquiry's own independent labels in all nine.
- **Remaining limit.** The same AI built the concept and classified the cases. A test by you is the next step.

**What changes elsewhere**
- **No new detection tool.** The concept organises existing checks (authority, support, responsiveness, economy, register, order, consistency) around one shared reference: the stated communication.
- **The prior finding is corrected** by an appended, dated note.

## Finding

### 1. Why this question, and what "I disagree" turned out to mean

**How we got here:**
- The Signals proposal draft read as "weird".
- Three inquiries followed. They found:
  - first, an ordering problem (18-38);
  - then a missing "shape" (19-31);
  - then "harmony": every part fitting one stated frame (19-42).
- You then rejected the word "harmony" as too generic. You also rejected the idea that it could be checked by asking "is it harmonious?".

**The three readings of your disagreement, answered:**
- **"The three checks are insufficient until the thing is understood."** Partly right. The checks offered in 19-42 were built on an inflated evidence list (section 2). They now follow from a precise definition (section 3).
- **"'Harmony' is too generic to check against."** Right. The precise concept has three regards, each with its own reference. Nothing is ever checked against "harmony" itself.
- **"Misheard."** Possibly. My earlier sentence said harmony could be checked *without* asking "is it harmonious?", and we agree on that.

### 2. The evidence was re-tested, and most of it did not hold

**How the re-test was done.** I re-read your verbatim Signals substrate and draft, and Duncan's thread, item by item. The prior finding's 13 "misfits" were read against the whole text, not against single quoted lines. Of the 13:

**Real in the message (5):**
- **Item 7:** the opening is a background fact ("We have one collected week of 1,633 Signals.") where the act, asking approval, needs the proposal.
- **Item 8:** a researcher's register ("the available work and result evidence") in a message meant to be "direct, practical and precise".
- **Item 12:** operating rules nobody decided ("Straightforward responses … would follow our existing working arrangements") presented as part of your proposal.
- **Item 5, partly:** research detail out of proportion to a need that said "without reconstructing the research".
- **Item 9, partly:** "Signals", "reports" and "feedback" used for one thing.

**A different problem (2): two outputs mixed in one block.** These are the AI's notes to you, not misfits inside the message. You quoted the message as starting at "We have one collected week…".
- **Item 1:** "…still need your decision".
- **Item 3:** "[your chosen prioritisation approach]".

**Not misfits (6):**
- **Item 2:** "we" for the team and "I" for your own recommendation. This is normal, unless your own voice says otherwise.
- **Item 4:** an open choice ("coordinated by [person or role]") correctly left open.
- **Item 6:** plan paragraph 3. It does serve the need for "what operational response you recommend".
- **Item 10:** a general recommendation followed by its specific form.
- **Item 11:** two different trial findings supporting two different points.
- **Item 13:** "a substantial body of feedback". This is accurate (1,633 reports).

**Why the prior finding was wrong.** Its critique said "all 13 misfits are caught". That tested the method against my own list, not against the text. This kind of circular self-checking is what the critique discipline calls self-reference.

**For you to confirm.** Items 2, 6, 10 and 11 were judged by general writing convention and by reading the substrate. If any reads wrong in your own voice, it counts.

### 3. The definition

**A communication has the property when every part of it is right as it stands in one communication.**

"One communication" means one presented voice, to these recipients, for one purpose (which may involve several coordinated acts), in one situation.
- **The simple test:** is every part addressed to a recipient of this communication by its speaker?
- **Why the AI's note fails it:** it was addressed to you, the author, who is not a recipient of the message.
- **Several authors:** when a document has several real authors, one voice is presented and the other voices are attributed.

"Right" has three regards. Each has its own reference and its own kind of check. 13-08 (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_13-08__materialization-check-vs-content-check-in-communication-craft/finding.md) defines the two kinds:
- **content:** is the meaning right? It needs sources or the author.
- **materialization:** is the meaning well expressed? It is checked from the text against what was intended.

| Regard | What it means | Checked against | Kind of check | Real failures |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Owned** | The part is presented as whose it really is (the speaker's own, or attributed), how firmly it is really held, and on what basis | the record of who decided or settled what. The meaning-unit finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md) calls this a unit's "standing". Without such a record, ask the sender | content (faithful and authorised) | Signals item 12; Duncan's "AI-produced material that you likely have not read" |
| **Apt** | The part is right for these recipients, this act, this need and this view: its role (point, support, background), place, weight, register and address | the stated situation: recipients, act, need, view, relationship, medium | materialization, given the situation | Signals items 7, 5, 8 and 3; Duncan's message parts 1–4; his "stakeholder who needs briefed"; his five-part structure |
| **Agreeing** | The part is consistent with the other parts (one name per thing, no contradiction, each point in one place) and with what the communication builds on | the rest of the text, earlier messages, other versions | materialization | Signals item 9 |

**Owned is not the same as true.**
- A claim can be honestly presented and still wrong. The 19-31 finding's "the missing concept is the point structure" was its real conclusion, later found too narrow. That is a soundness problem, outside this concept.
- A claim can be right and still presented on a basis it doesn't have. The prior finding's "all 13 caught" claimed a literal check that was actually circular. That is an owned failure.

**Where a part came from explains failures; it does not define them.**
- Parts brought in from elsewhere and never made the speaker's own cause most ownership and agreement failures. Examples: the AI's added rules; the trial document's wording; names copied from different sources.
- The speaker's own content can still be in the wrong place. Examples: Signals item 7; Duncan's conclusion buried in his third paragraph.
- So "look at the joins between material of different origin" says **where to look**. It never decides whether a part is right.

**When one failure seems to belong to two regards, ask what would have to change to fix it:**
- the speaker must decide, hold or attribute something → owned;
- the part must be moved, re-weighted, re-voiced or re-addressed → apt;
- another part must change to match → agreeing.

Classifying by reference (standing record, situation, other parts) gives the same placement for every real case.

A part can fail twice. Duncan's document descriptions were apt failures (descriptions where conclusions were needed) and ownership failures (material sent as his account but likely unread). Those are two failures. One failure is never counted twice.

**What lies outside the concept:**
- missing parts (the need's unanswered requirements);
- unclear wording (explicitness, vagueness and the other intelligibility checks);
- untrue content (support);
- unstated reasons (connection).

**Alberti's test of a whole divides the same way.** He said a beautiful whole is one where "nothing may be added, taken away, or altered, but for the worse":
- "taken away" and "altered" belong to this concept;
- "added" belongs to the need.

### 4. How it is checked

1. **Whole-level, written.**
   - A fresh reader writes four lines: who it is from, to whom, what it is for, and what it wants.
   - They quote any part that did not seem to belong.
   - This is compared with the stated situation.
   - Applied to Duncan's original message, this reproduces his real reply ("Tell us what you now understand, what you've concluded…").

2. **Per part, written.** Three questions. Each answer names its evidence, so a bare "yes" is invalid.
   - **"Is it theirs, as presented?"** Who decided or holds this, how firmly, and on what basis? Compare with the standing record, or ask the sender.
   - **"Is it apt here?"** Which slot is this, which recipient and requirement does it serve, in what register? Quote the requirement or situation line.
   - **"Does it agree?"** What else is this called or claimed as?

3. **Which parts get checked.** The places where the real failures occurred:
   - the slots the act creates: the opening, each paragraph's lead, the ask, each reader's part. These come from the 19-31 point structure (main point, key points, ask) and from genre conventions where they can be quoted;
   - every decision or judgment presented as the speaker's;
   - every join between material of different origin;
   - every name of a key thing.

4. **When the situation doesn't say,** for example a voice preference: follow the need finding's sourcing order. That is the request, then standing arrangements, then quotable convention, then asking, then a visible assumption. Convention is a default, never a verdict against the sender's own voice.

**Three of the questions, applied word for word to real parts:**

| Question | Part | Written answer | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| Is it theirs? | "Straightforward responses within AI Operations' remit would follow our existing working arrangements." | Presented as part of your proposal. Not in any substrate section; not among the open choices in §5. | stray part (owned) |
| Is it apt here? | "We have one collected week of 1,633 Signals." | Slot: the opening. The act needs the proposal here (§6). This sentence is background (§3: "establishes the available starting material"). | stray part (apt) |
| Does it agree? | "Signals" | Also called "reports" (§3) and "feedback" (essence; paragraph 2). | stray part (agreeing) |

### 5. How it applies across views, acts and uses

**Views.** Operational, evaluative and conceptual are real; technical and strategic are constructed.

| View | Not owned | Not apt | Not agreeing |
|---|---|---|---|
| conceptual | a definition copied from a source, presented as the author's own position | a definition placed after the parts that depend on it | a term meaning two things in two sections |
| technical | a design rationale invented by the drafter, presented as the team's decision | a strategic argument where the reader asked how it works | component names that change |
| operational | Signals item 12; Duncan's unread material | Signals items 7, 5, 8; Duncan's message | Signals item 9 |
| strategic | a goal stated in the sender's voice that leadership never set | detail that buries the trade-off the decision turns on | value claims that contradict each other |
| evaluative | **real:** the prior finding's "all 13 caught", a basis not actually held; also a conclusion stated more strongly than the analyst holds it | a method narrative where a verdict was needed | a figure that differs between summary and body |

**Acts** (constructed):

| Act | Example failure |
|---|---|
| condolence | Ownership *is* sincerity: template sympathy is "not theirs". A request inside a condolence is not apt. |
| documentation | Intended behaviour presented as tested behaviour is not owned. Parameter names that change don't agree. |
| instructions to an AI | Requirements the requester never set are not owned. Contradictory instructions don't agree. |
| report-back | Results the sender hasn't checked, presented as verified, are not owned. |
| handoff | Constraints invented by the drafter are not owned. |

**Uses** (from the views foundation, devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_10-38__communication-aspect-views-foundation/finding.md):
- **Compose:** the sending-side checks.
- **Inspect someone's message:** the receiving side. List what is presented as the sender's own and ask them about it; reconstruct the situation they wrote for.
- **Re-express a colleague's work:** "owned" means correctly attributed, at the strength they held it.
- **Ask for another explanation:** name the stray part and its regard. Examples: "What did *you* conclude?"; "What does this mean for us operationally?".

**The views foundation said to consider the view, the purpose and the relationship "together".** This concept is that "together", named.

### 6. The tests behind it

**The literal test** (section 2 and section 4's table):
- every real failure caught;
- every non-misfit passed.

**The held-out test.**
- **The cases:** nine real cases from the 13-08 inquiry's table, written before this concept existed. One case from the Duncan thread was excluded because this inquiry had used that thread.

| Case (13-08's words) | 13-08's own label | This concept |
|---|---|---|
| "Revised document described instead of the answer" | materialization | apt |
| "the FreeAgent example" (one case where the process was needed) | materialization | apt |
| "Christine's prompt-level endorsement summarised as "signed off"" | content (faithful) | owned: an attributed claim presented stronger than its source held it |
| "That was the last thing in the way" | both | owned (presented as settled) + outside (whether it is true) |
| "Stages without why that order" | materialization | outside: a missing reason |
| "concerns of any age", "checking what still applies", a 50-word sentence | materialization | outside: vague wording, hard to read |
| "One evaluation explained, not the continuing operation" | content (sound) | outside: the operation wasn't designed yet |
| "Four people for a 15-minute job" | content (sound) | outside: an unsound plan |
| "Who does this instance vs who can run the process" | content (sound) | outside: the operating model |

- **The result:**
  - Nothing was forced, and no fourth regard was needed.
  - The kind of check matched 13-08's independent labels in all nine.
  - Christine's case was predicted by the "at the strength it is really held" clause, which was written *before* the test.
- **The limit:** I classified the cases myself. Agreement on the kind of check is an independent constraint, but agreement on the regard is still my reading.

### 7. The names

**What a good name had to do:**
- name the whole condition;
- be two or three words;
- say which regards it covers;
- work across all views;
- avoid this project's existing terms ("fit", "consistency", "key", "alignment");
- survive a search for outside collisions;
- be explainable in one plain sentence.

The decisive criterion was your own: could a colleague roughly guess its meaning from the name?

| Variant | Name | What it foregrounds | Covers | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Three-way congruence** | the whole, as one noun | all three: congruent with its source, its situation, and the rest | needs its one-line gloss; sounds slightly technical |
| 2 | **Owned, apt, agreeing** (or **Authentic, apt, agreeing**) | the definition itself; it is also the check | all three, explicitly | a list, not a noun. "Owned" is used for "has a responsible owner" in operational talk, and for "owned workers" in this project's orchestration skills. "Authentic" avoids that but can sound like a moral judgment |
| 3 | **One-voice integrity** | the single source: the risk AI drafting raises | owned and agreeing; apt only weakly | hides the most frequent failure, apt (10 of 14 real failures here); "voice" also means tone |

**Why "congruence".**
- It means agreement and compatibility. It is the most exact near-synonym of "harmony", without the aesthetic overtone.
- It already has a three-way form in communication psychology. Virginia Satir's congruent communication honours **self, other and context**, and her incongruent stances each ignore at least one of these. Carl Rogers' congruence is outward communication matching inner experience, which is the "owned" regard.
- "Three-way" counters the narrower one-way meaning that "communicative congruence" already carries in psychology (words matching the speaker's sense of self).

**How this concept differs from Satir's.**
- **Source, not self.** "Source" covers attributed material, not only the self.
- **Situation, not other plus context.** Her "other" and "context" merge into "situation".
- **Internal agreement is its own regard.** In her model it appears as words matching feelings. Here it is consistency among parts.

**Recommended:** "three-way congruence: owned, apt, agreeing". Keep "harmony" as the everyday word for what a reader feels when it holds.

**Rejected names:**
- **"Communicative congruence":** psychology already uses it for one-way authenticity.
- **"Situated coherence":** it drops ownership, which the Christine case and the prior finding's own claim need. It is also a narrower technical term in formal semantics.
- **"Of a piece":** an idiom from cloth meaning "consistent". Opaque, and covers only agreement.
- **"Message unity":** vague.
- **"Decorum":** now reads as etiquette. Its Latin root survives in "apt" (*aptum*, the fitting).

### 8. What changes in the project

- **No new detection tool.** The three regards organise existing checks from the peer taxonomy (compare/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-05_18-49__peer-categories-of-explicitness-in-communication-problems/finding.md):
  - owned gathers authority and support (for strength and basis);
  - apt gathers responsiveness, economy, register and order;
  - agreeing gathers consistency.

  What those checks lacked was one shared reference: the stated communication.
- **The frame from the prior finding stays as the statement of "one communication", with three changes:**
  - a **view** line is added;
  - the **drafter** moves out of the message, onto the output;
  - the **names** line becomes the reference for agreeing.
- **The prior finding is corrected** with a dated note. Precedent: the 13-08 inquiry added a dated note to the 12-40 finding.

## Reasoning

**Why "every part right as it stands", and not "belonging by origin".**
- The first version of this inquiry's reasoning said every misfit was "a part of another origin, never fitted".
- Tested item by item, that explained ownership and naming failures. It failed on Signals item 7 and Duncan's buried conclusion: the speaker's own content in the wrong place.
- So origin was demoted to "where to look".
- "As it stands" replaced "where it is" because ownership is about how a part is *presented* (its standing), not where it is placed.

**Why three regards, not two.**
- Halliday and Hasan define a text as "coherent with respect to the context of situation … and … with respect to itself". That covers apt and agreeing.
- A two-regard model was tested and killed. It misses three things:
  - Signals item 12 (rules nobody decided);
  - Duncan's sharpest complaint ("AI-produced material that you likely have not read");
  - the held-out Christine case (an endorsement presented stronger than given).
- Ownership has a separate reference (who decided what) and a separate kind of check (content).

**Why not four.** No real case, held-out case or view example needed a fourth regard. Continuity across a thread was folded into agreeing.

**Why the earlier evidence was corrected and not defended.**
- The re-read showed six of the thirteen items were not misfits once the whole text is read.
- The prior check was circular.
- Keeping the inflated count would have repeated the failure this finding names: claiming a basis that isn't held.

**Killed, with what each taught:**
- **No boundary rule** between regards. Counting one failure twice is what inflated the prior evidence.
- **Ownership excluded from the concept.** It came back as a content-kind regard.
- **A missing part counts as disharmony.** Missing parts belong to the need.
- **The thread as the unit, with no single communication.** Checks need a unit; each turn is one.
- **A part is a sentence.** Sentences mix parts of different standing.
- **Single-origin definition.** It fails on parts made for the message but placed wrongly.
- **Communicative congruence, situated coherence, of a piece, message unity, decorum** as names: see section 7.
- **Keeping only "harmony", or dropping it.** It is kept as the felt word, never as a check.
- **Superseding the prior finding, or calling the change a refinement only.** Its method survives, so it isn't superseded. Its count was wrong, so the change is a correction.

**Survived, and why it held:**
- **The three regards:** the literal test, the held-out test and the evaluative-view case.
- **The fix-route rule, cross-checked by reference:** both place all 14 real failures identically.
- **The whole-level written check:** on Duncan's message it reproduces his actual reply.
- **"Three-way congruence":** it names the three directions, sits in two established lines of thought, and has no in-project collision.
- **"Owned, apt, agreeing":** it is the definition and the check, with no collision except the mild "owned".

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

- **Human test.** Do your stray-part marks on two or three real messages match the AI's? Revisit after the human-classifier MUST.
- **"Owned".** Is it misheard as "has a task owner" in practice? Observable in the first three uses of the triplet with colleagues.

### Blocked

- **The final name** waits on your choice (MUST above).

### Research Frontiers

- **Grounding judgments of degree.** How much detail counts as "reconstructing the research"? How far may register drift? This is carried from 19-42 and still open: the pair can be quoted, the threshold cannot.
- **Technical and strategic views.** These need real texts.
- **Spoken and live exchanges.**

### Refinement Triggers

- **Reopen the three regards** if a real case passes all three questions and the whole-level check, yet a reader reports a part that doesn't belong.
- **Reopen "owned is a regard of this concept"** if, in your human test, ownership failures are never marked as felt problems and only surface through the record. That would mean ownership is fidelity, not part of the felt property.
- **Reopen the name choice** if colleagues, hearing "three-way congruence" without the gloss, consistently guess something other than the three directions.
- **Reopen the corrected evidence** if you judge any of Signals items 2, 6, 10 or 11 to be a misfit in your own voice. Then update the correction note's numbers.

# Finding: what harmony is, its kinds, and better words for it

## Update (2026-10-10)

**This finding was updated in place on 2026-10-10,** at your request. It now carries the results of devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_07-57__content-and-substrate-harmony/finding.md (called 07-57 below).
- **Where the changes are marked:** every changed passage carries "(updated 2026-10-10)" or "(added 2026-10-10)".
- **The full list of changes:** the refinement note at the end of this file.
- **The version as concluded on 2026-10-09:** kept at `docarchive/finding_before_2026-10-10_update.md` in this inquiry's folder.

**The changes in brief:**
- **A fifth kind, content.** What is said holds together. Only a change of meaning can break it.
- **The through-line is narrowed** to the path of the telling.
- **There are two levels.**
  - The substrate is where content and who-to-whom are decided.
  - The text realises all five kinds.
  - The text must match its substrate.
- **The repair rule.** The kind names what must change; the level names who decides.
- **The choice of unit** now reads five kinds or six.

## Question

**Your message, verbatim:**

> "u are mixing multiopel things and at the same time narrowing the scope which shoudl be the gneric scope. The harmony is not something that's just regarding this mm, examples we had, yeah. There are different parts of the harmony, different uh, variations of the harmony, let's say. And for example, one harmony is one of them is just the do paragraphs or sequential of the text contributes to contributes toward one meaning. This file harmony, for example, it checks the connections and then where this communication um, what this communication is trying to establish yeah it checks these things other harmony is are uh, are the meanings told they are in harmony with each other or not but actually it is different maybe phrasings more you know the skin of the communication is in harmony or not I guess I, can, I think that's correct and maybe and maybe there's a third one I'm not sure but there are these two types of harmony and first we should understand that okay what else exists and what what do we mean by harmony what's a better word for it because it's a generic word and I don't like it"

**The goal.** A general account of harmony in communication, covering:
- a definition;
- a criterion for what counts as a kind of it;
- a map of the kinds: your two, tested, plus any others found;
- what harmony is not;
- better words, for the whole and for each kind;
- how all this relates to the earlier model and to the project's existing checks.

It had to work for every kind of communication, not only the examples we had.

## Finding Summary

**What harmony means**
- **Harmony means the different parts of a communication belong together as one whole.** No part pulls away from the whole, and no part pulls against another part.
- **The word's own root says this.** The Greek *harmonia* meant a "means of joining", as in the joints between ship-planks.
- **The test is belonging, not sameness.** Parts can differ and still belong. A check for sameness would flag good writing, such as a deliberate joke in a sad letter.
- **Harmony compares a communication only with itself.** It needs no outside record, no request and no brief.

**Your critique held, item by item**
- **"Owned"** (whose a part is, how firmly it is held) was not harmony. It is fidelity: whether the text is true and authorised.
- **"Apt" mixed five things:**
  - the line of meaning (since 2026-10-10 this is content: does the part bear on the point?);
  - emphasis;
  - surface steadiness;
  - who is addressed;
  - suitability to the situation.
- **"Agreeing" mixed two things:** whether meanings agree, and whether names stay the same.
- **The kinds were narrow** because they were read off the failures our examples happened to show.

**The five kinds** (updated 2026-10-10: content added; the through-line narrowed)
- **Content** (added 2026-10-10, from your proposal "harmony of the content"). What is said holds together:
  - the meanings agree;
  - they bear on the point;
  - they stay within their grounds;
  - they establish one thing.

  Only a change of meaning can break it.
- **Through-line** (your first variation; narrowed 2026-10-10 to "the path of the telling"). Each told part follows from the one before. One topic is told in one place. Nothing loops back, and no jump goes unmarked. Only reordering can break it. On 2026-10-09 it also held "establishes one thing", "every part helps" and "no part contradicts another"; those now belong to content.
- **Skin** (your second variation). The surface belongs to one piece: words, names, tone, formality, style and format.
- **Who-to-whom** (the third kind, which you named two turns ago as "harmony of who's sending … to who"). Every part comes from the same speaker, to the same audience, at that audience's level.
- **Due weight** (a fourth kind you did not name, the classical third principle of composition, "emphasis"). The surface carries the meaning as it is:
  - what matters most stands out most;
  - space follows importance;
  - the sound matches how serious and how firm the meaning is.

**How the kinds fit together**
- **Four things stay one, and one thing matches** (updated 2026-10-10; it was three). Content, the path, the skin and who-to-whom each stay one from part to part. The emphasis gives each meaning its due weight.
- **Two kinds belong to the account** (added 2026-10-10): what is said, and who speaks to whom.
- **Three belong to the telling:** its order, its surface, and its emphasis.
- **Each kind is broken by one kind of change** (the change test, added 2026-10-10):
  - content: a change of meaning;
  - the path: reordering;
  - the skin: rewording;
  - who-to-whom: re-addressing;
  - due weight: re-emphasis.
- **Two levels** (added 2026-10-10).
  - **The substrate**, the account written before drafting, is where content and who-to-whom are decided, and where the order, names and rank are planned.
  - **The text** realises all five kinds.
  - **The text must match its substrate:** every part has a home in the account, and nothing contradicts it.
- **Every kind applies at every scale:** sentence, paragraph, whole message, thread.
- **Every kind applies on every channel:** text, slides, speech.

**What harmony is not**
- **Every quality of a communication is an agreement with something:**
  - harmony is agreement **with itself**;
  - fit is agreement **with its situation**;
  - fidelity is agreement **with the record**;
  - intelligibility (clarity) is agreement **with its own intended meaning**.
- **Completeness is the exception.** It concerns whether something is present at all.
- **A message can hang together and still fail every one of these.**

**One rule names every failure once**
- **Write each failure as "this clashes with that".**
- **It is a harmony failure only if "that" can be quoted from the communication itself.**
- **If the other side is outside the message,** the failure belongs to a neighbouring property:
  - the occasion or relationship → fit;
  - the record → fidelity;
  - the reader's ability to get the meaning → intelligibility.
- **If there is nothing on the other side,** something is missing: completeness.
- **Who repairs** (added 2026-10-10). The kind names what must change. The level names who decides:
  - a clash in the substrate goes to the author;
  - a clash only in the text is re-told from the substrate;
  - a mismatch between the two is diagnosed first.

**Real texts from outside the project confirm both kinds you did not name**
- **Due weight.** Microsoft's 2014 job-cuts memo opened "Hello there" and announced 12,500 layoffs in its 11th paragraph.
- **Who-to-whom.** A 2024 journal paper opened its introduction with "Certainly, here is a possible introduction for your topic". It was later retracted.
- **Complaints that belong to neighbours stay outside harmony:**
  - the Comic Sans slides at the Higgs-boson announcement (fit);
  - the UK's "Stay Alert" slogan (clarity).
- **A praised farewell letter that mixes a joke with being fired passes.** That shows belonging, not sameness, is the right test.
- **Content harmony on real texts** (added 2026-10-10):
  - spin in trial abstracts: conclusions that go beyond the paper's own results;
  - United Airlines' public apology against its internal email: two messages that disagree.
- **The UK's guidance of 11 May 2020** ("go to work" alongside "avoid public transport") lands in fit, not content.
- **"Stay Alert" trying to do six things** is now a content failure. Its vagueness stays intelligibility.

**Better words**
- **"Harmony" is accurate but too generic,** as you said. It also invites the "is it harmonious?" check.
- **Three variants for the whole:**
  - **"belonging together"** (recommended: the name is the test);
  - **"unity"** (the classical term);
  - **"well-joined"** (the literal root of "harmony").
- **Names for the kinds:**
  - content (added 2026-10-10; your word, glossed "what is said holds together, not whether it is true");
  - through-line (glossed since 2026-10-10 as "the path of the telling");
  - skin (your word, kept);
  - who-to-whom;
  - due weight.
- **For the text matching its substrate** (added 2026-10-10): "matches its substrate"; vividly, "every part has a home".
- **Names for what you find when a kind breaks:**
  - a stray part (anything that does not belong);
  - content's failure: not named yet (added 2026-10-10);
  - drift (the through-line). Since 2026-10-10, whether it still fits the narrowed line is part of your name test;
  - seams (the skin);
  - a misaddressed part (who-to-whom);
  - wrong weight (due weight).
- **Four proposed names were changed,** because their ordinary dictionary meaning leaks into a neighbouring property:
  - "true weight" suggests truth;
  - "false weight" suggests cheating;
  - "crossed lines" suggests misunderstanding;
  - "every part belongs" suggests "nothing irrelevant".

**One choice is yours**
- **Should the defined concept cover only what was composed, or the whole situation?**
  - **Only what was composed:** five kinds (updated 2026-10-10; it was four). Suiting the situation is the separate property, fit.
  - **The whole situation:** a sixth kind, "suits the situation", joins harmony (updated 2026-10-10; it was the fifth).
  - **Inside the substrate, the choice is now concrete** (added 2026-10-10). Is "the main point answers the need" harmony, or fit?
- **Both are coherent.** Fit is the same belonging-together, at the scale of the situation. The recommendation is the composed unit, because it keeps one kind of comparison inside the concept.

**What changes elsewhere**
- **The earlier claim that "adequacy becomes harmony" is withdrawn.** Adequacy needs fit and fidelity too.
- **The earlier claim of "no new detection tool" is withdrawn.** Six relations have no pattern in the existing catalogues. The largest is whether the skin stays even.
- **Prior findings are corrected, with dated notes.**

## Finding

### 1. Why this question

Communication Craft (this project's work on how messages are formed and checked) builds a **substrate** before any text is written: a structured account of what a message must convey.

**How we got here:**
- A draft of the Signals proposal, built from a full substrate, read as "weird".
- Three inquiries followed:
  - an ordering problem;
  - a missing "point structure";
  - then **harmony**: a word you offered for what was missing.
- **The 20-02 inquiry** tried to define harmony precisely. It proposed three "regards" (owned, apt, agreeing) and the name "three-way congruence".
- **You rejected that model.** It mixed several things, and it narrowed a concept that should be generic.
- **You offered two variations of harmony:**
  - the line of meaning;
  - the skin;
  - and suspected a third.

So this inquiry started over, from what harmony is in general.

### 2. What the earlier model mixed, item by item

Each regard of 20-02 was read word for word and each piece placed in its proper home. "Fit", "fidelity" and "through-line" are explained in sections 3 to 6.

| 20-02 regard | What it contained (verbatim) | Where it belongs now |
|---|---|---|
| Owned | "presented as whose it really is …, how firmly it is really held, and on what basis" | **fidelity**: checked against "the record of who decided or settled what", which the message does not contain |
| Apt | "right for these recipients, this act, this need and this view" | **fit**: suitability to the situation |
| Apt | "its role (point, support, background)" | **content** (updated 2026-10-10; was through-line): does the part bear on the point? |
| Apt | "place", "weight" | **due weight**: prominence and space against importance |
| Apt | "register" | **skin** (does it stay steady?) or **fit** (does it suit the relationship?) |
| Apt | "who it addresses" | **who-to-whom** (does it stay the same?) or **fit** (is it the right audience?) |
| Agreeing | "no contradiction", "each point in one place" | **content**, for "no contradiction"; **through-line**, for "each point in one place" (updated 2026-10-10; both were through-line) |
| Agreeing | "one name per thing" | **skin** |
| Agreeing | "with what the communication builds on (earlier messages, other versions)" | each kind, **applied across a thread** |

**The mixing, in short:**
- Each regard spanned two or more different relations.
- One regard, "owned", needed something outside the message altogether.

**The narrowing came from where the regards started:**
- the regards were read off the failures in one AI-drafted operational message (Signals) and one real reply (from Duncan, a manager);
- they were defined against a "frame" made of that kind of message's parts: speaker, recipient, act, need, view.

### 3. What harmony is

**The definition.** The different parts of a communication belong together as one whole. No part pulls away from the whole, and no part pulls against another part.

**The word's own history.**
- The Greek *harmonia* meant a "means of joining", used of ship-planks.
- *Harmos* meant a joint or a fastening.
- So harmony is about how parts are joined: not pleasantness, and not correctness.

**Belonging, not sameness.**
- Good wholes are made of different parts.
- The philosopher Francis Hutcheson put beauty in "a compound Ratio of Uniformity and Variety".
- The dramatist John Dryden, defending plays that mix comedy and tragedy, wrote that "contraries, when placed near, set off each other".
- **What breaks harmony is a part that does not belong.** Difference does not break it. That is why harmony can never be checked as "consistency" alone. The one exception is names and formats, where variation carries no meaning and should simply not happen.

**The test of belonging.**
- Aristotle's test for a unified plot: the parts are joined so that "if any one of them is displaced or removed, the whole will be disjointed and disturbed. For a thing whose presence or absence makes no visible difference, is not an organic part of the whole."
- Edgar Allan Poe's version, for the short story: "there should be no word written, of which the tendency, direct or indirect, is not to the one pre-established design."

**The criterion: what counts as a kind of harmony.**
- **A kind of harmony compares elements of the same communication with each other:**
  - a part with a part;
  - a part with the whole;
  - how a part is said with what it says.
- **Anything that needs an outside reference is a different property:**
  - the record → fidelity;
  - the request, the relationship, the occasion → fit.
- **Two levels inside the composed communication** (added 2026-10-10).
  - **The account:** the substrate, written before drafting.
  - **The text.**
  - Comparing across the two levels is internal.
  - The need, the sources and the relationship stay outside, even when they are written into the substrate, because they are recorded there, not composed.

**Why this criterion and not a wider one.**
- 20-02's regards mixed comparisons with three different things: the record, the situation, and the other parts. That mixing is what you objected to.
- Readers also feel the two failures differently:
  - a message that **does not hang together** reads as disjointed, stitched or "weird";
  - a message that **does not suit** its reader reads as inappropriate or tone-deaf.
- An email to a company's chief executive written casually from start to finish hangs together and does not suit. An email that is formal except for one slangy paragraph does not hang together.

### 4. The kinds (five since 2026-10-10)

Each kind is defined by what must stay together. The aspects listed are examples, not a checklist.

Since 2026-10-10, each kind is also the one that a particular kind of change can break:
- content: a change of meaning;
- the through-line: reordering;
- the skin: rewording;
- who-to-whom: re-addressing;
- due weight: re-emphasis.

#### 4.0 Content (added 2026-10-10)

- **Holds when** the meanings hold together, whatever order they are told in and whatever words are used:
  - the meanings agree;
  - they bear on the point;
  - they stay within the support the communication itself gives;
  - together they establish one thing.
- **"One thing" can be:**
  - a claim (an argument);
  - a purpose (a manual: help the user do X);
  - an effect (a story).
- **Covers your words:**
  - "Harmony of the. Uh, the content", which "is different from the like phrasing" (2026-10-10);
  - the phrase you started with in this inquiry and then turned away from: "are the meanings told … in harmony with each other".
- **The reader asks when it breaks:** "Wait, which is it? That doesn't follow from what you've shown. What does this have to do with the rest?"
- **What does not break it:**
  - a tension the communication declares, such as "we disagree on X";
  - an apparent clash that a dropped limit explains (restore the limit first).
- **What it is not:**
  - truth (a consistent account can be false);
  - soundness;
  - completeness;
  - fit.
- **What you find:** not named yet.
- **Lineage:**
  - de Beaugrande and Dressler's coherence of the "textual world";
  - classical rhetoric's *inventio*;
  - developmental editing.

Details are in 07-57, sections 2 to 4.

#### 4.1 Through-line (your first variation; narrowed 2026-10-10)

- **Holds when** (updated 2026-10-10): each told part follows from the one before, along the account's structure.
  - One topic is told in one place.
  - Nothing loops back to a topic already closed.
  - No jump goes unmarked.
- **As concluded on 2026-10-09, it held more:** "the communication establishes one thing; every part helps establish it; each part follows from, or connects to, what is around it; and no part contradicts another".
  - Only the telling part of the third clause stays here.
  - The rest is content (4.0).
- **Covers your words** (updated 2026-10-10):
  - "sequential of the text" and "checks the connections" stay here;
  - "contributes toward one meaning" has two faces:
    - the paragraph's meaning bearing on the point is content;
    - the paragraph's place on the path is the through-line.
- **The reader asks when it breaks:** "How does this follow? Why are we back here? I've lost the thread."
- **What you find:** drift. Since 2026-10-10, whether "drift" still fits here, or fits content's off-point parts better, is part of the name test.
- **On watch** (added 2026-10-10). The peer taxonomy (section 8) has an order property: "each meaning comes before what depends on it". The through-line merges into it if five real texts show no path failure that is not also an order failure or a content failure.
- **Lineage:**
  - van Dijk's local and global coherence;
  - Wendell's unity and coherence;
  - the theatre term "through-line".

#### 4.2 Skin (your second variation)

- **Holds when:** the surface (words, names for things, tone, formality, sentence style, person and tense, conventions, format) belongs to one piece. Variation stays within one character of writing. The joins do not show. Names and formats are one per thing.
- **The reader asks when it breaks:** "Why does it suddenly sound different? Are these the same thing?"
- **What you find:** seams.
- **Lineage:**
  - Mailchimp's style guide: "you have the same voice all the time, but your tone changes";
  - the "voice", "word choice", "fluency" and "conventions" traits of the 6+1 writing model.

#### 4.3 Who-to-whom (the third kind)

- **Holds when:** every part is said by the same speaker, to the same audience, at that audience's level.
- **Covers** your earlier words: "harmony of who's sending to this message this communication to who … all uh, paragraphs they should also have harmony with it too".
- **The reader asks when it breaks:** "Who is this for? Who's talking?"
- **What you find:** a misaddressed part, meaning a part from someone else or to someone else.
- **Lineage:** the linguist M.A.K. Halliday's "interpersonal" meaning, "an exchange of moves … between speaker and addressee", which he treats as its own plane beside content.

#### 4.4 Due weight (a fourth kind you did not name)

- **Holds when:**
  - what matters most stands out most;
  - space follows importance;
  - how it sounds matches how serious and how firm the meaning is;
  - headings, lists and signposts match the meaning's structure.
- **The reader asks when it breaks:** "Wait, *that* was the important part? Why so cheerful about this?"
- **What you find:** wrong weight.
- **Lineage:** the classical third principle of composition, alongside unity and coherence. The rhetorician Barrett Wendell called it "mass": placing "the chief parts" where they stand out.

### 5. How the kinds fit together

**Three things stay one, and one thing matches** (as concluded on 2026-10-09; updated below). Every communication is someone saying something to someone in some way, which gives three planes:
- **who** is speaking to **whom**;
- **what** is said;
- **how** it is said.

Each plane must stay one from part to part. That gives who-to-whom, the through-line and the skin. Across the planes, the "how" must carry the "what" at its due weight.

This structure is why there are four kinds and not some other number. Halliday's three kinds of meaning (interpersonal, content, and the text as a message) separate the same three planes.

**Updated 2026-10-10: two kinds in the account, three in the telling.**
- **The "what" plane held two kinds:**
  - what is meant: content;
  - the order it is told in: the through-line.
- **So four things stay one, and one thing matches:**
  - **the account has two kinds:** what is said (content), and who speaks to whom (who-to-whom);
  - **the telling has three:** its order (the through-line), its surface (the skin), and its emphasis (due weight).
- **Each kind of the telling is broken by changing that one thing:** reordering, rewording, or re-emphasis.

**The two levels** (added 2026-10-10).
- **The substrate** is the account written before drafting. There:
  - content and who-to-whom are **decided**;
  - the order, the names and the rank are **planned**.
- **The text** realises all five kinds.
- **The text must match its substrate:**
  - every part of the text has a home in the account;
  - nothing in the text contradicts the account.
- **The repair has two coordinates:**
  - **the kind** names what must change;
  - **the level** names who decides:
    - for a clash in the substrate, the author;
    - for a clash only in the text, it is re-told from the substrate;
    - for a mismatch between the two, a diagnosis comes first.

**The kinds depend on each other in two places** (updated 2026-10-10):
- **Due weight needs content.** You cannot match prominence to importance until the account has a main point and a rank.
- **An even skin can hide broken content.** Fluent text reads as if it holds together. This matters for AI drafting, where a fluent surface comes cheaply.

**Every kind applies at every scale and on every channel:**
- **Across a thread:** a follow-up that contradicts the last message breaks content (updated 2026-10-10; was the through-line).
- **Across a series:** weekly reports that rename the same measure break the skin.
- **Across channels:** a slide that stresses one thing while the speaker stresses another breaks due weight.

### 6. What harmony is not

**Four neighbouring properties are easily confused with harmony.** Each is the communication agreeing with something other than itself:

| Property | The communication agrees with… | The reader asks when it fails | Hangs together, yet fails this one |
|---|---|---|---|
| **fit** | its situation: the request, the relationship, the occasion | "This isn't what I asked. Why are you telling *me* this?" | a coherent, evenly casual email to a chief executive |
| **fidelity** | the record and the sources | "Is that true? Did anyone decide that?" | a well-joined message announcing a decision nobody made |
| **intelligibility** | its own intended meaning (the text says it, one way) | "Which one? What do you mean?" | an evenly vague memo with one point |
| **completeness** | (the exception: concerns presence, not agreement) | "Where's the ask?" | a coherent proposal with no ask |

**Harmony is never a sign of truth.** A well-joined falsehood persuades more easily than a broken one, so fidelity must be checked separately.

**The rule that names each failure once:** write "this clashes with that". It is a harmony failure only if "that" can be quoted from the communication itself.

| Case | What clashes with what | Named as |
|---|---|---|
| a formal letter in which one paragraph turns curt | this paragraph's surface against the rest of the surface | skin |
| an apology that slides from "I'm so sorry" to "The company regrets any inconvenience" | this part's speaker against the letter's speaker | who-to-whom |
| a board memo with one paragraph written for the engineers | this part's audience against the memo's audience | who-to-whom |
| an executive summary saying "on track" over a body showing two missed milestones | one meaning against another | content (updated 2026-10-10; was through-line) |
| "There are three reasons:" followed by two | the signpost against the structure | due weight |
| the author's holiday story inside a recipe | this part against the recipe's point | content (updated 2026-10-10; was through-line). Cut it; there is nothing to connect. |
| an evenly casual email to a chief executive | the manner against the actual relationship | fit |
| a proposal with no ask | nothing on the other side | completeness |

**Two lenses on one failure.** Some failures are also seen through a neighbour's lens. That is still one failure:
- an ask hidden in a closing parenthesis is **due weight** inside the message, and **fit** against the request;
- a contradiction is a **content** failure (updated 2026-10-10; was through-line), and also blocks understanding (**intelligibility**).

**A felt contradiction can be fit** (added 2026-10-10). The UK's guidance of 11 May 2020 said "go to work" and "avoid public transport if possible". The two clash only against many readers' circumstances, which lie outside the text. So it is fit, not content.

### 7. Evidence from real texts outside the project

**Five real texts were read against their published reactions.** None was used to build the model. The classification is this inquiry's, but each is tied to the critics' own words, which were written long before.

| Real text | What readers said (verbatim) | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|
| **Microsoft, Stephen Elop's job-cuts memo** (July 2014) | "started with 'Hello There,' and critics on social media seized on the opening as tone deaf"; "announced 12,500 layoffs in the 11th paragraph"; "stiff with 'appropriate financial envelopes', 'ramp-downs' and 'ecosystems' which, towards the end, casually mentions that thousands of Microsoft jobs are to go" | **due weight**, twice: cheer against grave news, and the news buried. Also **intelligibility** (euphemisms) and **fit** (the manner, for people losing their jobs). |
| **A paper in the journal *Surfaces and Interfaces*** (Elsevier, March 2024; retracted) | its introduction opened: "Certainly, here is a possible introduction for your topic" | **who-to-whom**: a line from an AI to the author, inside a paper addressed to readers. Also **fidelity** (undisclosed authorship). |
| **CERN's Higgs-boson seminar slides in Comic Sans** (July 2012) | "peculiar for a serious scientific talk"; "the equivalent of showing up to the office in flip-flops" | **fit**: the critics compared the typeface with the *occasion*. The surface was even throughout. |
| **The UK's "Stay Alert, Control the Virus, Save Lives"** (May 2020) | "what does it mean to stay alert"; "trying to do a minimum of six things" | **intelligibility** (vague). Also **content** (updated 2026-10-10; was through-line): one slogan trying to do several things. |
| **Groupon founder Andrew Mason's farewell letter** (February 2013) | "Just kidding – I was fired today"; reported as "brutally honest" | **holds**: one line, one speaker and audience, the firing stated in the second sentence. The joke belongs. |

**What the five texts show.**
- **All five land, and none lands nowhere.**
- **Two show the kinds you did not name**, and both are famous cases: due weight in Elop's memo, who-to-whom in the paper.
- **Two complaints belong to neighbours, and stay outside harmony:** Comic Sans (fit) and "Stay Alert" (intelligibility).

**Three more real texts** (added 2026-10-10, from 07-57):

| Real text | Where it lands |
|---|---|
| spin in trial abstracts (Boutron and colleagues, *JAMA* 2010): conclusions claiming a benefit the paper's own results do not support | content (grounds); the stress on subgroup results is due weight |
| United Airlines, April 2017: a public apology against an internal email calling the passenger "disruptive and belligerent" | content, across a series of messages |
| the UK's guidance of 11 May 2020: "go to work" alongside "avoid public transport if possible" | fit, not content |

**Elop and Mason are the decisive pair.**
- Both mix a light tone with grave news. One was condemned and one was praised.
- In Mason's letter the plain truth comes second, and the joke sets it off. In Elop's memo the cheer delays and softens the news until paragraph 11.
- A test of sameness cannot tell the two apart. **Belonging can.**

**The earlier held-out cases, read again.** The nine cases 20-02 used as a held-out test now land as follows:

| Case (20-02's quotation) | Now |
|---|---|
| "Revised document described instead of the answer" | fit |
| "the FreeAgent example" | fit |
| "Christine's prompt-level endorsement summarised as 'signed off'" | fidelity |
| "That was the last thing in the way" | fidelity |
| "Stages without why that order" | intelligibility |
| "concerns of any age", and a 50-word sentence | intelligibility |
| the remaining three | flaws in the content itself (the operation, the plan, the operating model) |

20-02 counted four as "inside" harmony because its definition had fit and fidelity mixed in.

### 8. Where harmony sits in the project

**The pattern catalogues, read in full.**
- There are five catalogues and 76 patterns. A pattern is one named failure with a test.
- Many harmony failures are already caught, but scattered:
  - **who-to-whom:**
    - an instruction meant for another audience (P14);
    - a note to the author left in recipient-facing text (F06);
  - **due weight:**
    - a buried answer (R01);
    - a buried ask (O04);
    - a buried qualification (R09);
    - a conclusion stronger than its support (F09);
  - **content and the path, asked as clarity** (updated 2026-10-10; was the through-line): the catalogue's tag "do the parts form one compatible, understandable account" (N6).
- **Six relations have no pattern at all:**
  1. whether the skin stays even beyond names;
  2. a change of speaker;
  3. a statement pitched at another audience;
  4. tone against the meaning's own gravity;
  5. space and signposts against importance, when there is no request;
  6. a part judged against the communication's own point (since 2026-10-10, this is content harmony's bearing).
- **Most covering patterns compare against something outside the message:** the request, the relationship, the genre or a voice instruction. Harmony needs only the message.
- **So 20-02's "no new detection tool" is wrong.** The gap is smaller than this inquiry first claimed; sensemaking had overstated it, and the catalogue check corrected it.

**The peer taxonomy.**
- The peer taxonomy is in compare/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-05_18-49__peer-categories-of-explicitness-in-communication-problems/finding.md. A "peer" there is one property of the text, checked against one thing.
- **Harmony is not a sixteenth peer.**
- It is a **second question about the text alone**, beside intelligibility:
  - intelligibility asks: can each meaning be read off the text?
  - harmony asks: do the parts belong together?
- The **consistency** peer straddles two kinds: claims belong to content (updated 2026-10-10; was the through-line), names to the skin.
- The **register** peer is fit: does the register suit the relationship? Whether register stays steady across the text belongs to the skin.

**The three gaps of Communication Craft** (need, meaning, conveyance):
- **Harmony is not a fourth gap.** Content and who-to-whom are formed in the meaning; the through-line, the skin and due weight are planned there and realised in the text (updated 2026-10-10).
- **"Adequacy becomes harmony"** (from 19-42) is withdrawn. Adequacy is the verdict over the gaps, and it needs fit and fidelity as well as harmony.

**Earlier findings, re-described:**
- **The point structure** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-31__the-substrate-concept-that-arranges-the-message/finding.md) forms content (one point, with every part bearing on it) and plans the through-line (updated 2026-10-10). The ranking it uses comes from the need, which belongs to fit. Of its three checks on the substrate, "no orphans" is content harmony; the other two are fit.
- **The weird Signals opening** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_18-38__why-a-full-substrate-still-produced-a-weird-opening/finding.md) was a **due-weight** failure: a background fact took the first position while the line made the proposal the main point. The same failure, seen as clarity, is an ordering failure.

### 9. Better words

**Your word "harmony", tested first.**
- It is **accurate**. Its root is joining.
- It is **too generic**: it applies to music, colour, society and relationships.
- It carries a feeling ("pleasing"), which invites the forbidden "is it harmonious?" check.
- In text linguistics it is already a narrow technical term: Ruqaiya Hasan's "cohesive harmony".
- You rejected it twice. So it is replaced as the defined word, and stays only as an everyday word.

**What a good name had to do:**
- name *different parts belonging together*;
- not suggest:
  - sameness ("consistency", "uniformity");
  - correctness ("integrity");
  - suitability ("fit", "aptness", "decorum");
  - completeness ("wholeness");
  - one kind only ("coherence", which means meaning-only in linguistics; "cohesion", which means surface ties);
- avoid this project's existing terms;
- be **never guessed into a neighbour**, checked against dictionary senses.

**For the whole, three variants:**

| Name | What it foregrounds | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|
| **belonging together** (recommended) | the test itself: does this part belong? | Plain and generic, with no collisions; pairs with "stray part". It does not work as an adjective. |
| **unity** | the classical name: Aristotle's unity of action, Poe's unity of effect, Wendell, Hasan's book *Unity in Discourse* | Precise in composition theory, but sounds as generic as "harmony", which was your objection. |
| **well-joined** | the literal root of "harmony"; joints | Vivid, and works as an adjective ("a well-joined message"). It may sound like a craft word. |

**For the kinds:**
- **content** (added 2026-10-10). This is your word. The dictionary gives "the substance or material dealt with … as distinct from its form or style". Gloss it "what is said holds together, not whether it is true", because 13-08's "content check" asks whether the meaning is right.
- **through-line**. It was glossed as "the line of meaning"; since 2026-10-10 it is glossed "the path of the telling", because the line of meaning is now content.
- **skin** (your word, kept: the surface a reader meets first);
- **who-to-whom** (your phrase);
- **due weight**. "Give due weight to" means to give proper importance, which is exactly the sense.

**For what you find when each breaks:**
- **stray part** (the umbrella);
- **drift**;
- **seams**;
- **misaddressed part**;
- **wrong weight**.
- **content's failure:** not named yet (added 2026-10-10).

**For the text matching its substrate** (added 2026-10-10): "matches its substrate"; vividly, "every part has a home".

**Names that were changed, and why.** The dictionary check found each one leaking into a neighbour:

| Changed name | The ordinary sense that leaks | Replacement |
|---|---|---|
| "one line" | a one-line message (length) | through-line |
| "true weight" | truth (fidelity) | due weight |
| "false weight" | dishonest trade weights (Proverbs 11:1) | wrong weight |
| "crossed lines" | close to "get your wires crossed" (misunderstand) and "cross the line" (misbehave) | misaddressed part |
| "every part belongs" | "nothing irrelevant here" (economy, a part of fit) | belonging together |

### 10. The choice left to you

**Should the defined concept attach to what was composed, or to the whole situation?**

**What the two options share.** Both are belonging-together. Fit is that same relation at the scale of the whole situation: the communication belonging with its relationship and occasion.

**Option 1: the composed communication (recommended).**
- Five kinds (updated 2026-10-10; it was four).
- Suiting the situation stays the separate property, fit.
- The concept compares the message only with itself.
- Inside the substrate (added 2026-10-10), "the main point answers the need" is fit, checked beside harmony under its own label.

**Option 2: the whole situation.**
- A sixth kind, "suits the situation" (updated 2026-10-10; it was the fifth).
- The register check joins harmony.
- Harmony then needs the situation as an input.
- Inside the substrate (added 2026-10-10), the need and the relationship become parts that must belong.

**Why option 1 is recommended.** It keeps one kind of comparison inside the concept, and keeping to one kind was the point of your correction. But your own earlier phrase, "paragraphs … in harmony with" who is sending to whom, can be read either way, so the choice is yours.

## Inherited Commitments Re-test

(Note, 2026-10-10: these re-tests stand as concluded on 2026-10-09. 07-57 re-tested this finding's own commitments, including the 13-08 entry below on materialization failures.)

**From 20-02 (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_20-02__naming-and-defining-what-harmony-points-at/finding.md):**

- **Commitment:** harmony = "every part of a communication is right as it stands in one communication".
  - **Source:** 20-02, Finding Summary.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID.
  - **Evidence:**
    - "Right" mixes truth (fidelity), suitability (fit) and consistency (sections 2 and 3).
    - Real texts show that a message can hang together while failing truth or suitability (section 7).
    - The new definition is belonging together.
- **Commitment:** three regards, owned, apt and agreeing.
  - **Source:** 20-02, §3.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID.
  - **Evidence:** the item-by-item mapping in section 2. Owned is fidelity; apt is five things; agreeing is two.
- **Commitment:** the names "three-way congruence", "owned, apt, agreeing", "one-voice integrity".
  - **Source:** 20-02, §7.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID (withdrawn).
  - **Evidence:**
    - They named the dissolved regards.
    - "Congruence" carries Satir's and Rogers' sense of authenticity, which is a fidelity sense.
- **Commitment:** 5 of 19-42's 13 misfits are real; the "13/13" claim was circular.
  - **Source:** 20-02, §2.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Unchanged.
    - Re-sorted, three of the five are harmony: item 7 is due weight, item 9 is skin, item 5 is partly due weight. Item 8 is fit; item 12 is fidelity.
- **Commitment:** never check by asking "is it harmonious?"; checks are written questions answered by quoting.
  - **Source:** 20-02, §4.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** each kind has a written question, and the clash rule requires a quote (section 6).
- **Commitment:** a whole-message written check (from, to whom, for what, wants what), compared with the intended frame.
  - **Source:** 20-02, §4.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - "From" and "to whom" check who-to-whom.
    - "For what" and "wants what" check fit. They are two checks, not one.
- **Commitment:** the held-out test (four of nine cases inside the concept).
  - **Source:** 20-02, §6.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID.
  - **Evidence:** under the new definition none of the nine is harmony (section 7). The four "inside" were fit and fidelity.
- **Commitment:** "No new detection tool. The concept organises existing checks (authority, support, responsiveness, economy, register, order, consistency)".
  - **Source:** 20-02, §8.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID.
  - **Evidence:**
    - All 76 catalogue patterns were read: six harmony relations have no pattern (section 8).
    - Five of the seven checks it named are neighbours, not harmony.
- **Commitment:** a part can fail twice, but one failure is never counted twice.
  - **Source:** 20-02, §3.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** the clash rule makes this mechanical. Each failure is one clash pair, and a second lens (fit, intelligibility) is recorded as the same failure.

**From 19-42 (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-42__harmony-across-the-communication-substrate/finding.md):**

- **Commitment:** harmony = "every part of a communication fits one frame, and fits the parts it depends on".
  - **Source:** 19-42, §3.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID.
  - **Evidence:**
    - The frame's eight lines split across fit, fidelity and harmony:
      - speaker and recipient → who-to-whom;
      - relationship, need and medium → fit;
      - drafter → fidelity, plus who-to-whom;
      - act → through-line, plus fit;
      - names → skin.
    - The frame survives only as a statement written before drafting.
- **Commitment:** seven kinds of misfit (decorum, coherence, cohesion and consistency, alignment, proportion, frame leak, standing).
  - **Source:** 19-42, §4.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** all seven re-sort into the four kinds plus fit and fidelity, with none left over:
    - coherence and alignment → through-line;
    - cohesion → skin;
    - frame leak → who-to-whom;
    - proportion → due weight;
    - decorum → fit;
    - standing → fidelity.
- **Commitment:** "'adequacy' … becomes harmony".
  - **Source:** 19-42, §10. 20-02 confirmed it.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID.
  - **Evidence:** adequacy needs fit and fidelity, which harmony excludes. A message can be harmonious and inadequate (section 6).
- **Commitment:** harmony is not a sixteenth peer, nor a fourth gap.
  - **Source:** 19-42, §10; 20-02.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** harmony is a second question about the text alone, beside intelligibility (section 8).

**From 19-31 (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-31__the-substrate-concept-that-arranges-the-message/finding.md):**

- **Commitment:** the point structure (ranked need → main point → key points → placed facts).
  - **Source:** 19-31, §3.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** it forms the through-line. Its ranking comes from the need, which belongs to fit.

**From 18-38 (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_18-38__why-a-full-substrate-still-produced-a-weird-opening/finding.md):**

- **Commitment:** arrangement is conveyance at the scale of the whole message; the weird opening was copied from the substrate's own order.
  - **Source:** 18-38, §3.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** in the text, the opening was a due-weight failure: prominence against rank. Seen as clarity, it is an ordering failure. Both readings hold.

**From the peer taxonomy (compare/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-05_18-49__peer-categories-of-explicitness-in-communication-problems/finding.md):**

- **Commitment:** 15 peers, each one property of the text checked against one thing, in three families by what they are checked against (the text alone, the sources, the request).
  - **Source:** peer taxonomy, Summary and §4.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Harmony shares intelligibility's reference (the text alone) but asks a different question.
    - The consistency peer straddles the through-line and the skin.
    - Register's steadiness belongs to the skin.
    - The catalogue tag "do the parts form one compatible, understandable account" (N6) already asks the through-line's question as clarity.

**From the foundations 20-02 inherited:**

- **Commitment:** the substrate foundation's map of facets in four groups: situation, contribution, expression, reception.
  - **Source:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_22-09__communication-substrate-foundation-and-dimensions/finding.md, Summary.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Harmony's three planes match its groups: participants → who-to-whom; contribution → through-line; expression → skin.
    - Its view that communication includes its situation is what makes the choice of unit (section 10) real.
- **Commitment:** each unit of meaning has a standing, and an AI never settles its own units.
  - **Source:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md, §5.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** standing still holds, but checking it is fidelity's job, not harmony's. Only its internal shadows land in harmony: the same claim held at different firmness in two places, or a change of speaker.
- **Commitment:** every check declares its kind, either content or materialization (how well the text expresses settled meaning).
  - **Source:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_13-08__materialization-check-vs-content-check-in-communication-craft/finding.md.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Harmony failures in the text are materialization failures.
    - A through-line or who-to-whom that was never formed in the substrate is a content failure.
- **Commitment:** the five views (conceptual, technical, operational, strategic, evaluative).
  - **Source:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_11-56__additional-work-communication-views/finding.md.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** all four kinds occur in all five views, and in five forms outside work (constructed cases). They also occur in real public and scientific texts (section 7).
