## Reasoning

**Killed, and what each kill taught:**
- **Harmony as "right as it stands" (20-02).** Rightness mixes three references: the record, the situation, the other parts. That is the mixing you named. Lesson: define a concept by one relation and one kind of reference.
- **Harmony as fitting one frame (19-42).** The frame's lines belonged to three different properties. Lesson: the frame is a useful statement to write before drafting, never a concept's definition.
- **Harmony mainly as suiting the situation.** This was raised as the strongest counter-argument. It brings the mixing back, and it cannot be checked from the text alone. Lesson: suitability is the same relation at the scale of the situation. That became the choice of unit, not a kind.
- **Only your two kinds, each enriched.** Killed on outside evidence:
  - Elop's memo kept one even corporate voice throughout, so the skin held. It still failed, on due weight.
  - The AI-preamble paper's stray line was on topic, so the through-line held. It still failed, on who-to-whom.
  - Lesson: keep your two kinds first, by name; the other two are what your own earlier words and composition theory add.
- **Kinds sorted by relation** (belonging, agreeing, matching). Each kind would span every plane, mixing meaning and surface. Lesson: those words work *inside* a kind, not as kinds.
- **No umbrella word.** You asked for one, and the four kinds share one test, one reference and one felt sign.
- **"Supersedes" as the relation to 20-02.** It would discard parts of 20-02 that are still valid:
  - the evidence correction;
  - the written-question method;
  - the rule against "is it harmonious?".
- **Names killed:**
  - integrity: read as honesty;
  - wholeness: read as completeness;
  - coherence: meaning-only;
  - cohesion: surface-only;
  - congruence: authenticity;
  - "of a piece": sameness;
  - concord;
  - organic unity;
  - continuity;
  - well-knit;
  - fit and finish: collides with "fit";
  - oneness;
  - Stimmigkeit: not English;
  - the musical and body families: they extend the word you dislike, or collide with "the body of the message".

**Survived, and why each held:**
- **Belonging together, not sameness.** Supported by:
  - the word's root;
  - Aristotle, Poe, Hutcheson and Dryden;
  - the decisive real pair, Elop and Mason.
- **The internal criterion.** It keeps one kind of comparison, and readers feel "disjointed" and "inappropriate" as different complaints. The unit view explains why both readings of your phrase felt right.
- **The four kinds** (refined on 2026-10-10 to five; see 07-57). Each can fail while the other three hold. The kinds:
  - come from the three planes of any communication, plus one relation across them;
  - reach every view and form tested;
  - are borne out in real outside texts.
- **The clash rule with its quoting requirement.** It named every border case once. The Comic Sans case forced the quoting requirement: the other side must be quotable from the message for the failure to be harmony.
- **The new names.** Each passed the dictionary check that it is never guessed into a neighbour. "Skin" stays because it is your word and it fits.

**Where this inquiry corrected itself:**
- **Sensemaking claimed that no peer covered four harmony relations.** The catalogue check showed that the change of audience and most prominence cases are already covered, so the list was corrected to six narrower relations.
- **Four names proposed by innovation leaked into neighbours** under the dictionary check, and were replaced.

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

- **Do your marks match the AI's?** When you mark real messages with the five kinds (updated 2026-10-10), do your marks and the AI's agree? Observable after the marking MUST.
- **Does "through-line" get read as "the main point"?** Since 2026-10-10, that reading would put content back into the line. Observable in the first three uses with colleagues.
- **Does "content harmony" get heard as "the content is right"?** (added 2026-10-10) Observable in the same uses.

### Blocked

- **The number of kinds** (four, five or six; updated 2026-10-10) waits on two things: your choice of unit, and the through-line test.
- **The final names** wait on your test of them without explanations.

### Research Frontiers

- **How far a variation may go before it stops belonging.** Mason's joke belongs; Elop's cheer does not. The pair can be quoted; the threshold cannot yet.
- **Harmony in live spoken exchanges,** where who-to-whom shifts turn by turn.

### Refinement Triggers

- **Reopen the map of kinds** if a real message has a part that readers say does not belong, and the clash rule cannot quote the other side either inside the message (a kind) or outside it (a neighbour).
- **Reopen the internal criterion** if, in your marking, you consistently mark messages that are consistent but unsuitable as "not in harmony". That would mean your concept is the whole-situation unit.
- **Reopen "belonging, not sameness"** if a real case shows a variation that serves the whole, yet readers still report it as jarring.
- **Reopen the umbrella name** if colleagues, hearing it without explanation, consistently guess a neighbouring property: relevance, honesty, completeness or clarity.

## Refinement note (added 2026-10-10)

This finding is refined by devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_07-57__content-and-substrate-harmony/finding.md. Read these changes before using the four kinds.

- **A fifth kind: content.** What is said holds together. The meanings agree, bear on the point, stay within their grounds, and establish one thing. Only a change of meaning can break it.
- **The deferred split is made, and widened.** "The communication establishes one thing", "every part helps establish it" and "no part contradicts another" all move from the through-line to content.
- **The through-line is narrowed** to the path of the telling:
  - each told part follows from the one before;
  - one topic is told in one place;
  - nothing loops back;
  - no jump goes unmarked.

  Whether it stays a kind or merges into the order property is being tested on five real texts.
- **There are two levels.**
  - **The substrate** (the account before any text) is where content and who-to-whom are decided, and where the order, names and rank are planned.
  - **The text** realises all five kinds.
  - **The text must match its substrate.**
- **Cases re-placed in content:**
  - the "on track" summary over missed milestones;
  - the holiday story in a recipe;
  - "Stay Alert" trying to do several things.
- **The repair rule.** The kind names what must change; the level names who decides.
- **The unit choice** now reads: five kinds (the composed communication) or six (the whole situation).

## Alignment note (added 2026-10-10)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_09-00__harmony-as-alignment-aligncraft-layers-in-communication/finding.md places this finding inside a larger frame:
- **Alignment names the family.** In AlignCraft's sense, alignment is agreement between what was meant and what is understood or produced. It is the family 21-40 called "every quality is an agreement with something".
- **Harmony is one member:** the alignment a communication can check against itself. It has two parts:
  - **internal coherence:** content, the path, the skin, who-to-whom;
  - **the telling matching its account:** due weight, and the text matching its substrate.
- **There are two relationships:**
  - drafting: the author and the drafter. The substrate is its alignment artifact.
  - communicating: the sender and the recipient. The text is its instrument.
- **The open choice of unit is dissolved by naming,** pending the user's confirmation:
  - the whole-situation concept is communication alignment;
  - harmony is its composed-unit member.


## Further note (added 2026-10-10)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_11-29__alignment-checks-and-textual-integrity-in-the-commcraft-design/finding.md places harmony's internal kinds differently from the alignment note above.
- **The kinds:** content, the path, the part of the skin that changes what is taken, and who-to-whom.
- **Their new place:** the consistency part of the text's integrity, at the action-set layer.
- **Not:** at AlignCraft's coherence layer, as the alignment note above says.
- **This finding's test "belonging, not sameness"** is now the wording of that consistency rule.
- **"Integrity" as a name still leaks to honesty,** which was this finding's reason for rejecting it. In textual criticism it also means "unaltered from the source".

# [14] Finding: content harmony, and harmony in the substrate

**Source:** `/Users/nsstorm/Desktop/projects/AIOpsMan/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_07-57__content-and-substrate-harmony/finding.md`

status: active

# Finding: content harmony, and harmony in the substrate

## Changes from Prior

**Prior path:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_21-40__kinds-of-harmony-and-a-better-word/finding.md. This is the earlier finding that defined harmony as "the different parts of a communication belong together as one whole" and found four kinds. It is called 21-40 below.

**Revision trigger.** There were two.
- **Your new framing.** You proposed another kind, "harmony of the content", which is different from phrasing. You also called "content's and substrate's harmony" "another version of it".
- **New evidence gathered for this inquiry:**
  - a change test that separates the kinds (which change to a text can break which kind);
  - three real texts from outside the project, each with published reactions;
  - the dictionary sense of "content";
  - the project's existing substrate checks, re-read word for word.

**What's preserved from 21-40:**
- **The definition.** The different parts of a communication belong together as one whole. The test is belonging, not sameness.
- **The criterion.** A kind of harmony compares elements of the same composed communication. Anything that needs an outside reference is a neighbouring property.
- **Three kinds**, unchanged in what they cover: skin, who-to-whom and due weight.
- **The neighbours:** fit, fidelity, intelligibility and completeness.
- **The clash rule.** Write each failure as "this clashes with that". It is a harmony failure only if "that" can be quoted from the communication itself.
- **The rule never to check by asking "is it harmonious?".**
- **The open choice of unit.** It is still yours.

**What's changed:**
- **The through-line is narrowed.**
  - **21-40 defined it as:** "the communication establishes one thing; every part helps establish it; each part follows from, or connects to, what is around it; and no part contradicts another".
  - **It is now** the path of the telling. Everything about *what* is said has moved to the new kind, content.
- **Four kinds become five.**
- **Some of 21-40's cases are re-placed:**
  - an executive summary saying "on track" over a body that shows two missed milestones: now content (21-40 had through-line);
  - the author's holiday story inside a recipe: now content (21-40 had through-line);
  - "Stay Alert … trying to do a minimum of six things": now content (21-40 had through-line). Its vagueness stays intelligibility.
- **21-40's rule about repairs becomes one rule for all five kinds.** 21-40 said: "Harmony failures in the text are materialization failures. A through-line or who-to-whom that was never formed in the substrate is a content failure." A materialization failure is a failure of how well the text expresses the meaning the author settled. Now: the kind names what must change, and the level names who decides (section 8).
- **21-40's summary of the structure changes.** "Three things stay one, and one thing matches" becomes "four things stay one, and one thing matches":
  - **stay one:** content, the path, the skin, who-to-whom;
  - **matches:** due weight.

  Section 5 gives the finer structure: two kinds belong to the account, and three to the telling.
- **The two links between kinds shift:**
  - "Due weight needs the through-line" becomes "due weight needs content": you cannot match emphasis to importance until the account has a main point and a rank;
  - "An even skin can hide a broken through-line" becomes "an even skin can hide broken content".

**What's new:**
- content harmony: its definition, its four aspects and its boundaries;
- the change test: each kind is the one that a particular kind of change can break;
- two levels, the substrate and the text, and the check that the text matches its substrate;
- one repair rule in two parts: the kind names what must change, and the level names who decides;
- the recorded-reference rule. Inside the substrate, the need, the sources and the relationship are recorded, not composed, so comparing with them is fit or fidelity;
- the existing substrate checks, sorted by property, which shows a gap: no check compares one meaning with another;
- three real texts:
  - spin in the abstracts of medical trials;
  - United Airlines' apology and its internal email;
  - the UK's guidance of 11 May 2020.

**Migration:**
- a dated note is appended to 21-40. Later the same day, at your request, 21-40's body was also updated in place, with each change marked. Its earlier version is kept in 21-40's `docarchive/finding_before_2026-10-10_update.md`;
- one line is appended to the correction notes in devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-42__harmony-across-the-communication-substrate/finding.md (19-42): its levels return, re-described;
- the project memory note on harmony is updated.

**MUST/COULD drift** (changes in the action list compared with 21-40):

1. **The dated notes (MUST).**
   - **Prior text:** "Append a dated note to the 20-02 finding saying what is replaced and what is kept, and point to this finding."
   - **New text:** "Append a dated note to the 21-40 finding (a fifth kind; the through-line narrowed; the two levels), add one line to the 19-42 correction notes, and update the memory note."
   - **Rationale:** the finding being refined is now 21-40.

2. **Choose the unit (MUST).**
   - **Prior text:** "Choose the unit (section 10): the composed communication (four kinds), or the whole situation (a fifth kind, 'suits the situation')."
   - **New text:** "Choose the unit, under two options:
     - the composed communication: five kinds; inside the substrate, 'the main point answers the need' is fit;
     - the whole situation: a sixth kind, 'suits the situation'; inside the substrate, the need and the relationship become parts that must belong."
   - **Rationale:** the count rose by one, and the choice now has a concrete case inside the substrate.

3. **Test the names (MUST).**
   - **Prior text:** "Test the names without their explanations." It covered:
     - the umbrella;
     - the kinds: through-line, skin, who-to-whom, due weight;
     - the failures: stray part, drift, seams, misaddressed part, wrong weight.
   - **New text:** the same test, with these added:
     - "content";
     - "the path of the telling", as the through-line's gloss;

     - whether "drift" still fits the narrowed line.

4. **Mark real messages (MUST).**

   - **New text:** "Apply the five kinds' questions and the clash rule to two or three real messages. Include one you received, and one whose content clashes while its wording stays even. Add the UK's 11 May 2020 guidance. Compare your marks with the AI's marks."
   - **Rationale:** the kinds changed. The two additions test your own distinction (content against phrasing) and the boundary between content and fit.

5. **New MUST: test the narrowed through-line on five real texts.**
   - **Prior text:** none.

   - **Prior text:** "Apply the four kinds and the clash rule to the Signals draft, and to a redraft if one is made."
   - **New text:** "Apply the five kinds, at both levels, to the Signals substrate and draft."
   - **Rationale:** the substrate level adds two placements:
     - the substrate's own frame and its planned order;
     - an added operating rule in the draft, which has no home in the substrate.

8. **Notes on the catalogues (COULD).**
   - **Prior text:** "note that the consistency peer straddles the through-line and the skin."
   - **New text:** "note that the consistency peer straddles content and the skin."
   - **Rationale:** agreement among claims and figures moved to content.

9. **Signals items 2, 6, 10 and 11 (COULD).**
   - **Prior text:** "If any is a failure, it would be who-to-whom (item 2) or through-line (items 6, 10, 11)."
   - **New text:** "If any is a failure:
     - item 2 is who-to-whom;
     - items 6, 10 and 11 are placed by the change test, as content or as the path;
     - items 10 and 11 (the recommendation stated twice; trial evidence in two paragraphs) would be the path's own failure, one topic told in two places."
   - **Rationale:** the split. Your answer on items 10 and 11 is also evidence for the through-line test.

10. **Real technical and strategic texts (COULD).**
    - **Prior text:** "Read one real technical text and one real strategic text with the four kinds."
    - **New text:** "… with the five kinds."
    - **Rationale:** the kinds changed.

11. **The deferred split (DEFERRED).**
    - **Prior text:** "Split 'meanings agree' out of the through-line as a kind of its own."
    - **New text:** done in this finding, and widened: "one thing", bearing and grounds moved too.
    - **Rationale:** it was made on structural grounds and on your proposal, not on the evidence gate 21-40 set. Reasoning gives the details.

12. **The sketch of where each kind is formed (DEFERRED).**
    - **Prior text:** "Sketch where each kind is formed in the substrate: fit chooses the point; the point structure forms the through-line; who-to-whom is stated once; names are fixed for the skin; due weight is applied when the text is written, following the rank."
    - **New text:** done (section 6), with one change: the point structure forms content (one point, every part bearing on it) and plans the path.
    - **Rationale:** the split.

13. **Questions for the uncovered relations (DEFERRED).**
    - **Prior text:** "Draft written questions for the six relations no pattern covers".
    - **New text:** unchanged. Relation 6, "a part judged against the communication's own point", is now content harmony's bearing.
    - **Rationale:** the split.

## Question

**Your message, verbatim:**

> "realize that the harmony also has, I guess, the third one or maybe the fourth one, I don't know. Harmony of the. Uh, the content. It's not content is different from the like phrasing here, yeah, like, but content's and substrate's harmony is also another version of it. It's also important."

**The goal: a conceptual account covering:**
- what content harmony is, as distinct from the skin (phrasing) and from the through-line;
- what substrate harmony is: a kind, a level, or a unit;
- how both fit the current model: the four kinds, the neighbours, the open choice of unit;
- where "the text agrees with its substrate" belongs;
- why it matters;
- plain names.

It had to hold for any communication and any substrate. Examples serve only as tests.

## Finding Summary

**Your proposal held**
- **Content harmony is a kind of its own.** What is said holds together, whatever order it is told in and whatever words are used.
- **It is different from phrasing (the skin).** Rewording can break the skin. It cannot break content harmony.
- **It is different from the order of telling.** Reordering can break the through-line. It cannot break content harmony.
- **Only a change of meaning can break it.**
- **The earlier model had folded it into the through-line.** 21-40 (the earlier finding on the kinds of harmony) left this split open. This finding makes it.

**What content harmony asks.** These are examples, not a checklist:
- **The meanings agree.** No two facts, figures, claims, decisions or statuses contradict.
- **They bear on the point.** Nothing is an orphan.
- **They stay within their grounds.** No claim goes further than the support the communication itself gives.
- **Together they establish one thing.**

**Two things that look like clashes and are not**
- **A tension the communication declares,** such as "we disagree on X" or two options set side by side.
- **An apparent clash that a dropped limit explains.** "Costs fell" and "costs rose" are both true if one is about the EU and the other about the US. Restore the limit first.

**What content harmony is not**
- **Not truth.** A consistent account can be false.
- **Not soundness.** A consistent plan can be a bad plan.
- **Not completeness.** A coherent account can still lack its ask.
- **Not fit.** A clash that appears only against the recipient's circumstances is fit.

**Five kinds, each broken by one kind of change**

| Kind | What must belong together | Only this change can break it |
|---|---|---|
| **content** (new) | what is said | changing a meaning |
| **through-line** (narrowed to "the path of the telling") | the order of telling | reordering |
| **skin** | the surface: words, names, tone, format | rewording |
| **who-to-whom** | the speaker and the audience | re-addressing |
| **due weight** | the emphasis, against what matters | re-emphasising |

- **Two kinds belong to the account:** what is said, and who speaks to whom.
- **Three belong to the telling:** its order, its surface, and its emphasis.

**The substrate is a level, not another kind**
- **Your phrase names one thing from two sides.** "Content's and substrate's harmony" is content harmony together with the place where it is first formed and checked.
- **The substrate is that place.** It is the account written before drafting. There, what is said and who speaks to whom are decided, and the order, the names and the rank are planned.
- **The text is a telling of that account.** All five kinds are realised in the text.
- **Check in this order:**
  1. the substrate;
  2. the text;
  3. that the text matches its substrate.
- **"Matches its substrate"** means every part of the text has a home in the account, and nothing in the text contradicts it.

**Where "the text agrees with its substrate" belongs**
- **It is harmony judged across the two levels.** It is where harmony meets the project's materialization check (does the text express well what the author settled?). The two are two lenses on one failure.
- **The harmony lens adds two things:**
  - it finds a text part that has no place in the account;
  - it asks which side should change.

**Who repairs**
- **The kind names what must change:** meaning, order, wording, addressing or emphasis.
- **The level names who decides:**
  - a clash in the substrate goes to the author or the sources;
  - a clash only in the text is re-told from the substrate;
  - a mismatch between them is diagnosed first: which side is wrong?

**Why it matters**
- The text copies whatever its substrate gives it.
- Content clashes can only be settled by the author.
- AI drafters add parts that have no home in the substrate.
- So the substrate is the first place to check.

**What inside the substrate is not harmony**
- **The need, the sources and the relationship are recorded in the substrate, not composed by the author.** So checking the main point against the need is fit, and checking standing against the sources is fidelity. They are checked in the same place, under their own labels.
- **The project's existing substrate checks are mostly fit, fidelity and completeness.** Only "no orphans" is harmony.
- **No existing check compares one meaning with another.**

**Real texts from outside the project**
- **Spin in trial abstracts.** Conclusions claim a benefit that the paper's own results do not support: content (grounds). Emphasis on subgroup results: due weight.
- **United Airlines, April 2017.** A public apology and an internal email about the same event disagree: content, across a series of messages.
- **The UK's guidance of 11 May 2020.** "Go to work" alongside "avoid public transport" clashes only against many readers' circumstances: fit, not content.

**Open, and yours to decide**
- **The unit:** five kinds, or six with "suits the situation". Inside the substrate, the choice decides whether "the main point answers the need" is harmony.
- **The names.**
- **Whether the narrowed through-line survives as a kind.** Real texts must show a failure of the path that is not also a failure of order or of content. Otherwise the line merges into the project's existing order property ("each meaning comes before what depends on it").

## Finding

### 1. Why this question

**The project.** Communication Craft is this project's work on how messages are formed and checked. It builds a **substrate** before any text is written: a structured account of what a message must convey. A substrate holds:
- the need;
- the main point and key points;
- the facts, each with its standing (how firmly it is held, and on what basis);
- the open choices;
- the ask;
- a plan for the message.

**The previous step.** 21-40 defined harmony as "the different parts of a communication belong together as one whole". It found four kinds:
- **through-line,** your first variation;
- **skin,** your second;
- **who-to-whom;**
- **due weight.**

21-40 kept "meanings agree" inside the through-line, and deferred the question of splitting it out.

**Your message.** You then said that harmony has another kind, harmony of the content. You said it is different from phrasing. You added that "content's and substrate's harmony" is another version of harmony, and that it is important.

**This inquiry tested three things:**
- whether content harmony really is a kind;
- what "the substrate's harmony" is;
- what both change in the model.

### 2. What content harmony is

**Definition.** The meanings of a communication hold together, whatever order they are told in and whatever words are used.

**The four aspects.** These are examples of how it holds, not a checklist.

| Aspect | Holds when | Fails as |
|---|---|---|
| **agree** | no two facts, figures, claims, decisions or statuses contradict | "costs £10k" in one paragraph, "£50k" in another |
| **bear on the point** | every meaning relates to what the communication is establishing | a paragraph that relates to nothing else (an orphan) |
| **stay within their grounds** | no claim goes further than the support the communication itself gives | a conclusion stronger than the evidence stated in the same text |
| **establish one thing** | the meanings together establish one claim, purpose or effect, each level summing up what is below it | a slogan "trying to do a minimum of six things" |

**What it is made of.** The project's unit of meaning (from devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_16-16__the-meaning-unit-what-a-communication-conveys/finding.md, 16-16) has three parts:
- a content;
- a force (what is done with it: stated, asked, decided);
- internal limits.

Units are linked by relations such as support, condition, contrast and sequence. Content harmony is these relations holding:
- **a contradiction** is an unmarked clash between two units;
- **an orphan** is a unit with no relation to the point;
- **an overreach** is a unit whose force is stronger than the units supporting it allow.

**It occurs in every form tested.** These cases were constructed to show reach, not frequency:

| Form | Content disharmony |
|---|---|
| an explainer | a term defined one way in section 1 and used another way in section 4 |
| a design document | the constraint "no new dependencies", then a proposal that adds a queue service |
| an incident update | "started 09:40" in the summary, "started 09:10" in the timeline |
| a board paper | the goal "grow share" beside the plan "cut all marketing", with the trade-off never stated |
| a performance review | an "exceeds expectations" rating over findings that expectations were missed |
| a condolence note | "I didn't know him well", then "we were so close" |
| a recipe | the ingredients say 2 eggs; the method uses 3 |
| a contract | clause 4 grants a right that clause 9 removes |
| a novel | a character's eye colour changes |
| a scientific paper | the abstract claims an effect that the results report as not significant |

**What readers say when it breaks:**
- "Wait, which is it?"
- "That contradicts what you said above."
- "That doesn't follow from what you've shown."
- "What does this have to do with the rest?"

These complaints differ from the through-line's ("I lost the thread", "why are we back here?") and from the skin's ("why does it suddenly sound different?").

### 3. How it differs from phrasing and from the order of telling

**Your distinction already exists outside the project.** Several traditions separate what is said from how it is told:
- **Text linguistics.** de Beaugrande and Dressler distinguish **coherence** from **cohesion**.
  - Coherence concerns "the components of the textual world, i.e., the configuration of concepts and relations which underlie the surface text".
  - Cohesion concerns how "the components of the surface text … are mutually connected within a sequence". ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coherence_(linguistics)))
- **The psychology of reading.** Kintsch separates "the surface form representation, the propositional textbase representation, and the situation model". ([source](https://sites.pitt.edu/~perfetti/PDF/Kintsch%20&%20Rawson.pdf))
- **Classical rhetoric** has three separate stages of composition:
  - *inventio*, "developing arguments and content";
  - *dispositio*, "organizing them in a coherent structure";
  - *elocutio*, "word choice, tone, and pace". ([source](https://pressbooks.pub/openrhetoric/chapter/five-canons-of-rhetoric))
- **Editing practice** works in three separate passes:
  - developmental editing: "story, organization, clarity, and overall structure";
  - line editing: "style, rhythm, tone, sentence construction";
  - copyediting: "mechanics … inconsistencies … house style". ([source](https://bernoff.com/blog/how-a-developmental-edit-differs-from-a-copy-edit-or-line-edit-and-why-that-matters))

**Content harmony comes apart from each of the four earlier kinds.** For each pair there is a case where one fails and the other holds, in both directions:

| Pair | Content fails, the other holds | The other fails, content holds |
|---|---|---|
| content / through-line | a well-ordered argument that moves cleanly to its conclusion, but gives the cost as £10k in paragraph 2 and £50k in paragraph 4 | correct, consistent installation steps told out of order |
| content / skin | even, polished prose whose summary says "on track" while the body shows two missed milestones | consistent facts, written half in formal register and half in chat slang |
| content / who-to-whom | one speaker to one audience throughout, but a goal ("grow share") that conflicts with the means ("cut all marketing") | consistent content, with one paragraph slipping from "I" into "the company regrets…" |
| content / due weight | the emphasis matches the importance, but the conclusion claims more than the evidence given | consistent content, with the key decision in a footnote |

**The change test.** Apply one kind of change to a text, and see which kind it can break:

| Change applied to a text | Content | Through-line | Skin | Who-to-whom | Due weight |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| reword every sentence, keeping the meanings and order | — | — | **can break** | — | can break (tone) |
| reorder the parts, keeping the wording | — | **can break** | — | — | **can break** (position) |
| change a figure, a claim or a status | **can break** | — | — | — | — |
| change who a part is addressed to | — | — | — | **can break** | — |
| change the emphasis (bold, space, place) | — | — | — | — | **can break** |

**What the test shows.** Content harmony is the one kind that only a change of meaning can break. That is your sentence, "content is different from the like phrasing", in testable form.

**How the test separates two kinds that one change can touch.** A buried main point is touched by both reordering and re-emphasis.
- **Position as a sign of importance** is due weight.
- **The order between parts that depend on each other** is the through-line.
- **Spin shows the separation on a real case** (section 11):
  - claims beyond the results are content;
  - emphasis on subgroups is due weight.

### 4. What content harmony is not

**Not truth.** A consistent account can be false. In de Beaugrande's words, "a textual world is created that does not have to comply to the real world". Whether the content is true is fidelity, together with the content check of devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_13-08__materialization-check-vs-content-check-in-communication-craft/finding.md (13-08). That check asks "is the meaning right?".

**Not soundness.** A consistent plan can be a bad plan. 13-08's example of "four people for a 15-minute job" is consistent and unsound.

**Not completeness.** A coherent account can lack its ask.

**Not the consistency peer.** The project's peer taxonomy (compare/devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-05_18-49__peer-categories-of-explicitness-in-communication-problems/finding.md) lists 15 properties of a text, each checked against one thing. One of them, consistency, says "no two statements, figures, counts or names disagree". It detects one face of content harmony, the agreement of statements and figures, in the text. It does not cover:
- bearing on the point;
- staying within grounds;
- establishing one thing;
- the substrate level.

It also covers names, which belong to the skin.

**Not fit.** A clash that appears only against the recipient's own circumstances is fit, because the other side of the clash is outside the communication. The UK's guidance of 11 May 2020 shows this (section 11).

**A declared tension is not a clash.** A contrast the communication declares does not break content harmony. Examples of declarations:
- "We disagree on X";
- "Clause 9 prevails over clause 4";
- two options set side by side as options.

Only undeclared clashes count. The declaration must be quotable: if none can be quoted, the contrast is a clash. This is the content counterpart of 21-40's rule for the skin, belonging, not sameness.

**Restore the limit first.** Many apparent clashes come from a dropped limit. "Costs fell" and "costs rose" are both true if one is about the EU and the other about the US. So the repair has an order:
1. Check whether the communication itself holds a limit that reconciles the two meanings, and quote it. If it does, restore the limit. That is an explicitness fix, and it needs no new decision.
2. Only if it does not, ask the author which side holds.

### 5. The five kinds after the split

**The map:**

| Kind | What must belong together | Only this change can break it | Lives in |
|---|---|---|---|
| **content** (new) | what is said: the meanings agree, bear on the point, stay within their grounds and establish one thing | changing a meaning | decided in the account |
| **through-line**, now "the path of the telling" | the order of telling: each told part follows from the one before, along the account's structure | reordering | planned in the account, realised in the text |
| **skin** | the surface: words, names, tone, formality, format | rewording | planned in the account (names), realised in the text |
| **who-to-whom** | the speaker and the audience | re-addressing | decided in the account |
| **due weight** | the text's emphasis against the account's importance and gravity | re-emphasising | planned in the account (rank), realised in the text |

**Why these five.** A communication is someone saying something to someone, told in some way.
- **What the account decides:** what is said (content), and who speaks to whom (who-to-whom).
- **What the telling adds:** an order (the through-line), a surface (the skin), and an emphasis (due weight).
- **Each kind of the telling is broken by changing that one thing:** reordering, rewording, or re-emphasising.

**What the through-line keeps.** 21-40's through-line had four clauses. The change test sorts them:

| 21-40's clause (verbatim) | Can reordering break it? | Now belongs to |
|---|---|---|
| "the communication establishes one thing" | no | content: the account has one point |
| "every part helps establish it" | no: a digression stays a digression in any order | content: every meaning bears on the point |
| "each part follows from, or connects to, what is around it" | split | **content**, for whether the relation between two meanings exists. **The through-line**, for whether the telling follows that relation. |
| "no part contradicts another" | no | content: agreement |

**The narrowed through-line, by its own failures.** It holds when:
- each told part follows from the one before;
- one topic is told in one place;
- nothing loops back to a topic already closed;
- no jump goes unmarked.

A concrete check: each part opens from something the reader already has, and moves on to something new, toward the point.

**Your first variation, placed.**
- "Paragraphs or sequential of the text … checks the connections" stays with the through-line.
- "Contributes toward one meaning" now has two faces:
  - **content:** the paragraph's meaning bears on the point;
  - **the through-line:** the paragraph is in its place on the path.

This assignment is the one most open to your reading. Section 13 returns to it.

**A watch on the through-line.** The project already has an order property among its peers: "each meaning comes before what depends on it".
- **The narrowed line has failures that order does not catch:**
  - scattering: one topic told in three places;
  - looping back;
  - unmarked jumps.
- **They can come apart in constructed cases, both ways:**
  - *the line holds while order fails:* instructions told along one steady path, where step 5 needs a tool that step 7 introduces;
  - *order holds while the line fails:* a report that returns to the budget three times, with every dependency satisfied.
- **No real text yet shows the line failing on its own.**

So the line stays a kind, on a stated test (section 13 and Next Actions).

### 6. Substrate harmony: a level, not another kind

**What the substrate is.**
- **The account.** What a communication means: its claims, facts, decisions, questions and requests, the relations between them, its main point, and who speaks to whom.
- **The substrate.** That account written down before drafting, or extracted from a draft afterwards. Two current forms:
  - the two-half brief of devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_17-45__signals-proposal-dialogue-as-communication-substrate/finding.md (17-45);
  - the point structure of devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_19-31__the-substrate-concept-that-arranges-the-message/finding.md (19-31).
- **The text.** What the recipient actually receives.

**No new kind lives in the substrate.** Every relation found inside it is one of the five kinds, or a neighbour:

| Relation inside the substrate | What it is |
|---|---|
| facts against facts; the ask against the open choices; the essence against its facts | content |
| the account written partly to the drafter and partly to the recipient | who-to-whom |
| the paragraph plan against the point structure | the through-line, planned |
| two names for one thing in the list of names | the skin, planned |
| the planned order putting background before the point | due weight, planned |
| the main point against the need; the register against the relationship | fit (section 9) |
| standing against the sources | fidelity |

What the substrate adds is a **place and a time**: the account, before any text exists.

**Formed, planned, realised.**
- **Formed in the account:**
  - content;
  - who-to-whom. The frame (who speaks, to whom, doing what) is chosen when the account is formed.
- **Planned in the account:**
  - the order (the through-line);
  - the names (the skin);
  - the rank (due weight).
- **Realised in the text:** all five kinds.

**The text is a telling of the account.** The same relation appears in two other fields:
- in software, a data model and its views;
- in Kintsch's psychology of reading, the situation model and the surface form.

Harmony belongs first to the account. The text adds its own telling and must match the account.

**When no substrate is written, the levels still apply.** The account can be:
- extracted from the draft and then confirmed (the backward route in 16-16);
- rebuilt by a receiver, who can check its content harmony without access to the sender's notes.

Live spoken exchanges, where the account is never written, are not yet examined (Open Questions).

**Your phrase, read.** "Content's and substrate's harmony" names a kind (content) and a level (the substrate) together. That pairing is accurate, not a confusion. In the content reading of "substrate" (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_15-59__communication-substrate-concepts-and-boundaries/finding.md, 15-59: "substrate names the meanings carried by a particular contribution"), the substrate *is* the content, held before the text. So content harmony is the kind, and the substrate is where it is first formed and checked.

**Your first framing of harmony, honoured.** Your first harmony message, in 19-42 (the earlier inquiry on harmony across the substrate), spoke of "a harmony of um, like other other concepts inside the substrate". 19-42 kept "the substrate" as a level. Two later inquiries dropped it:
- devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_20-02__naming-and-defining-what-harmony-points-at/finding.md (20-02);
- 21-40.

This finding restores it.

### 7. The text matches its substrate

**The relation.** Every part of the text has a home in the account, and nothing in the text contradicts the account. It is content harmony, and who-to-whom, judged across the two levels. A text part with no place in the account is a stray part across levels.

**A borrowed picture.** Databases have referential integrity: every reference must point to a record that exists. A text part with no home in the account is like a dangling reference.

**It meets the materialization check.** 13-08's materialization check asks whether the text expresses well what the author has settled. It covers:
- explicitness;
- order;
- view and level;
- length;
- readability;
- unintended implication.

On most mismatches the two lenses see the same failure, and the repair is to rewrite.

**What the harmony lens adds.** It is not a new, separate check. It adds two things the materialization list does not name:
- **A text part with no home in the account.** An AI drafter can add a sentence that nobody decided. It is "well expressed", and it belongs to nothing.
- **The question of which side should change.** Sometimes drafting reveals a better meaning. Then the text is right and the account is wrong, and the account is refined on supported grounds, as devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_19-33__communication-substrate-materialization-and-review-loop/finding.md (19-33, on the essence) puts it.

### 8. Who repairs: what changes, and who decides

**The rule has two parts.**
- **The kind names what must change:**
  - a meaning (content);
  - an order (the through-line);
  - a wording (the skin);
  - an addressing (who-to-whom);
  - an emphasis (due weight).
- **The level names who decides:**
  - **a clash in the account** is settled by the author or the sources, because it needs a decision about meaning or frame;
  - **a clash only in the text,** where the account holds, is re-told from the account; no new decision is needed;
  - **a mismatch between the text and its account** is diagnosed first: which side is wrong?

**The case that separates the two parts.** The account holds consistent figures, and the text mistypes one.
- **The kind is content:** a meaning in the text changed.
- **The level is the text:** the repair is re-telling the figure from the account, not asking the author.

**Where this comes from.** It extends 13-08's rules:
- every check declares its kind;
- "problems are classified by what is at fault, not by which tool found them".

Here, a harmony report names both what is at fault (the kind) and where (the level).

**One exception lowers the cost.** Suppose the account itself holds the limit that reconciles two meanings (the "restore the limit first" case in section 4). Then the clash can be resolved without a new decision. An AI may propose the fix, and the author confirms it.

### 9. What inside the substrate counts as harmony

**Recorded references stay outside harmony, by default.** Some things are written into the substrate but not composed by the author:
- the need, taken from whoever set it;
- the sources;
- the relationship.

Comparing composed content with these is what 21-40's criterion assigns to the neighbours:
- the main point against the need: **fit**;
- the register against the relationship: **fit**;
- standing against the sources: **fidelity**.

These are checked in the same place and at the same time, under their own labels.

**This is 21-40's open choice of unit, made concrete.**
- **The default** keeps the comparison inside the composed account.
- **Under the whole-situation unit,** the need and the relationship become parts that must belong. "The main point answers the need" would then be harmony, and so would the register against the relationship.

Your 19-42 words ("other concepts inside the substrate") support that reading. The choice stays yours (section 13).

**The project's existing substrate checks, sorted by what they compare.** They come from two earlier findings:
- 19-31, with three checks on the point structure;
- devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_17-15__essence-formation-moves-revisited/finding.md (17-15, on forming the substrate). Its checks are "open conditions": things still open while the substrate is formed.

| Existing check (verbatim) | Source | Property |
|---|---|---|
| "Main point vs top requirement. Quote both. Does the main point answer it?" | 19-31 | fit |
| "No orphans. Every fact names its point." | 19-31 | **content harmony** (bearing) |
| "Key points traced. Every key point names a requirement." | 19-31 | fit |
| "a requirement with no source" | 17-15 | fit |
| "a requirement with no answer" | 17-15 | completeness |
| "an answer with no standing, or with a lost limit" | 17-15 | fidelity; a lost limit can also make two meanings clash (content) |
| "an answer not traced to a requirement" | 17-15 | fit |
| "a produced answer unsettled or untested" | 17-15 | fidelity, soundness |

**What the sorting shows.**
- **Only one existing check is harmony:** "no orphans".
- **17-15's "'Formed' is not 'correct'" extends.** "Formed" is also not "in harmony": closing every open condition never checks whether the meanings agree.

**Four checks that compare meaning with meaning are missing:**
- the figures and claims agree across the account;
- the essence stays within its facts;
- the ask agrees with the open choices;
- the account speaks in one frame.

When the account argues a case, Minto's pyramid rules give a ready form for its structure: "Ideas at any level in the pyramid must always be summaries of the ideas grouped below them; ideas in each grouping must always be of the same kind of idea; ideas in each grouping must always be logically ordered". ([source](https://strategyu.co/pyramid-principle-2/))

### 10. Why it matters

**The text copies its substrate.** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_18-38__why-a-full-substrate-still-produced-a-weird-opening/finding.md (18-38) traced the Signals draft's weird opening to the substrate: "Its own order caused it". Disharmony in the substrate does not stay there.

**Content clashes need the author.** Two figures that disagree cannot be fixed by rewording; someone must decide which is right. Before any text exists, the author is still at hand.

**AI drafters add parts with no home.** Two examples:
- the Signals draft added an operating rule that nobody decided;
- a 2024 journal paper kept an AI's line to its user in the published text.

Checking that the text matches its substrate is therefore a safeguard for AI drafting, not a formality.

**The substrate is the one place where everything about meaning can be checked before drafting:**
- harmony;
- fit;
- fidelity;
- completeness.

Each is checked under its own label.

**Outside practice does the same.** Writers' rooms keep a "story bible", "the master document that keeps writers' rooms consistent", because content harmony cannot be held text by text. It catches failures such as "a character's eye color flips, a magic rule contradicts chapter 3". ([source](https://www.bookishelf.com/how-successful-authors-keep-their-story-worlds-consistent-even-across-10-books/))

**The claim is reasoned, not measured.** That the substrate is the *cheapest* place to check rests on one project case (18-38) and on outside practice. It has not been measured.

### 11. Evidence from real texts

**What was read.** Three real texts from outside the project, each read against its published reactions. None was used to build the model. The placements are mine, tied to the critics' own words.

| Real text | What readers said (verbatim) | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|
| **Spin in trial abstracts.** Boutron and colleagues (*JAMA*, 2010) studied 72 randomised trials whose primary outcome was not significant. | "More than half … spun their statistically nonsignificant conclusions in a positive way". Spin is defined as "reporting strategies to highlight that the experimental treatment is beneficial, despite a statistically nonsignificant difference for the primary outcome, or to distract the reader". Authors "parsed their data until they found statistically significant results (within a small subgroup), which they would then emphasize". | **Content (grounds):** conclusions claim more than the paper's own results support, inside one document. <br>**Due weight:** subgroup results stressed over the primary outcome. <br>The model separates spin's two faces. |
| **United Airlines, April 2017.** The CEO's public statement and his internal email about a passenger dragged off a flight. | **Public:** "I apologize for having to re-accommodate these customers". <br>**Internal:** the passenger was "disruptive and belligerent", and employees "followed established procedures". <br>**Press:** "An internal memo … conflicts with a previous statement of apology". | **Content, across a series:** two messages from one sender about one event do not agree. <br>**Intelligibility, and fit:** the euphemism "re-accommodate" was widely mocked. <br>**Fidelity:** Chicago's aviation department said staff "did not follow standard procedure". |
| **The UK government's guidance, 11 May 2020** | "anyone who can't work from home … should be actively encouraged to go to work", together with "avoid public transport if possible". Critics called it "confusing, hard to follow". | **Fit, not content.** The two instructions are compatible in the abstract: you can drive, walk or cycle. They clash only against many readers' circumstances (no car, a long commute), which lie outside the text. |

**What the three show.**
- **Every complaint lands** in a kind, a neighbour, or the series scale.
- **Content harmony appears in two real forms:**
  - grounds, inside one document (spin);
  - agreement, across a series (United).
- **The boundary with fit works.** The UK case is a felt contradiction that depends on the reader's circumstances, and it lands in fit.

**One case from 21-40, re-placed.** The UK's "Stay Alert" slogan was criticised as "trying to do a minimum of six things". That now lands in content: the account does not establish one thing. Its vagueness stays intelligibility.

**What is not yet evidenced.**
- **Bearing:** no real outside text yet shows an orphan.
- **The narrowed through-line:** no real text yet shows a path failure that is not also an order failure.
- **The series unit:** United's two messages went to different audiences (the public, and employees). The rule for when two messages count as one series has not been stated.

**Sources:**
- [MinnPost on Boutron et al.](https://www.minnpost.com/second-opinion/2010/06/how-medical-studies-are-spun-make-nonsignificant-appear-significant/)
- [ASCO Post on abstract spin](https://ascopost.com/News/21610)
- [WFTV on United's internal email](https://www.wftv.com/news/trending-now/united-ceos-internal-email-describes-man-dragged-off-flight-as-disruptive-belligerent/511348829)
- [Hawaiʻi Public Radio on United's apologies](https://www.hawaiipublicradio.org/2017-04-11/after-unsatisfying-answers-united-offers-deepest-apology-for-violent-video)
- [Washington Times / AP on the UK guidance](https://www.washingtontimes.com/news/2020/may/11/boris-johnson-uk-pm-go-to-work-advice-sparks-wide-/)

### 12. Names

**Your word "content", tested first.**
- **It matches the dictionary.** "The substance or material dealt with in a speech, literary work, etc., as distinct from its form or style". ([source](https://www.encyclopedia.com/humanities/dictionaries-thesauruses-pictures-and-press-releases/content-0)) That is exactly your contrast with phrasing.
- **It collides with one project term.** 13-08's "content check" asks "is the meaning right?", which is truth and soundness. Content harmony asks something else.

**Two ways to resolve the collision:**
- **Keep "content" with a gloss:** "what is said holds together, not whether it is true". This is the recommendation.
- **Rename 13-08's check.** 13-08 itself offered "substance check" "if the collision causes confusion", and left the choice to you. The risk: the dictionary defines "content" as "the substance …", so the collision may move rather than vanish.

**Names for the other new pieces:**

| Piece | Name | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| the narrowed through-line | keep "through-line", glossed as **"the path of the telling"** | the gloss carries the narrowing |
| the text matching its substrate | **"matches its substrate"** | plain; the default |
| the same relation, vividly | **"every part has a home"** | from databases' referential integrity |
| an alternative for content harmony | **"one account"** | "account" is the project's own word for what a communication means |

**Names kept in reserve or rejected:**
- **"coherence": kept in reserve.** It is de Beaugrande's exact term. In everyday English, though, "is it coherent?" means "does it all hang together?", so readers would take it for the whole of harmony. It is deferred until you want the linguistic term.
- **"it adds up": deferred.** It is an idiom that invites a yes-or-no verdict, close to the forbidden check "is it harmonious?".
- **"one picture" and "agreement": rejected.** "One picture" is vague. "Agreement" covers only clashes between two meanings.
- **"consistency", "substance", "sound" and "faithful": avoided.** Each is a neighbour's name or sense:
  - "consistency" is a peer;
  - "substance" is 13-08's alternative name for its check;
  - "sound" is 13-08's sense of "right";
  - "faithful" suggests fidelity.

**Not yet named:**
- **What you find when content harmony breaks.** 21-40 called the old through-line's failure "drift". "Drift" may now fit the path's wandering, or content's off-point parts. This is part of your name test.

### 13. The choices left to you

**1. The unit.**
- **The composed communication (recommended).** Five kinds. Inside the substrate, "the main point answers the need" is fit, checked beside harmony under its own label.
- **The whole situation.** A sixth kind, "suits the situation". Inside the substrate, the need and the relationship become parts that must belong.

**Why the recommendation.** It keeps one kind of comparison inside the concept, which was the point of your correction about mixing. Your 19-42 words can be read either way.

**2. The names** (section 12). This includes:
- whether "content" needs its gloss;
- a name for content harmony's failure.

**3. Your first variation's words** (section 5). Do you read "contributes toward one meaning" as having two faces: the meaning bearing on the point (content), and the paragraph's place on the path (the through-line)?

**Not yours to decide, but still open: whether the through-line survives.** Real texts decide this, by the test in Next Actions.

## Reasoning

**Killed, and what each kill taught:**

- **"Content harmony is not a kind; keep four".** This was the strongest objection: agreement is the consistency peer, and "within grounds" is fidelity. It was killed twice:
  - **in innovation,** because the consistency peer covers only agreement in the text, not bearing, grounds, one point or the substrate level;
  - **in critique, on outside evidence.** Spin's overreach inside one document is caught by none of the four kinds. United's clash across a series would have sat in the through-line, where reordering cannot repair it.
  - **Lesson:** the consistency peer is one of content harmony's detectors in the text, not the property itself.
- **Keeping 21-40's broad through-line, with content as its "substance face".** It mixes the account level with the text level, which is the mixing you objected to.
- **No levels: harmony lives only in the text.** Killed for three reasons:
  - you named "substrate's harmony";
  - disharmony in the substrate is copied into the text (18-38);
  - every kind can fail in the account before any text exists.
- **A sixth, "substrate" kind.** Every relation found inside the substrate is one of the five kinds or a neighbour. Lesson: the substrate adds a place and a time, not a relation.
- **Labelling this finding "corrects" 21-40.**
  - "Corrects" is for confident claims shown wrong.
  - 21-40 itself had marked the split LOW and deferred it with a trigger.
  - Its umbrella, its criterion and three of its kinds stand.
- **"One picture" and "agreement" as names.** "One picture" is vague. "Agreement" covers only clashes between two meanings.

**Survived, and why each held:**

- **Content harmony as a kind.** Five independent grounds:
  - it comes apart from each earlier kind, in both directions;
  - the change test isolates it;
  - its repair needs a decision about meaning, not rewording or reordering;
  - outside traditions keep it separate (coherence and cohesion; textbase and surface; invention, arrangement and style; developmental and line editing);
  - real texts show it (spin, United).
- **The two levels and the match.**
  - They are told in one sentence.
  - They are grounded in Kintsch's levels and in the story bible.
  - They restore your first framing.
- **The change test.** Four separate routes in innovation converged on it as what unifies the kinds. It extends 13-08's rule that each check declares what is at fault.
- **The recorded-reference rule, with the unit choice left open.** It keeps one kind of comparison inside harmony, and the labelled checklist loses nothing: fit is checked in the same place.
- **"Refines", with a dated note.** Nothing 21-40 asserted with confidence is shown false. The split it marked LOW is now made.
- **"Content" as the name, with a gloss.** It is your word, and the dictionary's sense of it is exactly your distinction.

**Refined: the through-line.**
- **The case for merging it into the order property.** What remains of the line, once content is removed, looks like order, and no real text yet shows it failing alone.
- **The case for keeping it:**
  - it has failures that order does not catch (scattering, looping back, unmarked jumps);
  - it can come apart from order in constructed cases;
  - it is your first variation.
- **The result:** it is kept on a stated test (Next Actions).

**Where this inquiry corrected itself:**

- **One surfacing note conflated two kinds of trace.** It said "an answer not traced to a requirement" is a stray part in the substrate.
  - Traced to the *need*, it is fit.
  - Traced to the *point*, it is content harmony.
- **The repair rule was stated two ways.**
  - **Sensemaking** concluded that the level decides the repair: a content clash that exists only in the text, such as a mistyped figure, is re-told, not decided.
  - **Innovation's assembly and critique's change-test table** gave one repair per kind ("content needs a decision about meaning").
  - **The reconciliation, given here in section 8:** the kind names what must change; the level names who decides. The per-kind repair holds at the account level only.
- **The split went beyond 21-40's own gate.**
  - **What 21-40 set:** split "meanings agree" "if, across five real cases containing a contradiction, contradictions keep needing a different repair from digressions".
  - **What happened instead:** the split was made on your explicit proposal and on structural grounds, with two real cases, not five. It also moved digressions (bearing) to content, where 21-40's gate had treated them as the through-line's.
  - **Why the wider split:** reordering cannot repair a digression either.

- **Does "through-line" still get heard as "the main point"?** Observable in the same uses. That reading would put content back into the line.

### Research Frontiers

- **Accounts that are never written,** as in live talk. Who settles a clash in the account while it is being spoken?
- **The series unit.** Do two messages from one sender about one matter form one series when they go to different audiences? United's apology and internal email are the test case.
- **How far a variation may go before it stops belonging.** Carried from 21-40.

### Refinement Triggers

- **Merge the through-line into the order property** if, across five real texts with problems of order or path, no path failure is found that is not also an order or content failure.
- **Reopen content harmony as a kind** if a real case shows two meanings that clash in one order and agree in another. That would break the change test's claim that reordering cannot break content.
- **Reopen the recorded-reference rule** if, in your marking, you consistently mark "the main point does not answer the need" as "not in harmony". That would mean your concept is the whole-situation unit.
- **Reopen the placement of "matches its substrate"** if the materialization check is extended to name both a text part with no home and the question of which side should change. The harmony lens would then add nothing, and the relation would belong to materialization alone.

## Source Input

<summary>Raw user input for this finding</summary>

```text
realize that the harmony also has, I guess, the third one or maybe the fourth one, I don't know. Harmony of the. Uh, the content. It's not content is different from the like phrasing here, yeah, like, but content's  and  substrate's harmony  is also another version of it. It's also important.
from full traverse for this entire full reason traverse.
```

</details>

## Alignment note (added 2026-10-10)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_09-00__harmony-as-alignment-aligncraft-layers-in-communication/finding.md places this finding inside a larger frame:
- **Alignment names the family.** In AlignCraft's sense, alignment is agreement between what was meant and what is understood or produced. It is the family 21-40 called "every quality is an agreement with something".
- **Harmony is one member:** the alignment a communication can check against itself. It has two parts:
  - **internal coherence:** content, the path, the skin, who-to-whom;
  - **the telling matching its account:** due weight, and the text matching its substrate.
- **There are two relationships:**
  - drafting: the author and the drafter. The substrate is its alignment artifact.
  - communicating: the sender and the recipient. The text is its instrument.
- **The open choice of unit is dissolved by naming,** pending the user's confirmation:
  - the whole-situation concept is communication alignment;
  - harmony is its composed-unit member.


## Proposed note (added 2026-10-10, from devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_19-51__what-went-wrong-in-the-substrate-answer-checks-for-the-brief-and-content-in-it/finding.md; the owner adopts or removes)

- **Section 1's list, "the facts, each with its standing (how firmly it is held, and on what basis)", is read as "the basis of each point, referenced, with its standing".**
- **The owner's rule (2026-10-10):** "Brief should say like if there's this point, it should mention the raw context, but it should refer to it. And it can talk about it, but … it doesn't include it itself."
- **So:** the material (facts, figures, evidence, earlier texts) lives in the raw context. The substrate refers to it, may say what it uses and how firmly it holds, and does not carry it. This places the substrate on 15-59's content-reading side, with references to the supporting basis.
- **Why the note:** an AI reply copied the section-1 wording and put "the facts" inside the brief.
