# Finding: harmony as alignment, and AlignCraft's layers in communication

## Changes from Prior

**Prior paths:**
- devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_07-57__content-and-substrate-harmony/finding.md. This defined harmony's five kinds and two levels. It is called 07-57 below.
- devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_08-07__aligncraft-six-layers-in-communication-craft/finding.md. This mapped AlignCraft's six layers onto communication from the same pasted request, in another session. It is called 08-07 below.

**Revision trigger.** There were two:
- **Your new claim:** "by harmony all this time we were trying to talk about the alignment".
- **New evidence gathered for this inquiry:**
  - AlignCraft's own definitions, quoted;
  - Workcraft's definition of Alignment;
  - three real texts with published reactions;
  - the explicitness detector's own list of slots;
  - dictionary senses of the proposed names.

**What's preserved:**
- **From 07-57:** harmony's definition, its five kinds, its two levels, the change test and the repair rule (the kind names what must change; the level names who decides).
- **From 08-07:**
  - the six questions;
  - the distinction between the approach and the particular acts;
  - "coherence does not require sameness";
  - outcome needing evidence after the fact;
  - "the substrate supports all six";
  - the pillars applied qualitatively;
  - no claim that six is the only possible grouping.

**What's changed:**
- **Harmony is no longer the umbrella.** It is one member of a family, alignment, the member a communication can check against itself.
- **08-07's "local composition concerns" are given an exact place:**
  - internal coherence (content, the path, the skin, who-to-whom);
  - the action-set's match (due weight, and the text matching its substrate).
- **The open choice of unit is dissolved by naming,** pending your confirmation. The whole-situation concept is alignment. The composed-unit concept is harmony.
- **Completeness stops being "the exception".** It is an alignment of coverage.
- **20-02's rule against using "alignment" as a name is withdrawn.** It is the same concept, not a collision.

**What's new:**
- alignment as the name of 21-40's family ("every quality is an agreement with something");
- **two relationships:**
  - drafting: the author and the drafter;
  - communicating: the sender and the recipient;
- a reference for each layer in communication, tested on six real texts;
- the generalised clash rule;
- the four pillars, read as AlignCraft means them: how context is uncovered so that it can be aligned, at every layer. This was corrected after your review; an earlier version wrongly mapped them onto the project's explicitness detector and checking tools (see Reasoning);
- what detection can and cannot reach;
- the substrate as AlignCraft's alignment artifact, with four criteria and three new entries;
- the meaning gaps filled, and the new ones named.

**Migration:**
- dated notes appended to 07-57, to 08-07, and to devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_21-40__kinds-of-harmony-and-a-better-word/finding.md (21-40);
- the project memory note on harmony updated.

**MUST/COULD drift** (changes in the action list compared with 07-57 and 08-07):

1. **The unit (MUST).**
   - **Prior text (07-57):** "Choose the unit, under two options: the composed communication … or the whole situation …".
   - **New text:** "Confirm that the choice is dissolved: the whole-situation concept is communication alignment; harmony is its composed-unit member."
   - **Rationale:** your new claim names the whole-situation concept.

2. **The names (MUST).**
   - **Prior text (07-57):** "Test the names without their explanations", covering the kinds, failures, and the substrate-matching names.
   - **New text:** the same test, plus: "alignment" for the family, and the member's name ("harmony", "internal coherence" or "belonging together").
   - **Rationale:** the family now has a name, and the member needs one.

3. **Your marking of real messages (MUST).**
   - **Prior text (07-57):** "Apply the five kinds' questions and the clash rule to two or three real messages".
   - **New text:** "… writing each finding as relationship · layer · this against that, with both sides quoted".
   - **Rationale:** the clash rule is generalised.

4. **The through-line test (MUST).** Carried unchanged from 07-57.

5. **The dated notes (MUST).**
   - **Prior text (07-57):** notes to 21-40 and 19-42.
   - **New text:** notes to 07-57, 08-07 and 21-40.
   - **Rationale:** new findings are being refined.

6. **The one-page reference (COULD).**
   - **Prior text (07-57):** five kinds, two levels, the repair rule, the labelled substrate checklist.
   - **New text:** a one-page alignment reference covering:
     - the family;
     - the two relationships;
     - the layers with their references;
     - harmony's place;
     - the clash rule;
     - the pillars;
     - what detection can reach.
   - **Rationale:** the frame widened.

7. **The four missing substrate checks (COULD, 07-57).**
   - **New text:** folded into "the substrate as an alignment artifact, in use" and "internal coherence checks".
   - **Rationale:** they belong to two different alignments.

8. **Worked alignment comparisons (COULD, 08-07).**
   - **Prior text:** "Develop a small set of worked alignment comparisons and corrections for Signals and a materially different exchange."
   - **New text:** the same, with relationships and the clash rule. 08-07's COULD comparing against existing guides is merged in as the baseline.
   - **Rationale:** the frame gained relationships and the rule.

## Question

**Your message, verbatim (the pasted AlignCraft introduction is omitted here; see Source Input):**

> "i want you to run full traverse about harmony and aligcraft similarities, i think by harmnoy all this time we were trying to talk about the alignment … this can be mapped into the communication as well … rather than just saying harmony … what I expect from this traverse is that it fulfilled the previous meaning gaps we had, maybe we didn't know we had … the substrate should be aligned with uh, the six layers … and then the materialization also … this is the base for us to run the detections we run the detections to check these six alignments"

**The goal. A conceptual account covering:**
- how harmony and alignment relate;
- a tested mapping of AlignCraft's six layers and four pillars onto communication;
- what that changes in the current model: the kinds of harmony, the neighbours, the three meaning gaps, the substrate, materialization, detection;
- which earlier meaning gaps it fills, and which new ones it reveals;
- plain names.

It had to hold for any communication, not only software or AI drafting.

## Finding Summary

**You were right, in a precise sense**
- **What you reached for under "harmony" is alignment.** Your first harmony message (19-42) spanned many references: meaning, structure, who sends to whom, "other concepts inside the substrate". That is the whole family of alignments.
- **The project already had this family, without a name.** 21-40: "every quality of a communication is an agreement with something". The three-gaps finding: "every Communication Craft check is a comparison". AlignCraft, your own methodology, names it: alignment.
- **Narrow harmony survives as one member.** It is the alignment a communication can check against itself. A rename would mix references, which was your own objection to 20-02.

**What alignment is**
- **Alignment is agreement between what was meant and what is understood or produced.** AlignCraft's question: "are we talking about the same thing?".
- **Documents stand in for understandings.** A match between two documents is evidence of alignment, not alignment itself.

**Two relationships, each with all six layers**
- **Drafting:** the author and whoever drafts (an AI or a person). The substrate is its alignment artifact.
- **Communicating:** the sender and the recipient. The text is its instrument.
- **Every alignment claim names its relationship.** The Hawaii false missile alert shows why. The drafting side failed: the officer misread a drill as real. The communicating side worked exactly as written: people understood and took shelter.
- **Why the mapping fits so well.** Communication is the medium of alignment, and alignment is the purpose of communication. Uncovering context so it can be aligned means stating it to someone, which is communicating.

**The six layers in communication, each with the thing it is compared against**
- **Workspace:** the drafter's understanding, against the actual situation.
- **Task:** the understood purpose and content, against the need and the intent.
- **Action-space:** the chosen approach, against what the task needs, among the alternatives.
- **Action-set:** what was produced, against what was meant.
- **Coherence:** each part or message, against what already exists, inside and beyond the communication.
- **Outcome:** what was understood and done, against the original need.

**All six alignments are required for every communication draft** (clarified with you, 2026-10-10)
- **None of the six is optional.** A draft needs workspace, task, action-space, action-set, coherence and outcome alignment.
- **What varies is the work each takes.** For a short reply, most are met at a glance. For a complex brief, each needs real work.
- **Outcome alignment is built in before sending and confirmed after.** Before sending, the draft is aimed at the intended effect, and you know how you would recognise it. After the recipient responds, you check what was understood and done.

**Your reading of each layer was right** (this tests your description of each layer, not whether any draft is aligned)
- **Workspace.** Refined: it is the actual situation; the substrate is where the drafter's understanding of it is written down.
- **Action-space.** Your open question is answered: it is the approach.
- **Coherence.** Your reading (disturbing other communications, future ones, other people) is right. Coherence also applies inside the communication.

**Where harmony sits**
- **Internal coherence:** content, the path, the skin, who-to-whom. These are AlignCraft's own coherence failures ("duplicate", "contradict established patterns", "introduce inconsistency") found inside one communication.
- **The telling matching its account:** due weight, and the text matching its substrate. This is the action-set's comparison.
- **Both have their reference inside the communication.** That is why harmony can be checked from the communication alone.

**The earlier concepts, placed**

| Earlier concept | Where it sits now |
|---|---|
| fidelity | the workspace |
| fit | the task, the approach and the wording |
| intelligibility | the action-set |
| completeness | an alignment of coverage |
| adequacy | all six alignments holding |

**The three meaning gaps**
- **They are the references the comparisons need:**
  - **the need** is the task layer's reference;
  - **the meaning** is the substrate's content;
  - **conveyance** is what the action-set's product makes available.
- **Why they are fundamental, in AlignCraft's terms:** they are context that must be uncovered (stated, and made accessible) before the task and the action-set can be aligned against them.

**Gaps you didn't know you had, now named**
- **The approach** (action-space).
- **Coherence beyond the communication:** other messages, other audiences, commitments.
- **Outcome**, split into uptake (understood) and effect (done).

**The four pillars: how context is uncovered so that it can be aligned** (corrected after your review)
- **What must be aligned is usually hidden:** in the author's head, in the environment, in earlier work, in what the recipient already has. The pillars uncover it, at every layer and in both relationships:
  - **Explicitness:** state it. The intent, the context and the boundaries are written down, not assumed.
  - **Visibility:** make it accessible to whoever has to align with it: the drafter, the author, a reviewer.
  - **Measurement:** say how a difference between what was meant and what happened would show.
  - **Comparison:** compare what was meant with what was produced.
- **The pillars are not the project's explicitness detector.** That detector checks one property of a finished text: whether its wording singles out what it needs. It is one check at the action-set layer, not a pillar.

**What detection can check: your expectation, made exact**
- **A check can only compare what has been uncovered.**
- **From the text alone:** only comparisons whose other side is in the text. That means internal coherence, which is defined but not built. The explicitness detector also runs on the text alone, as a check of the text's quality; it is measured.
- **Everything else needs its context uncovered first,** layer by layer, which is the pillars' work:
  - the substrate, for the action-set's match and for the task;
  - sources and other messages, for the workspace and for coherence beyond the communication;
  - replies, for the outcome.
- **A detector gap is not uncovered context.** It shows only that the text does not single something out. Whether that thing was ever established is a question about the context.

**One rule for every finding**
- Write it as **relationship · layer · this against that, with both sides quoted.**
- Harmony's clash rule is the internal special case. Your ban on "is it harmonious?" extends to "is it aligned?".

**The substrate, read as an alignment artifact**
- **AlignCraft's criteria:** "Captures a decision, not just information; Has a clear level; Is verifiable; Is current".
- **Three entries it lacks today:**
  - the approach, and why;
  - what must be preserved;
  - how the outcome would be recognised.
- **Its most important entry is the intent.**

**Real texts from outside the project**
- **Better.com's layoffs on one Zoom call:** the approach failed.
- **NASA's Columbia slide:**
  - its emphasis buried what mattered most (the match);
  - its title contradicted its own data (internal coherence);
  - the slide format was the wrong approach;
  - the uptake failed.
- **The Hawaii false alert:** the two relationships came apart.
- **Six cases were placed with nothing left over.** This does not prove six layers are enough.

**Yours to decide**
- **Confirm** that the unit choice is dissolved.
- **Choose the member's name:** "harmony", "internal coherence" (with its gloss), or "belonging together".

## Finding

### 1. Why this question

**Communication Craft** is this project's work on how messages are formed and checked. It is one of seven concepts in Workcraft, the project's framework for doing work with AI. Another of the seven is **Alignment**, which Workcraft defines as keeping "the intended work and its actual state comparable throughout execution". AlignCraft is your methodology for it, supplied on 16 September 2026 as "the core alignment methodology".

**Over the last day, Communication Craft defined harmony:**
- **21-40:** harmony is "the different parts of a communication belong together as one whole".
- **07-57:** five kinds and two levels.

**You now proposed that "harmony" was reaching for alignment.** You pasted AlignCraft's six layers and four pillars, and gave a reading of each layer for communication.

**Another session had already run a traverse on the same pasted text:** 08-07, concluded this morning. It mapped the six layers onto communication, and kept harmony's kinds as "local composition concerns". But it was built on 21-40's four kinds, before content harmony and the levels existed.

**So this inquiry's own ground:**
- the exact relation between harmony and alignment;
- how the layers meet the current five-kind, two-level model;
- the three meaning gaps;
- the pillars;
- what detection can check.

It builds on 08-07 and re-tests it.

### 2. What alignment is

**Definition.** Alignment is agreement between what was meant and what is understood or produced.

**AlignCraft's own words:**
- "When you delegate work — to a person, a team, or an AI — failure rarely happens at execution. It happens at understanding."
- "are we talking about the same thing? This is the alignment problem."
- Its Comparison pillar asks to "compare what was meant against what was produced".

([AlignCraft](https://karaposu.github.io/aligncraft/print.html))

**It is not limited to comparing with intent.** AlignCraft's coherence layer compares new work with "the existing aligned structure":

> "does this plan fit with what already exists? It might duplicate functionality. It might contradict established patterns. It might introduce inconsistency."

So alignment includes fitting what already exists, as well as matching what was meant.

**The same sense in other fields:**
- **Dialogue research:** "interlocutors achieve conversational success by aligning their understanding of the situation" (Pickering and Garrod).
- **Grounding:** the shared "mutual knowledge, mutual beliefs, and mutual assumptions" that conversation builds (Clark and Brennan).
- **The dictionary:**
  - "a state of agreement or cooperation among persons … with a common cause or viewpoint";
  - "the proper adjustment of the components … for coordinated functioning".

**Documents stand in for understandings.** A check compares documents, such as a text against its substrate, because understandings must be made explicit and visible before anything can be compared. A matching pair of documents is evidence of alignment. It is not alignment itself: a text can match a substrate that misstates what the author meant.

### 3. Why "harmony was alignment" holds, and in which sense

**The project already had the family, without a name:**
- 21-40: "Every quality of a communication is an agreement with something": harmony with itself, fit with the situation, fidelity with the record, intelligibility with its intended meaning.
- The three-gaps finding: "Every Communication Craft check is a comparison".
- The receiving finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-06_11-24__receiving-and-assessing-communication/finding.md): "Communication supplies the domain question … Alignment supplies the expected-versus-actual comparison."

**Your first harmony message spanned the family.** In 19-42 you spoke of "harmony of meaning", "harmony of the structure", "harmony of who's sending … to who", and "other concepts inside the substrate". That mixed references inside the communication with references outside it.

**Later inquiries narrowed "harmony" to its internal member.** 20-02 avoided the word "alignment" deliberately, as it put it, to "avoid this project's existing terms ('… alignment')". That avoidance kept apart two names for one concept.

**So your claim holds as follows:**
- The concept you were reaching for is alignment, your own methodology's word.
- Harmony, defined narrowly, is one member.

**A plain rename would mix references again.** Calling the narrow harmony "alignment" would join "fits with itself" and "fits with its situation" in one concept. That was your own objection to 20-02 ("mixing multiple things").

### 4. Two relationships

| Relationship | The parties | Its artifact or instrument |
|---|---|---|
| **Drafting** | the author, and whoever drafts for them (an AI or a person) | the substrate: the author's intent made explicit |
| **Communicating** | the sender and the recipient | the text: what the recipient actually receives |

**Every alignment claim names its relationship.**
- **Hawaii, January 2018.** An emergency officer sent "BALLISTIC MISSILE THREAT INBOUND TO HAWAII. SEEK IMMEDIATE SHELTER. THIS IS NOT A DRILL."
  - The investigation found the officer "misunderstood a drill as a true emergency". "The drill incorrectly included the language 'This is not a drill.'"
  - **The drafting relationship failed.** A drill instruction that contradicted itself led the officer to misread the situation.
  - **The communicating relationship worked exactly as written.** People understood and sought shelter.
  - **The outcome failed,** against what the agency actually intended.
  - Without two relationships, these cannot be told apart.

  ([FCC report summary](https://blog.npstc.org/?p=6859); [MPR News](https://www.mprnews.org/story/2018/01/30/hawaiii-worker-thought-missile-alert-real))
- **When the author writes their own message,** the drafting relationship collapses into one person. The substrate is then a thinking aid, not an alignment artifact.

**Why the mapping fits so well.** Communication is the medium of alignment, and alignment is the purpose of communication.
- AlignCraft's pillars start by stating intent, context and boundaries so that they can be aligned, and stating something to someone is communication.
- Every communication tries to make the recipient's understanding agree with the sender's.

Applying AlignCraft to communication is not an analogy. It is the same problem seen from its two sides.

### 5. The six layers in communication

| Layer | AlignCraft's question (quoted) | In communication | Compared: what, against what |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Workspace** | "does the person doing the work understand the environment they're operating in?" | the situation: the people, the history, the current facts and sources, conventions, access | the drafter's understanding, against the actual situation |
| **Task** | "what are we doing and why?" | what the communication must achieve and convey, for whom; the expected effect | the understood task, against the need and the intent |
| **Action-space** | "where do we act? … which region of the solution space" | the approach: a direct answer, a comparison, an example, a narrative, a question first; its genre and channel | the chosen approach, against what the task needs, among the alternatives |
| **Action-set** | "what exactly are the steps? … Each action should be unambiguous" | the particular contribution: the meanings, their order, emphasis, wording, delivery | what was produced, against what was meant |
| **Coherence** | "Are the defined actions aligned with the existing aligned structure?" | the communication against what already exists: its own other parts; earlier messages; other audiences; commitments | each part or message, against the structure it joins |
| **Outcome** | "Did the result actually solve the original problem?" | uptake (what was understood) and effect (what was then done) | the observed result, against the original need |

**All six alignments are required for every communication draft.**
- None is optional; what varies is how much work each takes.
- Outcome alignment is built into the draft before sending (the intended effect, and how it would be recognised), and confirmed after the recipient responds.

**Each layer can fail while the others hold.** Constructed cases:
- a clear update with yesterday's figure (workspace);
- a polished answer to the wrong question (task);
- correct content told as an investigation diary to someone who needs a decision (action-space);
- a comparison that drops its limiting condition (action-set);
- a promise that conflicts with commitments already made (coherence);
- a message understood and then not acted on (outcome).

**Your reading of each layer.** This table checks your description of what each layer means for communication against AlignCraft's definition. "Right" means your description of the layer is correct. It says nothing about whether any particular draft is aligned.

| Layer | Your words | Is your reading right? |
|---|---|---|
| Workspace | "the communication context and the environment like the past data … communication substrate and maybe context" | **Right,** for context, environment and past data. Refined: the workspace is the actual situation; the substrate is where the drafter's understanding of it is written down. |
| Task | "the communication substrate is not defined enough … why we are communicating … what we are trying to convey" | **Right.** A task misalignment shows up as an under-defined substrate. |
| Action-space | "we don't know which action space we should use … understand better what this maps exactly" | **Answered:** it is the approach. The need and meaning in the substrate do not settle it; it is a separate choice. |
| Action-set | "different ways to communicate things … which one is preferable" | **Partly right.** Choosing among broad ways is the approach (action-space). Choosing the particular acts that are "preferable and feasible" is the action-set. |
| Coherence | "disturb some other communication … in the future … someone else" | **Right,** for coherence beyond the communication. It also applies inside the communication, which is where harmony sits. |
| Outcome | "what it will actually cause … opposite of what we intended" | **Right.** It splits into uptake (what was understood) and effect (what was then done). |

**Outside grounding:**
- **Weaver's three levels of communication problems:**
  - technical (do the symbols arrive?) → delivery and access;
  - semantic (is the meaning conveyed precisely?) → the action-set's explicitness;
  - effectiveness ("How effectively does the received meaning affect conduct in the desired way?") → the outcome's effect.
- **Austin:** uptake (the force understood) and perlocution (the effect achieved) are the outcome's two parts.
- **Grice's maxims:**
  - Quality → the workspace;
  - Quantity → coverage;
  - Relation → the task, and bearing on the point;
  - Manner → the action-set and the path of the telling.

**Six is not proven enough.** Six real cases fitted with nothing left over (section 11). A pure task failure has only constructed cases. AlignCraft's claim that misalignment "can only occur at these six layers" stays untested for communication.

### 6. Where harmony sits

**Harmony is the alignment whose references lie inside the communication.** It has two parts, along the line 21-40 itself drew between "three things stay one" and "one thing matches".

| Part | Harmony's kinds | AlignCraft layer | The other side is quoted from |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Internal coherence** ("stay one") | content; the path of the telling; the skin; who-to-whom | coherence, inside the communication | the communication's other parts |
| **The telling matching its account** ("matches") | due weight; the text matching its substrate | action-set | the account: what was meant, and how much each part matters |

**AlignCraft's coherence failures are harmony's kinds:**

| AlignCraft's failure | Harmony's kind |
|---|---|
| "duplicate functionality" | the path: one topic told twice |
| "contradict established patterns" | the skin; who-to-whom |
| "introduce inconsistency" | content |

**Both parts appear separately on real texts.**
- **NASA's Columbia slide** carried the title "Review of Test Data Indicates Conservatism for Tile Penetration". The engineers' real finding sat at the bottom: one debris estimate was "640 times larger" than the data used to calibrate the model.
  - The title clashes with its own bottom line: **internal coherence.**
  - The emphasis inverts what mattered most: **the match.**
- **Spin in trial abstracts.**
  - Conclusions go beyond the paper's own results: **internal coherence.**
  - Subgroups are stressed over the primary outcome: **the match.**

**Why this is the part of alignment that can be checked from the communication alone.** Both parts' references are inside the composed communication. This is 21-40's internal criterion, now explained.

**Harmony's earlier findings are unchanged:**
- the definition;
- the five kinds;
- the levels;
- the change test;
- the repair rule.

Only its place changed: from the umbrella to one member.

### 7. The earlier concepts, and the three meaning gaps

| Earlier concept | Its reference | Where it sits |
|---|---|---|
| **fidelity**: agreement with the record | the record and sources | workspace; coherence with commitments on record |
| **fit**: agreement with the situation | the request, the relationship, the occasion | task (the meaning answers the need); action-space (the approach suits); action-set (the register suits) |
| **intelligibility**: the text agrees with its intended meaning | the intended meaning | action-set |
| **completeness** | what the task requires | coverage, at the task and the action-set |
| **adequacy** | all of the above | all six alignments holding, at the task's scope |

**"Fit" crosses three layers because it was named by its reference,** the situation, which is met at three stages. That is one reference at several stages, not a mixture.

**Completeness stops being "the exception".** 21-40 said it "concerns whether something is present at all". Under alignment, an absence is a mismatch between what the task requires and what was produced. It can be quoted: "requirement R, with no answer in the text".

**The three meaning gaps are the references the comparisons need:**

| Gap | Whose reference | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| **the need** | the task layer's (and later the outcome's) | the task cannot be compared until the need is explicit |
| **the meaning** | the substrate's content | the action-set's match cannot run until the meaning is held and quotable |
| **conveyance** | what the action-set's product makes available | the explicitness detector checks one corner of it |

The three-gaps finding called the need and the meaning "the two abstract things every comparison relies on". In AlignCraft's terms, they are context that must be uncovered before anything can be aligned against them. The gaps' own closure work is still open (Next Actions).

**Gaps you didn't know you had:**
- **The approach (action-space).** Choosing how to communicate is an alignment of its own. Better.com laid off about 900 staff on one Zoom call ("If you're on this call, you are part of the unlucky group that is being laid off"). Its chief executive later wrote: "I realize that the way I communicated this news made a difficult situation worse." ([Al Jazeera](https://www.aljazeera.com/amp/economy/2021/12/8/better-boss-apologizes-for-mass-firing-900-workers-on-zoom-call))
- **Coherence beyond the communication.** A message must fit earlier messages, what other audiences were told, and existing commitments. United Airlines' public apology and its chief executive's internal email disagreed.
- **Outcome.** What was understood (uptake) differs from what was then done (effect). Neither can be observed before the communication happens.

### 8. The pillars, and what detection can check

**What the pillars are for** (this subsection was corrected after your review)

What must be aligned is usually hidden:
- in the author's head;
- in the environment;
- in earlier work;
- in what the recipient already has.

The four pillars are AlignCraft's way of uncovering that context so it can be aligned. They apply at every layer, in both relationships.

| Pillar | AlignCraft's gloss | What it uncovers, in communication |
|---|---|---|
| **Explicitness** | "State it clearly. Intent, context, and boundaries are documented, not assumed." | the situation, the purpose, the approach and why, what exactly must be said, what must be preserved, the intended effect. All stated, not left in someone's head. |
| **Visibility** | "Make it observable. All relevant state, data flows, and transformations are accessible." | that stated context, and the actual state (sources, the thread, earlier messages, commitments, and later the recipient's response), made accessible to whoever has to align with it: the drafter, the author, a reviewer |
| **Measurement** | "Make it verifiable. Differences between intent and outcome are quantifiable, not vague." | how a difference between what was meant and what happened would show. Qualitative is fine: Workcraft says "Measurement can be qualitative". |
| **Comparison** | "Check intent vs outcome. Continuously compare what was meant against what was produced." | the comparison itself, made at each layer |

**Two examples, at two layers:**
- **At the workspace layer,** the drafter needs the situation stated and the sources accessible. Otherwise they work from assumptions about it.
- **At the outcome layer,** the intended effect is stated before sending, and the actual response is made accessible afterwards.

**The pillars are not the project's explicitness detector.** The shared word "explicitness" hides two different things:
- **The pillar Explicitness** is about uncovering context: stating intent, context and boundaries so they can be aligned.
- **The explicitness detector** checks one property of a finished text: whether its wording singles out the particular thing it demands. It is one check at the action-set layer (can the recipient tell what is meant?). It is not a pillar. Nor is it "the base pillar", which an earlier version of this finding claimed.

**What detection can reach.** A check can only compare what has been uncovered.

| Alignment | Reference it needs | From the text alone? | Today |
|---|---|---|---|
| action-set: explicitness | the text's own demands | **yes** | **measured** (the explicitness detector) |
| coherence inside the communication | its own parts | **yes** | defined, not built |
| action-set: matches its account | the substrate | no | a concept (the materialization check) |
| task | the need, the request | no | guides only |
| workspace | sources, the record | no | guides only |
| coherence beyond it | other messages, commitments | no | nothing |
| outcome | replies, readback | no; only afterwards | Pulse (a concept) |

**So "we run the detections to check these six alignments" holds as a direction, with one precondition.**
- Each alignment can be checked once its context has been uncovered, which is the pillars' work at that layer.
- The substrate is where much of that uncovered context is written down for a drafter.

**A detector gap is not uncovered context.**
- A gap shows only that the text does not single something out.
- It does not show whether that thing was ever established. It may be known to the author and simply unwritten, or never worked out at all.
- Telling those apart means going back to the context, which is pillar work, not detection.
- An earlier version of this finding claimed the detector "elicits" missing context. That claim is withdrawn.

**One rule for every finding:**

> relationship · layer · this (quoted from what was produced) differs from that (quoted from where its reference lives).

- Harmony's clash rule is the case where "that" is quoted from the communication itself.
- The rule is also how a failure is placed: the layer is the one whose reference can be quoted on the other side.
- It carries your ban on "is it harmonious?" over to "is it aligned?".

### 9. The substrate as an alignment artifact

**The substrate is the drafting relationship's alignment artifact.** AlignCraft says such a document "aligns human intent with AI understanding at a particular level", and gives four criteria for a good one:

| AlignCraft's criterion | In the substrate |
|---|---|
| "Captures a decision, not just information" | each entry carries its status: settled, open, or proposed by an AI and unconfirmed |
| "Has a clear level" | each entry is marked with the layer it serves |
| "Is verifiable" | each entry can be quoted at check time |
| "Is current" | its facts are checked against the present state |

**What each layer asks the substrate to hold:**

| Layer | What the substrate holds |
|---|---|
| Workspace | the situation and its grounds |
| Task | the contribution and its requirements |
| Action-space | **the approach and why** (new) |
| Action-set | the intended meanings, order and emphasis |
| Coherence | **what must be preserved elsewhere** (new) |
| Outcome | **the intended effect, and how it would be recognised** (new) |

The three entries marked "new" do not exist in today's substrate forms (the 17-45 brief and the 19-31 point structure).

**Its most important entry is the intent.** Military orders carry the commander's intent, the purpose and the end state, so subordinates can act in alignment when the plan meets reality. A drafter who holds the communication's intent can make aligned choices where nothing was written down.

**The limit.** An aligned substrate is not a guarantee. A correct account can still be expressed badly, delivered wrongly, or understood differently (08-07).

### 10. Names

| Name | Use | Test |
|---|---|---|
| **"alignment"** | the family | **Your word, and your methodology's.** Dictionary: "a state of agreement … among persons", "the proper adjustment of the components … for coordinated functioning". **Risks:** the AI-safety sense, and "a straight line" read as sameness. Context and AlignCraft's gloss settle both. |
| **"communication alignment"** | the whole, as applied to communication | 08-07's term. Plain. |
| **"drafting alignment" / "communicating alignment"** | the two relationships | Plain, with the gloss author–drafter / sender–recipient. |
| **the member: your choice** | harmony's self-checkable part | **"harmony":** your word, now with an exact place; you disliked it as generic. <br>**"internal coherence":** AlignCraft's word, scoped. **Risk:** read as meaning only, leaving out tone and names; use it with the gloss "the communication fits with itself in meaning, path, surface and address". <br>**"belonging together":** plain, covers all four, and 21-40's recommendation. |

### 11. Evidence from real texts

**What was read.** Three new real texts, plus three carried from 07-57, each read against its published reactions or official findings. None was used to build the model. The placements are mine, tied to the quoted words.

| Real text | What was said (verbatim) | Relationship · layer |
|---|---|---|
| **Better.com, December 2021** | "If you're on this call, you are part of the unlucky group that is being laid off"; "this is inhumane"; "I realize that the way I communicated this news made a difficult situation worse" | communicating · **action-space** (the approach and channel) |
| **NASA Columbia slide, 2003** | "Review of Test Data Indicates Conservatism for Tile Penetration"; the "640 times larger" estimate sat at the bottom; Tufte blamed "the cognitive style of powerpoint" | communicating · **action-set** (the match: emphasis), **coherence inside** (title against data), **action-space** (slides instead of a report), **outcome** (uptake) |
| **Hawaii false alert, January 2018** | "THIS IS NOT A DRILL"; the officer "misunderstood a drill as a true emergency"; "The drill incorrectly included the language 'This is not a drill'" | **drafting:** coherence inside the instruction, then the workspace. **Communicating:** worked as written. **Outcome:** failed. |
| **Spin in trial abstracts** (carried) | "spun their statistically nonsignificant conclusions in a positive way" | communicating · **coherence inside** and **the match** |
| **United Airlines, April 2017** (carried) | "I apologize for having to re-accommodate these customers" against "disruptive and belligerent" | communicating · **coherence beyond the communication** |
| **The UK's guidance, 11 May 2020** (carried) | "go to work" with "avoid public transport if possible" | communicating · **task and workspace** (fit with recipients' circumstances) |

**What the six show:**
- Every complaint is placed.
- Both relationships appear.
- Both parts of harmony appear separately.
- Every layer except a pure task failure has a real case. The UK case shows the task layer, but through the recipients' situation.

**Sources:**
- [Better.com: Entrepreneur](https://www.entrepreneur.com/business-news/bettercom-ceo-lays-off-900-employees-during-zoom-meeting)
- [Columbia slide: "Death by PowerPoint"](https://mcdreeamiemusings.com/blog/2019/4/13/gsux1h6bnt8lqjd7w2t2mtvfg81uhx)
- [Columbia slide: Gigazine](https://gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20190506-powerpoint-slide-killed-seven-people)
- [Hawaii false alert: FCC report summary](https://blog.npstc.org/?p=6859)

### 12. The choices left to you

1. **Confirm that the unit choice is dissolved:** the whole-situation concept you meant is communication alignment, and harmony is its self-checkable member.
2. **Choose the member's name:** "harmony", "internal coherence" (with its gloss), or "belonging together".

## Reasoning

**Killed, and what each kill taught:**
- **Renaming harmony "alignment".** It mixes the internal reference with external ones. On the Columbia slide it would erase the difference between the title clashing with its data and the emphasis burying what mattered. **Lesson:** the family needs its members distinct.
- **Zero layers:** the family named by reference is enough. It fails: the approach (Better.com) and the outcome (Hawaii) have no reference-named home. **Lesson:** the layers add stages that references alone miss.
- **One relationship.** It fails: the drafter's failures (silent decisions, parts with no home, a misread situation) are real and separate. **Lesson:** AI drafting makes the drafter a party.
- **Three relationships,** with "the work" as a third party. It fails: the work has no understanding to align. It is the existing structure that coherence compares against. **Lesson:** a relationship needs two understandings; a structure is a reference.
- **Harmony stays the umbrella.** It ignores your claim and leaves the unit choice open.
- **Keeping "harmony" as the family's name.** Your dislike of the word, and the internal member would be left unnamed.
- **`corrects` 21-40.** 21-40's family statement is confirmed and named, not overturned. **Lesson:** naming an unnamed family is a refinement.

**Survived, and why each held:**
- **The family reading.**
  - Workcraft's own definition of Alignment, 11-24's division, and AlignCraft's Comparison pillar all describe the same comparison of intended with actual (premise test FP-1).
  - 08-07 independently gave "communication alignment" the broader role.
- **The two relationships.** Hawaii separates them on a real case.
- **Harmony's two-part place.** Columbia and spin show both parts separately. 08-07 had already said the kinds "do not all map exclusively to Coherence".
- **The layers.** Six real texts were placed with nothing left over, and an independent run (08-07) found the same six questions.
- **The generalised clash rule and what detection can reach.** They extend rules already in use, and they claim no detection beyond what exists.
- **The substrate as an alignment artifact.** AlignCraft's own four criteria apply, and the three missing entries have a real case (Better.com's unexamined approach).

**Refined:**
- **"Internal coherence" as a name.** It is usable only with its gloss, because of the risk of a meaning-only reading.

**Withdrawn after your review: the four pillars confused with the project's checking tools.**
- **What happened.** Sensemaking, innovation and critique mapped each pillar onto a tool the project already had:
  - **Explicitness** onto the substrate and the explicitness detector;
  - **Visibility** onto the quoting rule;
  - **Measurement** onto the detector's answer values;
  - **Comparison** onto "every check is a comparison".
- **What was then claimed:**
  - "the project's pipeline is the pillar chain, applied twice";
  - the detector "is the base pillar";
  - the detector "elicits" missing context.

  The critique even used the detector's build history as evidence.
- **Why it was wrong.** The match was made through the shared word "explicitness", and by fitting a new idea onto existing tools, not by purpose.
  - The pillars are how context is uncovered so it can be aligned, at every layer.
  - The detector checks one property of a finished text, at one layer.
- **Why it matters.** The mistake would point building effort at the text detector as if it were the foundation of alignment. The real pillar work, uncovering the context at each layer, would be left undone.
- **What changed.** Sections 1 and 8 and the summary are corrected. The pillar claims and the detector-sorting COULD are withdrawn.

**Where this inquiry corrected itself:**
- **Surfacing found 08-07, a completed traverse on the same request.** The inquiry was re-scoped to build on it and re-test it, instead of repeating the mapping.
- **Sensemaking's first impression** placed all of harmony under AlignCraft's coherence. The structure of 21-40 itself ("stay one" against "matches") showed that due weight and the substrate match belong to the action-set instead.

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

- **Do your marks match the AI's,** with relationships and layers? Observable after the marking MUST.
- **Is "alignment" read in its AI-safety sense** by colleagues? Observable in the first three uses.
- **Do the layer labels add effort without adding understanding?** Record when they do (08-07's monitoring item, carried).

### Blocked

- **The member's name** waits on your choice.
- **The unit choice** waits on your confirmation.

### Research Frontiers

- **Chains of relationships.** How an alignment failure travels from an instruction to a drafter to a recipient, as in Hawaii.
- **Whether six layers are enough,** including a real pure task failure, live talk, and exchanges between AI agents.
- **How the outcome can be observed for human recipients,** beyond what readback gives.

### Refinement Triggers

- **Revise the layers** if a real communication failure cannot be placed in any of the six by the clash rule.
- **Revise harmony's two-part split** if a real clash cannot be decided as either internal (the other side quoted from the communication) or a match (quoted from the account).
- **Revise the family reading** if you say that "harmony" should name the whole-situation concept after all.

Welcome
AlignCraft is a stack of six fundamental alignment layers for AI-driven action — applicable to AI-Human and AI-AI collaboration, with a focus on software development.

This book presents AlignCraft as a structured methodology with repeatable patterns that bridge the gap between coding by feel and engineering discipline. It starts with one observation:

When you delegate work to AI, any misalignment can only occur at these six layers:

Workspace Alignment — The environment and context aren't set up correctly
Task Alignment — The task is not understood well in terms of intent, depth and scope.
Action-Space Alignment — AI doesn't know what action space should be used
Action-Set Alignment — AI doesn't understand what set of actions is preferable and feasible
Coherence Alignment — AI doesn't understand how the actions taken disturb existing alignments
Outcome Alignment — AI doesn't understand how actions taken and expected results are in mismatch
AlignCraft provides patterns for maintaining alignment across all six levels — primarily for AI-assisted software engineering.

Unlike pure vibe coding, AlignCraft offers systematic, repeatable foundations and fine-grained control at every stage of development.

This book doesn't introduce new concepts. Instead, it codifies what many developers already do instinctively, turning scattered practices into a cohesive methodology.

The Four Pillars
Alignment requires Comparison.

Comparison requires Measurement.

Measurement requires Visibility.

Visibility requires Explicitness.

These four pillars define what we need at each layer to maintain alignment. Applied across all six alignment layers, and combined with iteration, they form the foundation of AlignCraft

The rest of this book explains how to implement them in your workflows.

Why AlignCraft?
As software developers, we navigate real requirements, team dynamics, and technical constraints while balancing timelines, quality standards, and accountability.

Recently we face immense pressure in our craft to harness the power of AI-assisted coding. Yet many treat AI as just a syntax generator, missing out on 10x productivity gains. Others hand over control entirely, inheriting brittle code and hidden vulnerabilities.

AlignCraft bridges this gap. An evolving framework for true developer-AI partnership without the compromises.

## Correction note (added 2026-10-10, from a later inquiry)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_11-29__alignment-checks-and-textual-integrity-in-the-commcraft-design/finding.md refines this finding and corrects one placement.

- **Corrected: harmony's internal kinds are not at the coherence layer.**
  - **What section 6 did:** it placed content, the path, the skin and who-to-whom at "coherence, inside the communication", and matched AlignCraft's coherence failures to them.
  - **What AlignCraft says:** coherence is fit "with what already exists": "A plan can be correct in isolation and still be wrong in context". Its cost table reads "3 - Action-Set | Right approach, wrong steps" and "4 - Coherence | Right steps, poor fit".
  - **So:** a text whose own parts contradict each other fails at the action-set. The kinds are now **the consistency part of the text's integrity, at the action-set layer**.
  - **The table "AlignCraft's coherence failures are harmony's kinds" is withdrawn.** It matched AlignCraft's failure words, not its definition. That is the same kind of shared-word mapping as the withdrawn pillar mapping.
- **Refined: checks of a text in itself are the reader's side of alignment.**
  - Explicitness, and whether the parts belong together, are judged against the rules writer and reader share: "textual integrity", the base reference.
  - They are checked from the text and its declared setting.
  - They detect. The six-layer comparisons decide, against settled meaning: a settled substrate entry, or the author, and never an unconfirmed AI proposal.
- **Confirmed:**
  - alignment as the family;
  - the two relationships;
  - the six layers, at both of the design's checks;
  - all six alignments required for every draft;
  - the pillars as how context is uncovered so it can be aligned;
  - the detector at the action-set, now as the text's integrity check for determinacy;
  - the match (due weight against the substrate's rank; the text matching its substrate) at the action-set.
- **Still open from this finding:**
  - confirming that the unit choice is dissolved;
  - the through-line test.
- **The member's name** is now chosen with the name for the reader's side of alignment (the later finding, section 12).


## Further note (added 2026-10-10, from a later inquiry)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_13-28__something-is-off-base-references-and-workspace-alignment/finding.md refines this finding.

- **The placement rule** "the layer is the one whose reference can be quoted on the other side" gets a second coordinate for standing rules: rules about what every text of a kind is expected to be.
  - **Such a finding names two things:**
    - the rule, which lives in the workspace's base reference;
    - the layer the rule is about (for example, the action-set for determinacy).
  - **For particular references** (the substrate, the request), the two coordinates coincide, as before.
- **"What was meant" has two scopes:**
  - **standing:** what every text of its kind is expected to be;
  - **particular:** what is meant for this text.
  - Both run across the same six layers. There is no seventh layer.


## Note (added 2026-10-10: standalone correction)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_13-28__something-is-off-base-references-and-workspace-alignment/finding.md (its second iteration) records that this project is standalone.

- **Outside this project:** this finding's references to another local project's product, used as a worked example for comparisons, and to other projects' guides.
- **Worked examples** for these comparisons should come from this project's own texts, or from outside public texts.
- **Unchanged:** the layers, the two relationships and the pillars' reading.
