# Finding: alignment checks and textual integrity in the Communication Craft design

## Changes from Prior

**Prior paths:**
- **Main:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_09-00__harmony-as-alignment-aligncraft-layers-in-communication/finding.md. Called 09-00 below. It named alignment as the family of all communication qualities and placed harmony inside it.
- **Second:** devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_13-08__materialization-check-vs-content-check-in-communication-craft/finding.md. Called 13-08 below. It split checks into "is the meaning well expressed?" and "is the meaning right?".

**Revision trigger.** There were two.
- **Your new diagram, and your one doubt about it:** whether a check of a text "in itself" is alignment, given that "alignment … means it should be aligned with something".
- **New evidence gathered for this inquiry:**
  - AlignCraft's chapters on the alignment chain, alignment documents, precise wording and ground rules, read in full;
  - four outside sources on soundness against fit (listed in section 13).

**What's preserved:**
- **From 09-00:**
  - alignment as the family;
  - the two relationships (drafting, communicating);
  - the six layers and the thing each is compared against;
  - all six alignments required for every draft;
  - the four pillars as how context is uncovered so it can be aligned;
  - the explicitness detector at the action-set layer;
  - the rule for writing a finding.
- **From 13-08:**
  - its two checks, which turn out to be the diagram's two alignment checks;
  - its rule that problems are classified "by what is at fault, not by which tool found them".

**What's changed:**
- **Harmony's internal kinds move.** These are content, the path, the skin and who-to-whom. 09-00 placed them at AlignCraft's coherence layer. They are now the consistency part of the text's integrity, at the action-set layer. 09-00's table "AlignCraft's coherence failures are harmony's kinds" is withdrawn as a mapping by shared words (section 11).
- **"From the text alone" becomes "from the text and its declared setting".** It describes what an integrity check needs.
- **13-08's declaration rule gains one field.** Each check also declares what its reference stands for.
- **09-00's way of writing a finding gains a form for integrity findings:** the rule broken, plus the quoted candidates, marked "detected, not decided".

**What's new:**
- the "something" for every check;
- integrity as the reader's side of alignment;
- textual integrity defined, with its base reference;
- a test that sorts any check (the substitution test);
- detect, then decide against settled meaning;
- a placement for every check discussed;
- the twelve layer comparisons with an integrity step in each check;
- the design key;
- a name test.

**Migration:**
- dated notes appended to 09-00, 13-08, and 21-40's alignment note;
- the memory note on communication alignment updated.

These are done at conclusion; see Next Actions.

**MUST/COULD drift** (compared with 09-00's actions):

1. **The dated notes (MUST).**
   - **Prior text:** "Append dated notes to 07-57, 08-07 and 21-40".
   - **New text:** "Append dated notes to 09-00, 13-08 and 21-40's alignment note; update the memory note".
   - **Rationale:** this finding corrects one placement in 09-00 and refines 13-08.

2. **Marking real messages (MUST).**
   - **Prior text:** "Mark two or three real messages, writing each finding as 'relationship · layer · this against that', with both sides quoted".
   - **New text:** the same, plus for each mark: could it be decided from the text and its declared setting (integrity), or did it need another object (comparison)?
   - **Rationale:** it tests this finding's answer on your own judgment.

3. **The member's name (MUST).**
   - **Prior text:** "Choose the member's name ('harmony', 'internal coherence' or 'belonging together')".
   - **New text:** "Choose the name for the reader's side of alignment ('textual integrity' with its gloss, 'readable as meant', 'well-formed', or no single name). Its consistency part keeps 'belonging together' as a candidate."
   - **Rationale:** the member is now part of integrity. "Internal coherence" would also suggest the coherence layer, which section 11 corrects.

4. **The one-page reference (COULD).**
   - **Prior text:** "Write a one-page alignment reference covering the family, the two relationships, the six layers … what detection can reach".
   - **New text:** the same, plus the base reference, the substitution test, the integrity step in each check, and pillar wording that names no project tool.
   - **Rationale:** the frame gained these parts.

5. **The internal-coherence questions (COULD).**
   - **Prior text:** "Build the internal-coherence questions (content, the path, the skin, who-to-whom) as quoted-pair checks".
   - **New text:** "Build the consistency checks as the text's integrity step". It includes only the reading-relevant part of the skin, and due weight against the text's own declared importance.
   - **Rationale:** re-placed and split (sections 8 and 11).

6. **The substrate trial (COULD).**
   - **Prior text:** "Form a substrate for a real communication with the three new entries".
   - **New text:** the same, plus reading-frame entries, and a status that keeps "open" apart from "left open on purpose".
   - **Rationale:** the integrity step needs both.

**Carried from 09-00 unchanged:**
- confirming that the unit choice is dissolved;
- the through-line test.

## Question

**Your message (verbatim in Source Input):**
> "I want you to consume this … diagram, explicitly explains like what we are trying to build. … There is a raw context. There is a substrate. And there are two alignment checks. … one alignment check is regarding the substrate versus the raw context … other one is between the materialization versus the … communication substrate. And when we talk about alignment checks, it means the six layers of alignment. And they also include … references, for example, how to write an operational communication … how a communication message should be structured and how communication message should be in harmony … the first paragraph and second paragraph are in harmony … and then like how it is formatted …"

**Your one doubt, in your words:**
> "check often if a text has explicitness or … checking if a text is has integrity in itself is this alignment or not or is this the quality or is this something else because alignment It means it should be aligned with something"

**Your possible answer:**
> "we can maybe define like textual integrity … it will be like a base reference for alignment … it's a text message, so we expect this text message to have textual integrity. So it is actually about alignment … of the workspace"

**The goal.** A conceptual account of six things:
- what the two alignment checks are and include;
- whether checks of a text against itself are alignment, quality or something else, with a rule that decides it;
- where every check discussed so far belongs, including its layer;
- what "textual integrity" is and does;
- what this reveals about the design;
- names.

You said the diagram "is definitely correct … for now", so it is treated as fixed.

## Finding Summary

**Your question, answered in one line**
- **A check of a text "in itself" is part of alignment: the reader's side of it.** It is aligned with something, namely how the text will be read. It is judged against the rules writer and reader share, not against another object.

**What the "something" is**
- **For the diagram's two checks:** the other object.
- **For explicitness, or a text's integrity:** the reading. That is, how anyone with only the text and its declared setting will take it.
- **AlignCraft already puts this inside alignment:**
  - its action-set layer requires "Each action should be unambiguous enough that someone could execute it without asking clarifying questions";
  - "A document full of ambiguous terms is a false checkpoint."

**Alignment, quality, or something else?**
- **Both alignment and quality.** Integrity is the quality a document needs for alignment to pass through it. AlignCraft calls a vague document "a broken alignment instrument".
- **Not a separate family.** An integrity check can only *detect*. Deciding whether a detection is really a fault, and what must change, needs the other object.

**Textual integrity, made exact: your proposal holds**
- **Definition.** The text can be read as meant by the rules writer and reader share:
  - each part carries one meaning (what it refers to, and what it does: request, report, offer, decision);
  - the parts belong together;
  - its form is one the medium can show.
- **The base reference.** Those shared rules, in two parts:
  - a **standing core**, the same for every text;
  - a **declared part**: the kind of text, the medium, and what the reader has already been given.

  It never includes what a particular reader happens to know.
- **Where the base reference lives.** In the workspace, as AlignCraft's "Universal Rules" and "What conventions are in place". But checking a text against it is not workspace alignment. Workspace alignment asks whether the drafter understands the situation, which is a different question.

**The test that sorts any check**
- **Ask:** if this were broken, would a reader with only the text and its declared setting take a different meaning, or none?
  - **Yes:** an **integrity check**. It needs one object, and it detects.
  - **No, but the text fails what someone meant, needs or must fit:** a **layer comparison**. It needs two objects, and it decides.

**Where each check goes**
- **Explicitness detection:** the text's integrity, at the **fourth layer (action-set)**, where AlignCraft itself demands unambiguous acts.
  - Not the third (the approach) or the fifth (fit with what already exists).
  - Those two are where your *references* sit.
- **Harmony between paragraphs:** the text's integrity (consistency), also at the action-set.
  - **The text matching its substrate is a comparison,** at the second check.
- **Formatting:**
  - **integrity,** when it is about whether the medium can show it;
  - **a comparison,** when it is house style.
- **The operational writing guide:** each item is asked twice.
  - "Was it formed?" at the first check.
  - "Is it stated?" at the second.
- **Fidelity and fit:** comparisons.

**What it reveals about the design (the key)**
- **Each check asks two things of the document it produced:**
  - Can its reader take it as meant?
  - Does it agree with what came before, at all six layers?
- **Each document has its own reader:**
  - the drafter reads the substrate;
  - the recipient reads the text.
- **A detection is decided against settled meaning:** a settled substrate entry, or the author. Never an unconfirmed AI proposal.
- **The diagram needs no new box.** The outcome is confirmed after sending, past its right edge.

**A correction to 09-00**
- **What 09-00 did:** it placed harmony's internal kinds at AlignCraft's coherence layer, by matching AlignCraft's failure words.
- **What AlignCraft actually says:**
  - **coherence** is fit "with what already exists" ("Right steps, poor fit");
  - **a text's own consistency** is the action-set's soundness ("Right approach, wrong steps").
- **This is the same kind of shared-word error as the pillars mistake.** It is corrected here.

**Names: your choice**
- **"Textual integrity" works with its gloss, but two senses pull away from it:**
  - honesty;
  - in textual criticism, "transmitted … without material change", which means *matching the source*.
- **Alternatives:** "readable as meant"; "well-formed".

**Confidence**
- **Grounded in:** AlignCraft's text, the project's findings, and four outside sources.
- **Not yet tested on real texts.** That is the first set of MUST actions.

## Finding

### 1. Why this question

**Communication Craft** is this project's work on how messages are formed and checked. It is part of Workcraft, the project's framework for doing work with AI.

**The previous inquiry (09-00) set up the frame this one builds on.**
- Its finding concluded that every quality of a communication is a kind of **alignment**. Alignment is the concept at the centre of AlignCraft, your methodology for keeping delegated work matched to what was meant.
- That finding listed six layers of alignment:
  - **workspace:** the situation;
  - **task:** what the communication must achieve;
  - **action-space:** the approach;
  - **action-set:** the particular contribution;
  - **coherence:** fit with what already exists;
  - **outcome:** what was understood and done.

**You then drew the design.** It has three objects:
- **Raw Context:** everything the exchange starts from;
- **Comm Substrate:** the written account of what the message must achieve and say;
- **Materialisation:** the text.

It has two "Alignment Check" diamonds, one over each adjacent pair.

**You were confident in the design, with one doubt.** Explicitness detection checks a text against its own wording. Harmony checks a text's paragraphs against each other. Neither compares two objects. So are they alignment, or "the quality", or something else?

### 2. The diagram, read

The diagram ([on GitHub](https://github.com/karaposu/workcraft/blob/main/communicationcraft/benchmarks/explicitness/commcraft.drawio.png); a copy is saved in this inquiry's folder as `commcraft.drawio.png`) shows:

```
Raw Context  →  Comm Substrate  →  Materialisation
      ◇ Alignment Check ◇   ◇ Alignment Check ◇
```

**The two checks are the two checks the project already named in 13-08:**

| Diagram | 13-08's name | 13-08's description |
|---|---|---|
| Substrate against raw context | the content check, "is the meaning right?" | "the intended meaning, against sources, the request and the work" |
| Text against substrate | the materialization check, "is the meaning well expressed?" | "the text, against the intended meaning and the recipient's need" |

**Each check runs all six layers.** That is your "it means the six layers of alignment". Section 9 lists the twelve comparisons.

### 3. Every check has a "something"

**The project already counts a text compared with itself as a comparison.** The three-gaps finding (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-59__three-fundamental-meaning-gaps-of-communication-craft/finding.md) listed four comparisons. One was "the text … against what the text itself makes available".

**So the question is not whether such a check has a "something". It is what that something is.**

**The something is the reading: how the text will be taken.**
- Every rule an integrity check applies exists only because a reader must take the text as meant.
- A definite wording like "the change" must pick out one change.
- A text's parts must agree, or the reader cannot take one account.
- Without a reader, no rule has a point.

**AlignCraft says the same, in its own words:**
- **The action-set layer asks:** "Is the set of actions understood clearly? … Each action should be unambiguous enough that someone could execute it without asking clarifying questions."
- **On alignment documents:** "A document full of ambiguous terms is a false checkpoint. Both sides pass through it thinking they agree, but they're agreeing on different interpretations of the same words."
- **The explicitness work states the same principle from the other side:** "when a slot is left open, whoever fills it fills it fluently and wrongly".

**The reading is not a reader profile.**
- It is how *anyone* with only the text and its declared setting would take it.
- This keeps your earlier rule: detect the text's explicitness, never what a particular reader happens to know.
- The detector's contract says the same: "A property of the text, not of a reader."

### 4. Alignment, quality, or something else?

**Both alignment and quality, and the two words are not in conflict.**
- AlignCraft treats the precision of a document as what lets it align at all: "When you see documentation this way, the quality bar changes. A vague doc isn't just unhelpful — it's a broken alignment instrument."
- So integrity is a *quality* in AlignCraft's own sense: the quality bar for an alignment document.
- And it is *part of alignment*: the reader's side of it.

**It is not a separate family.** The tempting alternative is two families:
- alignment checks, between objects;
- integrity checks, of one object.

This was tested and rejected (Reasoning). The decisive reason:

- **An integrity check can only detect.**
  - On the project's trip benchmark, "about 90% of the explicitness detector's gaps could only be answered by the author" (13-08).
  - The non-ambiguity guide lists cases "When ambiguity is acceptable or necessary".
- **A separate family would issue verdicts it cannot ground.**
  - It cannot tell a real gap from intended openness.
  - It cannot say whether the text or the substrate must change.

**Integrity also comes before comparison.**
- If "the change" could be either of two changes, no comparison with the substrate's "the schema migration" can say whether the text carries it.
- This is the pattern XML names: "All valid documents must be well-formed."

**So integrity relates to the comparisons in two directions:**
- **a precondition:** no comparison can run where it fails;
- **a detector:** it shows the comparisons where to look.

### 5. Textual integrity and the base reference

**Definition.** Textual integrity means the text can be read as meant by the rules writer and reader share. It has three parts.

| Part | What it requires | Example failure |
|---|---|---|
| **Determinate** | Where the wording needs one particular thing, the text singles it out among those in play. This covers what each part refers to, and what each part does: request, report, offer, decision. | "Approve the change" when two changes are in play. A request that reads as a report. |
| **Consistent** | The parts belong together: none contradicts another or pulls against the whole. This is 21-40's test, "belonging, not sameness": a shift breaks consistency only when it does not serve the whole, or when it changes what the reader takes. | A title that contradicts the data beneath it. A thing renamed halfway, so the reader takes it as a new thing. |
| **Readable form** | Its form is one the declared medium can show. | A markdown table sent as a plain text message. |

**The base reference: the rules writer and reader share.**
- **The standing core is the same for every text:**
  - the language's rules;
  - a definite wording picks out one thing;
  - the parts belong together.
- **The declared part belongs to this exchange.** The three-gaps finding already lists what counts as supplied: "the text, the stated setting, earlier turns in the exchange, conventions of the genre, declared shared ground. What never counts: undeclared reader knowledge."

**The declared kind sets how strictly the core applies.**
- This is your own point: "it's a text message, so we expect this text message to have textual integrity".
- **Examples:**
  - a contract needs stricter determinacy;
  - a chat message can be looser in form;
  - a poem's deliberate ambiguity is openness that was declared.
- **When exactly a wording "needs" one particular thing remains the open question.** The three-gaps finding calls it "the boundary of the implicit". This finding locates it inside the determinacy rule; it does not solve it.

**Where the base reference lives: in the workspace.** AlignCraft keeps rules there:
- "Universal Rules: Apply to every project, every time";
- "Project Preferences: Specific to your tech stack, team, or domain";
- "convention guides", which lock down "The environment and its rules".

**Your "alignment of the workspace" is right about where the rules live.**

**But checking a text against them is not workspace alignment.**
- AlignCraft's workspace layer asks whether "the person doing the work understand[s] the environment they're operating in".
- An open slot in a fluent AI text rarely means the drafter misunderstood the environment. Most were meanings nobody had settled (the 90% above).
- Repairing them by briefing the drafter about the environment would miss.

### 6. Detect, then decide, against settled meaning

**The integrity step produces detections. The six-layer comparison decides each one.** There are three outcomes, as in the explicitness fix skill:

| Outcome | When |
|---|---|
| **fixed** | The substrate holds the meaning as a settled entry. The fix quotes it. |
| **asked** | The substrate is silent, or marks the point as still open. The question goes to the author. |
| **declined** | The text already supplies it, or the substrate marks it "left open on purpose". In that case the openness is made visible in the text, not resolved. |

**The deciding rule: a detection is decided against settled meaning.**
- Settled meaning is a substrate entry whose status is settled, or the author's own answer.
- It is never an entry an AI proposed that nobody confirmed. That keeps the project's rule that "an AI never settles its own units".

**When receiving a message,** settled meaning is the sender's. So detections become questions back.

**Pure form failures need no one's meaning.** A table the medium cannot show is fixed by restoring readable form to the same content.

**The substrate needs one distinction for this.** "Open" (not yet settled) must be kept apart from "left open on purpose" (intended openness). The project's status list already has "left open on purpose"; substrate forms should carry it.

### 7. The test that sorts any check

**The substitution test.**

> "If this were broken, would a reader who has only the text and its declared setting take a different meaning, or be unable to take one?"

- **Yes:** an **integrity check**.
  - It runs on one document and its declared setting.
  - It detects.
- **No, but the text fails what someone meant, needs, or must fit:** a **layer comparison**.
  - It needs both objects: the substrate, raw context, or later the response.
  - It decides.

**Then two more steps:**
- **Which object is checked?**
  - the substrate, at the first check;
  - the text, at the second;
  - the response, after sending.
- **Which layer?** The one whose reference can be quoted on the other side. This is 09-00's rule.

**The test excludes as well as includes.** That is why "everything is alignment" here does not mean "alignment means nothing". Two examples sorted as comparisons, not integrity:
- **House style,** such as British spelling: breaking it leaves the reading unchanged.
- **The author's dislike of unsolicited meeting offers:** the same.

**Each check declares three things.** This extends 13-08's rule "every check declares its kind":
- **(a)** what its reference stands for: integrity (the reader's means of reading) or a comparison, and against what;
- **(b)** where its findings can route: a rewrite from the substrate, or a question about content;
- **(c)** what it cannot vouch for.

**The explicitness bank, declared this way:**
- **(a)** integrity: determinacy;
- **(b)** findings route to a rewrite from a substrate quote, or to a question for the author;
- **(c)** it cannot vouch for whether the meaning is right.

### 8. Where each check goes

| Check | Kind | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **Explicitness detection** | integrity: determinate | the text, at the second check; **fourth layer, action-set** |
| **Harmony between parts:** content, the path, who-to-whom, and the parts of the skin that change what is taken (a renamed thing, a change of speaker) | integrity: consistent | the text, at the second check; action-set |
| **Harmony at the substrate** (07-57's "content and who-to-whom are decided" there) | integrity: consistent | the substrate, at the first check |
| **The skin's purely stylistic evenness** | comparison | the sender's voice and conventions, at the second check (coherence) |
| **Due weight, against the text's own declared importance** ("most importantly…" buried) | integrity: consistent | the text |
| **Due weight, against the substrate's rank; the text matching its substrate** | comparison | the second check, action-set |
| **Formatting the medium cannot show** | integrity: readable form | the text |
| **House style** | comparison | the second check, coherence ("contradict established patterns") |
| **Structure common to all texts** (a point, parts in an order the reader can follow) | integrity: consistent | the text |
| **Structure of the genre** (an operational update leads with its position) | comparison | the approach is chosen at the first check (action-space) and realised at the second |
| **The operational writing guide, items O1–O5** | comparison, asked twice | "formed?" at the first check; "stated?" at the second. Example: is there a position in the substrate, and does the text state it? |
| **Meaning and fidelity, M1–M7** | comparison | the first check (faithful to sources; answer formed) and the second (claims, status and corrections preserved) |
| **Fit to the recipient and purpose** | comparison | task, approach and register |
| **The author's preferences, E1–E3** | comparison (sender's side) | recorded in the substrate from raw context; checked at the second check |
| **The non-ambiguity guide** | detection is integrity; the verdict is a comparison | its "acceptable or necessary" list is the deciding step |
| **The substrate's own soundness** (AlignCraft: "Captures a decision", "Has a clear level", "Is verifiable") | integrity | the substrate, at the first check |
| **The substrate being current** (AlignCraft: "Is current") | comparison | workspace layer, at the first check |

**Your "third or fifth".**
- **You count the layers from one.** In your earlier message: "the third one is … action space … the fourth one is … action set … the fifth one is coherence".
- **The explicitness detector belongs at the fourth (action-set).** This is a match of purpose, not of words. AlignCraft wants each act carried out "without asking clarifying questions". The detector wants no slot left open for a reader to fill. AlignCraft gives the same reason: "The AI fills the gap with assumptions — and assumptions are where misalignment hides."
- **The detector sits at the action-set because the text is where every layer's content becomes an act of saying.** What it protects is every layer's content: a fact, a purpose, an ask or a commitment can each be left open.
- **Your guess is not wrong; it points at the references.**
  - **The third (action-space)** holds the choice of approach and genre, where "how to write an operational communication" is chosen.
  - **The fifth (coherence)** holds fit with earlier messages, commitments and the sender's conventions.

### 9. What each alignment check contains

**Each check has an integrity step for the document it produced, then six comparisons.**

| Layer | First check: the substrate against raw context | Second check: the text against the substrate |
|---|---|---|
| **Integrity step** | the substrate, read by the drafter: decisions captured, levels marked, entries verifiable, consistent and determinate; every entry has a status, with "left open on purpose" kept distinct | the text, read by the recipient: determinate, consistent, readable form, against the base reference |
| **Workspace** | the substrate's account of the situation (people, facts, what the reader can access, conventions in place) against the actual situation | the text's facts and assumptions against the substrate's account. *Example: the substrate has today's figure, and the text states yesterday's.* |
| **Task** | the substrate's need and purpose against the request and the author's intent | the text's evident purpose against the substrate's need. *Example: a report where a decision was needed.* |
| **Action-space** | the chosen approach and genre against what the need calls for, among the alternatives | the text's approach against the chosen one. *Example: an investigation diary where a comparison was chosen.* |
| **Action-set** | the selected meanings, reasons, limits, asks, order and rank against what raw context establishes. *Example: a reply's limits dropped from the account.* | the text's acts against the intended acts: due weight against the rank; every part has a home; nothing contradicts |
| **Coherence** | planned commitments against existing commitments, earlier messages and other audiences. *Example: "Friday delivery" against capacity already committed.* | the text against what the substrate says must be preserved, and against the sender's conventions |
| **Outcome** | the intended effect, and how it would be recognised, against the need. This is built in before sending. | the text's aim (the ask, the next step) against the intended effect |

**Where the references enter.**
- Your references (operational writing, structure, harmony, formatting) are inputs to both checks.
- The base reference is used in each integrity step.
- The genre's conventions are used in the action-space and action-set rows.
- The sender's conventions and preferences are used in the coherence rows.

**Checking the text directly against sources is allowed, as a tool.** Its findings are still located through the chain:
- **a stale fact in the substrate** is a fault at the first check;
- **a current substrate with a stale text** is a fault at the second.

**Where the pillars act.**
- AlignCraft's four pillars (Explicitness, Visibility, Measurement, Comparison) are how the context each check compares against is uncovered: stated, made accessible, made measurable, compared.
- They act on that context, before and around the checks.
- They are not the integrity step, and not the explicitness detector.
- No project tool is a pillar. That was the mistake corrected after your review of 09-00.

### 10. The design key

**Every document the craft produces has a reader:**
- the drafter reads the substrate;
- the recipient reads the text.

**So each alignment check asks two things of the document it produced:**
1. **Can its reader take it as meant?** This is integrity, checked against the base reference.
2. **Does it agree with what came before?** These are the six layers, checked against the particulars of this exchange.

**Alignment passes through documents.** A document that cannot be read one way breaks the chain even when everything upstream is right. This is AlignCraft's "false checkpoint".

**Three more things follow from this:**
- **Detections go upstream to settled meaning:**
  - the substrate's settled entries when drafting;
  - the author when the substrate is silent;
  - the sender when receiving.
- **The communicating relationship (sender and recipient) appears twice:**
  - before sending, as the text's integrity;
  - after sending, as the outcome. AlignCraft: outcome "happens after the work, not before". That comparison lies past the diagram's right edge.
- **Nothing new needs to be drawn.** The two diamonds already contain all of this. What changes is what each diamond is understood to hold.

**Three optional additions to the drawing, if you want them:**
- a small integrity step inside each diamond;
- the base reference as an input above both diamonds;
- a dashed outcome diamond past the right edge.

They are offered, not applied.

### 11. The correction to 09-00

**What 09-00 did.**
- It placed harmony's internal kinds (content, the path, the skin, who-to-whom) at AlignCraft's coherence layer, "inside the communication".
- It justified this with a table matching AlignCraft's coherence failures to harmony's kinds:
  - "duplicate functionality" → the path;
  - "contradict established patterns" → the skin, who-to-whom;
  - "introduce inconsistency" → content.

**What AlignCraft actually says about coherence.**
- It is fit with what already exists: "does this plan fit with what already exists? A plan can be correct in isolation and still be wrong in context."
- Its cost table separates the two cases:
  - "3 - Action-Set | Right approach, wrong steps | Partial rework — specific steps need fixing";
  - "4 - Coherence | Right steps, poor fit | Tech debt — works but doesn't belong".

**Why this decides it.**
- A coherence failure presupposes "Right steps".
- A text whose parts contradict each other does not have right steps. Its failure belongs to the action-set.

**How the error happened.**
- 09-00 matched AlignCraft's failure words to harmony's kinds without checking the layer's purpose. The purpose is the new work against an existing structure.
- That is the error you named after the pillars mistake: relating concepts by shared words.
- It was caught here by reading AlignCraft's chapter in full.

**What changes:**
- **Harmony's internal kinds** become the consistency part of the text's integrity, at the action-set.
- **Coherence** keeps everything beyond the communication: earlier messages, other audiences, commitments, the sender's conventions.
- **09-00's table "AlignCraft's coherence failures are harmony's kinds" is withdrawn.**

**What stays.** 09-00's other placements are confirmed:
- the match (due weight, the text matching its substrate) at the action-set;
- the detector at the action-set;
- the family, the relationships, the six layers and the pillars.

### 12. Names

**"Textual integrity", your term, tested first.**
- **Dictionary senses:**
  - "the quality of being honest and having strong moral principles";
  - "the state of being whole and undivided";
  - "internal consistency or lack of corruption in electronic data".
- **The second and third senses are what you mean.**
- **Two senses pull away from your meaning:**
  - **honesty.** 21-40 rejected "integrity" as a name for harmony for exactly this reason: "integrity: read as honesty".
  - **textual criticism.** There, integrity means the text "has been transmitted down to us without material change". That is a copy matching its source, which is *comparison*, the very thing integrity must be kept apart from.

**The alternatives, with their trade-offs:**

| Name | Trade-off |
|---|---|
| "textual integrity" | your word; usable only with its gloss: "the text can be read as meant by the rules writer and reader share: determinate, consistent, in readable form" |
| "readable as meant" | the definition as the name; plain; nothing leaks; long |
| "well-formed" | technical; can be heard as grammar only |
| no single name | name only the three parts, and call the step "the reading check" |

**Rejected:**
- **"readability":** a measure of reading ease, which collides with the recipient-and-purpose guide's item on reading effort;
- **"intelligibility",** as the umbrella: in 21-40 it also means agreement with the intended meaning, which needs the substrate;
- **"perspicuity":** obscure.

**For the two kinds of check inside each diamond:**
- "integrity check";
- "layer comparison".

### 13. Outside grounding

Four traditions outside the project separate a thing's own soundness from its fit to something particular. Each also puts the first in service of the second.

| Tradition | What it says | What it shows |
|---|---|---|
| **Grice, on conversation** | The maxims of Manner relate to "how what is said is to be said", not to "what is said": "Be perspicuous", "Avoid ambiguity". | Manner is separated from truth, amount and relevance. *Limit:* Grice also files "Be brief" under Manner. Under the substitution test, brevity depends on the need, so it is a comparison. Grice is cited only for the split. |
| **XML** | A document is well-formed under generic rules, and valid against its particular schema. "All valid documents must be well-formed." | Integrity as a precondition. |
| **Law: the vagueness doctrine** (Connally v. General Construction Co., 1926) | A statute fails when "men of common intelligence must guess at its meaning and differ as to its application". | It fails regardless of what the legislature meant. The standard is a reader of "common intelligence", not any one reader's knowledge. |
| **Compilers** | When a name could refer to more than one thing, the compiler reports an ambiguous reference and lists the candidates. The programmer, who knows the intent, then qualifies the name. | The same shape as an explicitness gap: one referent is needed, several are in play, the reader cannot choose, the author decides. |

**Data quality and text linguistics draw the same line:**
- Wang and Strong: "Intrinsic DQ: Data have quality in their own right" against quality "within the context of the task at hand";
- de Beaugrande and Dressler: text-centred standards (cohesion, coherence) against user-centred ones.

### 14. The choices left to you

1. **The name:** "textual integrity" with its gloss, "readable as meant", "well-formed", or no single name.
2. **The drawing:** leave the diagram as it is (recommended), or add the three optional marks from section 10.
3. **09-00:** an appended correction note (done), or an edit in place as well, as was done for 21-40 at your request.

## Inherited Commitments Re-test

**From 09-00** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_09-00__harmony-as-alignment-aligncraft-layers-in-communication/finding.md):

- **Commitment:** "Alignment is agreement between what was meant and what is understood or produced"; alignment names the family of all qualities.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** integrity agrees with the reading. AlignCraft's action-set sentence and "false checkpoint" (sections 3 and 4). Inside the family, two kinds of check are told apart by what their reference stands for (section 7).

- **Commitment:** two relationships, drafting and communicating.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** they now explain the two questions inside each check. The communicating relationship appears as the text's integrity before sending (section 10).

- **Commitment:** six layers, each with its reference; all six required for every draft.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** all six apply at both checks (section 9). The proposal to drop rows at the second check was killed, because the text can drift at the task, workspace and approach layers even when the substrate is correct.

- **Commitment:** harmony's internal coherence sits at "coherence, inside the communication"; "AlignCraft's coherence failures are harmony's kinds".
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment found INVALID.
  - **Evidence:** AlignCraft's coherence is fit "with what already exists". Its cost table: "Right steps, poor fit" for coherence, against "Right approach, wrong steps" for the action-set. The kinds move to the text's integrity at the action-set (section 11).

- **Commitment:** harmony's match (due weight; the text matching its substrate) sits at the action-set.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** due weight splits. Against the substrate's rank it is a comparison. Against the text's own declared importance it is integrity (section 8).

- **Commitment:** the four pillars are how context is uncovered so it can be aligned; they are not the detector.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** they act on the context each check compares against (section 9). The critique caught wording that nearly mapped a pillar onto the project's quoting rule again, and removed it.

- **Commitment:** the explicitness detector is "one check at the action-set layer".
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** it is an integrity check at the action-set. AlignCraft's own sentence grounds it. Its findings are decided by comparison (sections 6 and 8).

- **Commitment:** "What detection can reach" from "the text alone".
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** "from the text and its declared setting". The detector already takes a "setting" (section 5).

- **Commitment:** each finding is written "relationship · layer · this against that, with both sides quoted".
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** for integrity findings, the other side is the rule broken plus the quoted candidates, marked "detected, not decided" (Next Actions).

**From 07-57** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_07-57__content-and-substrate-harmony/finding.md):

- **Commitment:** two levels; "the text must match its substrate"; the repair rule.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** harmony at the substrate level is the substrate's consistency, checked in the first check's integrity step. The match is the second check's action-set comparison (sections 8 and 9).

**From 21-40** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-09_21-40__kinds-of-harmony-and-a-better-word/finding.md):

- **Commitment:** "Every quality of a communication is an agreement with something."
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** for integrity, the something is the reading (section 3).

- **Commitment:** the internal criterion ("Harmony compares a communication only with itself").
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** it is explained. The reference is the shared rules, which the checker carries.

- **Commitment:** "The test is belonging, not sameness."
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** it is the wording of the consistency rule (section 5). The critique caught a draft that drifted toward sameness.

- **Commitment:** "integrity" rejected as a name ("read as honesty").
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** the leak still holds, and a second one was found in textual criticism (section 12).

**From the three-gaps finding** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_15-59__three-fundamental-meaning-gaps-of-communication-craft/finding.md):

- **Commitment:** "Every Communication Craft check is a comparison"; the fourth comparison, "the text … against what the text itself makes available".
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** that comparison is the integrity check (sections 3 and 7).

- **Commitment:** what counts as supplied ("the text, the stated setting, earlier turns … declared shared ground"; never "undeclared reader knowledge").
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** it is the base reference's declared part (section 5).

- **Commitment:** "the boundary of the implicit" is undefined.
  - **Re-test status:** INHERITED-WITHOUT-RE-TEST.
  - **Reason:** out of scope. This finding only locates it inside the determinacy rule.

**From 13-08** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-08_13-08__materialization-check-vs-content-check-in-communication-craft/finding.md):

- **Commitment:** a materialization check and a content check.
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** they are the diagram's two checks, by their inputs (section 2).

- **Commitment:** "Problems are classified by what is at fault, not by which tool found them."
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** it classifies findings. The new test classifies checks. One check can produce findings of every fault kind (section 7).

- **Commitment:** "Every check declares its kind (materialization or content)"; the explicitness bank declared "materialization".
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed but frame revised.
  - **Evidence:** each check also declares what its reference stands for. The bank is an integrity check whose findings route either way (section 7).

- **Commitment:** "about 90% of the explicitness detector's gaps could only be answered by the author".
  - **Re-test status:** INHERITED-WITHOUT-RE-TEST.
  - **Reason:** an empirical result from an earlier benchmark, used here as evidence. It was not re-run.

**From 08-07** (devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_08-07__aligncraft-six-layers-in-communication-craft/finding.md):

- **Commitment:** "Text-only checks remain useful for declared textual properties; they cannot supply all the grounds this inquiry needs"; "Nor does a symptom reveal its cause by itself."
  - **Re-test status:** RE-TESTED — commitment confirmed.
  - **Evidence:** these are integrity, and detect-then-decide, stated in 08-07's own words (sections 4 and 6).

## Next Actions

### MUST

- **What:** Append dated notes, and update the memory note on communication alignment.
  - **To 09-00:** internal coherence moves to the text's integrity at the action-set; the table "AlignCraft's coherence failures are harmony's kinds" is withdrawn.
  - **To 13-08:** each check declares what its reference stands for; its two checks are the diagram's two checks.
  - **To 21-40's alignment note:** a pointer to this finding.
  - **Who:** this session.
  - **Gate:** now, at conclusion. Done; see the notes in those files.
  - **Why:** readers of 09-00 must not take up the coherence mapping unwarned.

- **What:** Mark two or three real messages yourself.
  - **How:** write each mark as "relationship · layer · this against that". For each, note one thing: could it be decided from the text and its declared setting, or did it need another object? Include one message with deliberate openness.
  - **Who:** you, with this session producing the AI's marks for comparison.
  - **Gate:** before the one-page alignment reference is treated as validated. This merges with 09-00's marking MUST, as one session.
  - **Why:** the answer rests on AlignCraft's text and outside sources, not yet on your judgment of real cases.

- **What:** Apply the substitution test to every item of the comm-critic-d guides and to the explicitness bank's slots. Name any item that resists sorting.
  - **The guides:** operational communication, recipient and purpose, meaning and fidelity, author preferences, non-ambiguity.
  - **Who:** a test inquiry.
  - **Gate:** before the placement table is written into any skill or reference.
  - **Why:** the test is the "rule that decides it". One item it cannot sort would show a hole.

- **What:** Sort a real detection run's gaps by the placement table: fixed from the substrate, asked of the author, or declined as left open on purpose.
  - **Who:** a test inquiry, reusing the trip benchmark's detection and fix records.
  - **Gate:** before the deciding rule is built into the fix skill or a review step.
  - **Why:** whether the table sorts real detections cleanly is the one claim the critique left untested.

- **What:** Choose the name. Read each candidate cold, then with its gloss.
  - **Who:** you.
  - **Gate:** before the one-page alignment reference uses the term.
  - **Why:** "textual integrity" leaks in two directions (section 12).

### COULD

- **What:** Write the base reference as a one-page standard.
  - **Contents:** the standing core; the declared part; how the declared kind sets the strictness.
  - **Who:** the Communication Craft reference.
  - **Gate:** after the name and the substitution test.
  - **Why:** any integrity check, by a person or an AI, can then cite it.
  - **Depends-on:** MUST items "choose the name" and "apply the substitution test". This COULD is GATED. Do not act until both resolve.

- **What:** Add reading-frame entries to the substrate form, and let the detector read them as its "setting".
  - **The entries:** the kind, the medium, the recipient, and the ground declared to them.
  - **Who:** the next substrate trial.
  - **Gate:** the next real drafting task.
  - **Why:** the base reference's declared part comes from these entries. A wrong frame is then caught at the first check.

- **What:** State the substrate's integrity standard. Trial it with 09-00's three new entries.
  - **The standard:** AlignCraft's decision, level and verifiable; consistency and determinacy; a status on every entry, with "open" kept apart from "left open on purpose".
  - **Who:** a test inquiry.
  - **Gate:** the next real drafting task.
  - **Why:** without the open / left-open split, intended openness cannot be decided.

- **What:** Build the consistency checks as the text's integrity step, as written quoted-pair questions.
  - **They cover:** content, the path, who-to-whom, the reading-relevant skin, and due weight against the text's own declared importance.
  - **Who:** the next version of the question bank.
  - **Gate:** after 07-57's through-line test.
  - **Why:** consistency is defined but not built. It is the other half of what can be checked from the text and its setting.
  - **Depends-on:** none of this finding's MUSTs. Its gate is 07-57's own MUST, carried in 09-00.

- **What:** Bring four existing pieces under one deciding rule, "decided against settled meaning". Cite that rule in the fix and review steps.
  - **The four pieces:** the fix skill's fixed / asked / declined; 13-08's author share; the non-ambiguity guide's "acceptable or necessary" list; receiving.
  - **Who:** the explicitness fix skill's maintainer.
  - **Gate:** after the real-detection sorting.
  - **Why:** one rule instead of four.
  - **Depends-on:** MUST item "sort a real detection run". This COULD is GATED. Do not act until the MUST resolves.

### DEFERRED

- **What:** Detect when a text's act could be read two ways (a request read as a report).
  - **Gate:** the next time the explicitness bank is extended.
  - **Why (if revived):** determinacy now covers force, and no detector checks it.

- **What:** Confirm the outcome against the response: replies, readback, actions.
  - **Gate:** when Pulse (the project's planned observation of replies) observes replies in practice. This is 09-00's deferred item.
  - **Why (if revived):** outcome alignment is then confirmed, not only built in.

- **What:** Integrity when receiving: turning open places in an incoming text into questions back.
  - **Gate:** when Communication Craft takes on receiving as an object of work.
  - **Why (if revived):** the same rule then applies in the other direction.

- **What:** Build order. Automate "fixed" wherever a settled substrate entry exists; send only true "asked" items to authors; invest in uncovering context for the first check.
  - **Gate:** after the real-detection sorting shows the share of fixed against asked.
  - **Why (if revived):** detections will outgrow what authors can answer.

## Reasoning

### Killed, and what each kill taught

**Two separate families of check: alignment between objects, and integrity of one object.**
- **Why it was strongest:**
  - it is your own second option ("alignment checks are preserved for … these more defined things");
  - XML names two properties;
  - your rule forbids mixing relations.
- **Why it was killed:**
  - integrity verdicts need the comparison (the 90%, and "acceptable or necessary" ambiguity);
  - its standard's declared part comes from the exchange;
  - its relation (meant against understood) is alignment's, with a different reference.
- **What it taught:** keep the name and the reference distinct inside the one family.

**A base reference built from what one reader knows.**
- **Why it was killed:** it contradicts your correction: detect the text's explicitness, not reader knowledge.
- **What it taught:** the reading is "anyone with only the text and its declared setting".

**Dropping rows at the second check** (only action-set and coherence).
- **Why it was killed:** the text can drift from a correct substrate at the task, workspace and approach layers too.
- **What it taught:** your "six layers" holds at both checks.

**"Integrity is AlignCraft's 'correct in isolation'."**
- **Why it was killed:** in AlignCraft, "in isolation" still includes agreement with the task and approach.
- **What it taught:** check AlignCraft's phrases for purpose, not just words. This is the same lesson as the pillars.

### Refined, and why

**"There is no standing core; everything depends on the kind of text."**
- **Refined to:** the rules' content is the same for every text; how strictly they apply depends on the declared kind.

**"Classify checks by how their findings are fixed", 13-08's way.**
- **Refined into a complement:**
  - 13-08 classifies findings;
  - the substitution test classifies checks.

### Defects the critique caught

These were caught in earlier drafts within this inquiry, and fixed:

| Defect | Fix |
|---|---|
| The pillar wording "differences observable: quoted pairs" re-mapped a pillar onto the project's quoting rule, the exact mapping 09-00 withdrew. | Stripped (section 9). |
| "Decided by whoever holds the meaning" would let an AI drafter decide from its own unconfirmed entries. | Reworded to "settled meaning" (section 6). |
| The consistency rule drifted toward sameness. | Reworded as belonging (section 5). |
| The skin and due weight each straddled the line. | Both split under the substitution test (section 8). |
| Grice's "Be brief" contradicted the rule. | The citation is limited to the split between how and what (section 13). |

### Survived, and why each held

**One family, with integrity as the reader's side.**
- AlignCraft's own sentences put it inside alignment.
- Four outside traditions show the same structure.
- It answers your question with a test that excludes as well as includes.

**Detect, then decide, against settled meaning.**
- Explains in one rule:
  - the fix skill's outcomes;
  - 13-08's 90%;
  - the non-ambiguity guide's acceptable cases;
  - receiving.

**Explicitness at the action-set.**
- AlignCraft's action-set sentence, matched by purpose.

**The correction to 09-00.**
- AlignCraft's cost table decides it.

**Textual integrity as a base reference: your proposal.**
- It held, made exact by two parts (standing core; declared part) and by where it lives (the workspace).

## Open Questions

### Monitoring

- **Does the substitution test give two people the same answer?** Observable in the MUST that sorts every guide item, if two people sort the same items.
- **Is "textual integrity" heard as honesty, or as "unaltered from the source"?** Observable in its first three uses with colleagues.

### Blocked

- **The name** waits on your choice.
- **The validation of the one-page reference** waits on your marking of real messages.

### Research Frontiers

- **The boundary of the implicit:** when a wording "needs" one particular thing. Inherited from the three-gaps finding; now located inside the determinacy rule.
- **Detecting force:** whether a text's act can be taken two ways.
- **How the outcome is observed for people, beyond readback.**

### Refinement Triggers

- **Reopen "integrity is part of alignment"** if, in your marking, several integrity marks can be neither decided nor repaired by reference to any upstream object or to the author. That would mean integrity does carry verdicts of its own.
- **Reopen the action-set placement of explicitness** if, in a real detection run, the action-set label sends fixes to the text when most faults lie upstream. That is, if more than half of the gaps are "asked" and the label still misleads the repair.
- **Reopen the correction to 09-00** if AlignCraft's definition of coherence is revised to include a plan's internal consistency. The correction rests on "Right steps, poor fit".
- **Reopen the base reference's exclusion of reader knowledge** only if you withdraw your correction that detection judges the text, not the reader.

## Correction note (added 2026-10-10, from a later inquiry)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_13-28__something-is-off-base-references-and-workspace-alignment/finding.md refines this finding and corrects two points.

- **Withdrawn: "checking a text against them is not workspace alignment"** (section 5).
  - The base reference lives in the workspace. Checking a text against it is alignment with the workspace's written, standing expectations.
  - The withdrawn sentence mixed two things:
    - where the reference lives;
    - what caused a failure (a rule the drafter never knew, or a slip).
- **Revised: author preferences E1–E3** (section 8's table).
  - Preferences that hold across an author's messages are standing expectations, in the base reference's house part.
  - A preference stated for one exchange only stays in that exchange's substrate.
- **Refined: "an integrity check can only detect".** Both ways are comparisons.
  - **A standing rule** (what every text of this kind is expected to be) finds a deviation, and shows how the text deviates.
  - **What was meant for this text** (the substrate, the request, the sources) decides whether the deviation is a fault, and what the text should say.
- **Added:**
  - the base reference must be written down. Much of it already exists: the explicitness bank, non-ambiguity N1–N6, R07–R09, and the genre catalogues;
  - one reference shared by writer and checker;
  - a growth loop from "no written rule yet";
  - a proposed clarifying note for AlignCraft, which is the owner's call.


## Further note (added 2026-10-10: standalone correction)

devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_13-28__something-is-off-base-references-and-workspace-alignment/finding.md (its second iteration) refines this finding again.

- **This project is standalone.** This finding's references to other local projects' guides, catalogues, preference items and products are outside this project. That covers sections 4, 7 and 8, the Next Actions and the correction note above. They are not part of its base reference.
- **Withdrawn from the correction note above:**
  - the list after "Much of it already exists";
  - the "house part".
- **The base reference is now built from this project alone:**
  - this finding's textual integrity (section 5) is its first principle;
  - the rules under it are the explicitness bank (tested) and drafted rules for consistency, complementing and readable form.
- **The "declared part" of section 5** is read as inputs that select rules: the kind of text, the medium, and what the reader has been given. The rules for a kind of text are content of the base reference.


## Proposed note (added 2026-10-10, from devdocs/inquiries/2026-10-10_19-51__what-went-wrong-in-the-substrate-answer-checks-for-the-brief-and-content-in-it/finding.md; the owner adopts or removes)

- **Section 9's check-1 row, "the substrate's account of the situation (people, facts, what the reader can access, conventions in place)", is read with the facts referenced, not carried.** The substrate's account names which facts it rests on and where they are; the facts themselves stay in the raw context.
- **The owner's rule (2026-10-10):** the brief refers to the raw context; "it can talk about it, but … it doesn't include it itself."
